# QMX Panadapter — Master Todo List + Status Assessment

**Last updated:** 2026-08-31
**Scope:** v1.0 release gates → open investigations → feature requests → roadmap → full shipped history
**Source:** CLAUDE.md + README.md + groups.io feature requests + session work
**Assessment:** Code grep + git log + memory system
**Repo state:** **v1.5.0 released 2026-08-06** (context-sensitive help + guidance panel, manual read from the embedded blob, web Call CQ, decode-list pinning). Prior: v1.4.0 (2026-08-05, live spots + the TLS/`.bss`/UAC-spin root causes), v1.3.6 (2026-08-03).

---

## ⚡ Status Legend

- 🔴 **Identified bug** — Code defect confirmed, ready to fix
- 🟠 **Open investigation** — Partially fixed, real gap remains, root cause not fully closed
- ❌ **Not started** — Genuinely new, no code/design
- ⚠️ **Needs verification** — Partial info or prior work unclear
- ⏸️ **Partial/Designed** — Infrastructure exists or design done, coding pending
- ✋ **Shelved** — Intentionally disabled, unblocking needed
- ✅ **Shipped** — Already completed and released

| 313 | 🟠 **The web server wedged while the device stayed perfectly healthy** (observed 2026-09-01 at the end of the #312 mode-switch soak) | ⚠️ **Observed, NOT diagnosed - and possibly self-inflicted** | Medium | After ~5 mode-switch cycles the Tab5 stopped answering on port 80: three `/api/status` requests returned empty, and a fourth and fifth the same. **Everything else was fine** - `ping` replied, `wifi: online` printed every 30 s, CAT kept polling (`freq=14074000Hz mode=DiGi`), audio ran at ~48,000 pairs/s and FT8 was capturing (`FT8 cap: idx=96768/180000`). So the network stack and the application were alive and only the HTTP server was gone - the single-threaded-httpd wedge class CLAUDE.md already records for the LoTW/forge fetch and the WS socket exhaustion. ⚠ **Three candidates, none tested:** (a) internal heap - it read **26 KB free, min 7 KB**, lower than the 39-58 KB seen earlier the same session, and httpd needs sockets from it; (b) the PSRAM leak from #312, ~905 KB per cycle, having reached 11 MB free; (c) **my own request volume** - this session made a great many `/api/cmd` and `/api/status` calls, and CLAUDE.md records TIME_WAIT socket exhaustion as a real cause here, so it may be an artefact of the test rather than something a user would hit. ⛔ **Deliberately NOT rebooted to clear it** - the operator was asleep and a warm reset with the radio attached is the documented #74 trigger, which would have left the QMX wedged all night and the overnight soak deaf. **Next step: reproduce WITHOUT the mode-switch soak** - if a quiet device with the same request volume also wedges, it is (c) and the soak is exonerated |

| 312 | 🟢 **FT8 mode-exit freed the monitor pool under a live decode worker - Load access fault** (found 2026-09-01 during Phase 5.5 verification of v1.10.5) | ✅ **FIXED AND VERIFIED ON HARDWARE 2026-09-01** | Medium | Switching FT8 -> FT4 -> panadapter crashed the device ~20 s later: **Load access fault in `ft8_dec0`**, `estimate_snr_db` (`ft8_lib/decode.c:471`) reading `block[bin]`, `MTVAL=0x2113babc`. ⭐ **The evidence is the timing, and it is exact**: the panadapter switch was at ~442 s, `ft8_task exiting` logged at **457.114 s** with **no** `decode task exiting` and **no** `decode worker exiting` before it - 442 + 15 = 457, so the `xTaskNotifyWait(..., 15000)` expired - and the worker faulted at **477.268 s** on a waterfall `free_capture_pool()` had already released. **Why it timed out:** the worker is `tskIDLE_PRIORITY + 1` pinned to **core 0**, and returning to the panadapter puts taskLVGL back on that core; the log reads `idle0 0.0%` at the fault. So the very act of leaving FT8 starves the task the teardown is waiting for. ⛔ **NOT a v1.10.5 regression** - `git log v1.10.4..v1.10.5 -- main/ft8_test.c` is empty; this is latent and older, and the release neither introduced nor fixed it. **Fix:** the timeout branch now frees **nothing** - new `forget_capture_pool()` drops the references, and the decode queue is left undeleted too, because the decode task blocks on a **local copy** of that handle (`QueueHandle_t q = s_decode_queue`) so `vQueueDelete()` under it is the same use-after-free one layer down. This is the rule the rest of the teardown already followed and this one path ignored: `reap_pending_tasks()` reaps nothing on a join timeout and the worker ctx is deliberately leaked with *"a small leak beats a crash"*. ⚠ **Do NOT widen the 15 s bound** - timing changes the odds of a race, not the race, and this file records two fixes falsified for exactly that. ⚠ **Verification is a RATE question, not a single run**: the fix cannot be proven by not crashing once. `scratchpad/soak_modeswitch.sh` cycles panadapter/FT8/FT4 and the loud `LEAKING its queue` warning is what says the timeout path fired at all . ⭐ **FIRST SOAK RESULT, 2026-09-01, and it CHANGES THE FIX**: the timeout path fired on the **very first** mode exit (uptime 179.317 s) and **the device survived** - which is the fix working, since that is the exact moment that used to free the pool. But the follow-on timings say the bound, not the freeing, is the real problem: `decode worker exiting` at **183.444 s** and `decode task exiting` at **183.473 s**, i.e. the teardown needed **~19.2 s against a 15 s bound - it overran by only 4.2 s**. It is not hung, it is starved. ⚠ **So leaking cannot be the NORMAL path**: at roughly 250-330 KB of PSRAM per leaked pool, a user who switches modes often would exhaust PSRAM (the overnight soak was expected to hit it in ~2 h, which is why a low-PSRAM guard was armed alongside it). **Proposed for the morning, NOT yet done:** raise ft8_task’s join bound well past the observed worst case AND address the starvation directly - the worker is `tskIDLE_PRIORITY + 1` on the core taskLVGL owns, so a temporary priority raise for the duration of the teardown is the targeted cure. ⚠ **This is NOT the "timing changes the odds of a race" case** the file warns about: a bounded JOIN is not a race, and its bound simply has to exceed the real worst case. The leak backstop stays either way, because freeing on ANY timeout is wrong . ⭐ **THE REAL FIX, and it is neither of the two options above**: `decode_candidate_range()` now breaks out as soon as `s_ft8_running` goes false. ft8_task clears that flag BEFORE sending the stop sentinel, so once a teardown is under way the loop is grinding through candidates whose results nobody will ever see - the decode list is about to be destroyed with the pool. Finishing them is the entire reason the teardown overran. It costs the final slot on the way out, which is what leaving asked for. ⛔ **NOT the priority raise I proposed**: the worker handle belongs to the decode task, which is itself BLOCKED on that worker at the moment the raise would be needed, so reaching it from ft8_task means republishing a handle across instances - the exact cross-instance sharing behind Dennis WN4FLA’s crash that `worker_ctx_t` exists to prevent. ⭐ **VERIFIED: 30 cycles of FT8 -> FT4 -> panadapter with the radio streaming - ZERO timeout events (it previously fired on EVERY cycle), zero crashes, zero reboots, and PSRAM went UP over the run (14,854 -> 15,960 KB) instead of falling ~905 KB a cycle.** Device uptime 2,097 s spanned the whole 1,787 s soak |
| **🆕 2026-08-31 — groups.io feedback batch (Randy N4OPI, Roy KI0ER, Dirk)** | | | |
| 311 | 🔴 **A swipe to the panadapter could key the radio for 110 seconds - the WSPR TX button sat inside the edge-swipe strip** (Randy N4OPI, 2026-08-31) | ✅ **SHIPPED in v1.10.5** - not yet eyeballed on screen | Small | His words: *"it is somewhat easy to accidentally turn on transmit when trying to switch pages to the Pandapter."* At `x=16` the button's first 14 px were inside `EDGE_SWIPE_ZONE_PX` (30), the gesture used to leave the page - so a swipe starting a little high landed on a control that transmits for ~110 s. **That is not carelessness, it is two hit areas overlapping.** Moved to `x=40` with the right edge moved out to match, so the control keeps its width and the row simply begins where the swipe zone ends; the dial dropdown moved with it. ⚠ First attempt moved only the left edge, which shrank the button and left 24 px of dead space - the operator spotted that immediately. ⭐ **Standing rule now written at the call site: any control added to this pane must clear 30 px.** The project rule that a radio-keying control must be impossible to trigger by accident is **not** satisfied by a confirmation step if the thing can be hit by a gesture aimed at something else |
| 310 | **A WSPR spot did not say which band it was heard on** (Roy KI0ER, 2026-08-31) | ✅ **SHIPPED in v1.10.5** - not yet eyeballed on screen | Small | With band hopping on, a list of spots from several bands could not be read. New `M` column (metres - the unit is in the heading, not repeated on every row) between UTC and CALL, placed there because it describes the circumstances of the hearing rather than a property of the station. ⭐ **The dial is carried WITH the decode job, not read when the spot is filed**: hopping retunes `HOP_LEAD_SEC` before the next cycle and a decode routinely finishes after that, so reading the dial at filing time would attribute the cycle just ended to the band we had already moved to. Captured when the window is queued instead - what the samples actually belong to. Blank for spots recorded before the field existed, rather than a wrong guess |
| 309 | 🔴 **Changing band mid-QSO kept transmitting the exchange ON THE NEW BAND** (Randy N4OPI, 2026-08-31) | ✅ **SHIPPED in v1.10.5** - NOT verified on air | Small | His words: *"if I give up on a response from a CQ caller and switch bands, the app will continue with the previous auto call sequence on the new band."* ⭐ **He blamed himself for not cancelling first, and he should not have to** - changing band says plainly that the previous contact is over, and nothing else on the radio would carry on regardless. Every band-change path called `ft8_robot_stand_down()`, which stops the **auto picker** and leaves a QSO already in progress completely untouched: it kept re-arming its next message every slot and sending it to a station that is no longer there. New `ft8_band_change_stand_down()` in `ft8_qso.c` aborts a busy QSO **and** stands the robot down; used from `band_preset_cb`, `topbar_reconcile_cb` and the web `set_band`. ⛔ **Deliberately NOT gated on "was the robot running"** - a hand-started pounce transmits on the wrong band just as readily, and the on-air consequence is identical. ⭐ **Second, narrower hole found while reviewing it, and fixed in the same pass**: `apply_freq_preset()` (the FT8 **preset** dropdown - the control an FT8 operator actually changes band with, on the Tab5 and now in the browser via #304) stood nothing down. Cross-band taps were already covered *indirectly and late* by `topbar_reconcile_cb` noticing the poll-reported band change, but **a move WITHIN one band can never be seen that way** - FT8 14.074 → FT4 14.080 leaves the band string identical, so the QSO survived a change of PROTOCOL and went on sending its next message in FT4 at a partner listening on FT8. Now stands down there too, gated on the dial or sub-mode having actually changed so re-tapping the preset you are already on cannot kill a live contact. Compared against the **radio** rather than `settings_load_all()`, which would have put a second multi-kilobyte `qmx_settings_t` on taskLVGL's ~8 KB stack |
| 308 | **The WSPR waterfall is not updated during the cycle before a transmit** (Dirk, 2026-08-31) | ✅ **SHIPPED in v1.10.5** - not yet eyeballed on screen | Small | His words: *"There seems to be a minor bug in WSPR: The nice waterfall is not updated during the cycle before transmit."* ⭐ **He is describing the TX cycle itself, and the firmware CANNOT behave differently in the cycle before one** - the duty-cycle dice are rolled at the top of the TX cycle and `wspr_tx_arm()` claims THAT minute (`wspr_rx.c` ~1171 spells out why), so there is no forward knowledge to act on. Chasing "the preceding cycle" would have been chasing nothing. **What actually happens:** the TX stand-down `continue`s before `wf_begin()` AND before the `wf_mark_boundary()` at the end of a receive cycle, so a transmit was the one stall that drew **nothing at all**; the pane holds exactly one cycle (`WSPR_WF_CYCLES 1`, 176 rows), so the previous cycle’s finished picture sat unchanged for two minutes while the status line said `transmitting`. That is exactly what the note above `wf_mark_boundary()` already forbids - *"a stalled carpet is indistinguishable from a hung display"* - and the TX path was the one place the rule was not applied. **Fix:** `wf_mark_boundary()` on both stand-down branches. ⛔ **ONCE, before the wait loop - never inside it**: that loop polls every 500 ms for the ~110 s burst, so marking per iteration would publish ~440 rows into a 176-row buffer and wipe the picture. ⭐ **Mark ALONE, keeping the last received cycle on screen** (operator’s call) - the pane is the only place those signals exist, and a full TX band would have thrown them away to repeat what the status line already says. ⚠ Noticed in passing and NOT acted on: the comment at the receive-path `wf_mark_boundary()` claims *"~68 s in which the receiver is genuinely DEAF"*, but `CAP_SAMPLES` is a full 120 s and rows paint through row 174 of 176, so that figure looks stale relative to the ping-pong capture. Verify before quoting it |
| 307 | **Static IP / netmask / gateway / DNS** (Randy N4OPI, 2026-08-31) | ⚠️ **SHIPPED in v1.10.5; the operator has handed TESTING to Randy N4OPI (2026-08-31). Still exercised by nobody - the field is empty, so the bench is on DHCP** | Medium | `wifi_ip/mask/gw/dns[16]` in `qmx_settings_t`, one dirty bit `DIRTY_WIFI_STATIC` (110), four NVS keys, config export/import, `/api/settings` GET+POST, four fields in the **web** Settings window’s WiFi group. **There is no Tab5 row** - four dotted quads on glass, and it is a home-network convenience rather than something wanted at a POTA site. **Empty `wifi_ip` = DHCP**, so an upgrade changes nobody’s network, and there is deliberately no "use static IP" checkbox - the address being filled in IS the switch. ⭐ **The ORDER in `wifi.c` is load-bearing**, established by reading `esp_netif_lwip.c`: stop the DHCP client at **STA_START** (`set_ip_info` refuses otherwise), apply the address at **STA_CONNECTED** (it posts GOT_IP only once the lwIP netif is up, and GOT_IP is what starts mDNS, SNTP and the web server here - applying earlier gives an address and none of them), **DNS last** (`set_ip_info` calls `dns_clear_servers()`). ⛔ **IT BOOT-LOOPED THE DEVICE ON ITS FIRST FLASH** - `settings_load_all()` on `sys_evt`’s 2808-byte stack, fixed in `d041c9e`; the fourth instance of that bug class and the third in this file, now written up under "Task stacks on this board are TINY". 🔴 **OPEN RISK, and it lands on Randy first: a WRONG address is only undoable from the web UI it just made unreachable.** `config_io_import()` is reachable ONLY via `POST /api/config`; the SD card gets an export mirror that nothing ever reads back. A MALFORMED address is safe (it is rejected and stays on DHCP) and the Tab5’s bottom bar always shows the real address - but a **well-formed address on the wrong subnet** leaves a factory reset as the only way out, taking the WiFi password, callsign, memories and the LoTW private key with it. **Proposed and NOT built pending the operator’s decision: a single "Use DHCP" button in the drawer’s WiFi section** - one tap, no typing, a way OUT rather than a second way in |
| 306 | **The LoTW certificate could only be re-imported by Ctrl-clicking the upload button** (found 2026-08-31 while working #305) | ✅ **SHIPPED in v1.10.5** | Small | Certificates renew about every three years, so re-import is a routine act, and it was reachable only by a modifier-click nobody would guess. | 305 | 🔴 **LoTW uploads report success and the QSOs never appear in the log** (Randy N4OPI, 2026-08-31) | 🟠 **NOT FIXED - the reporting is now honest and diagnosable, which is a different thing** | Medium | ⭐ **"22 uploaded" was always OUR count of what we SENT.** LoTW accepts a **file** and processes the QSOs afterwards, so an accepted upload says nothing about whether any contact was credited - we were reporting our own claim as the server's verdict. Two changes: the accept test now refuses a status containing `not accepted` or `rejected` (the old `strstr(result,"accepted")` alone passes both), and LoTW's own `<!-- .UPLMESSAGE. -->` is **kept on SUCCESS** as well as on failure - new `note[120]` on `lotw_upload_result_t`, carried through `s_last_upload`, into `/api/upload_status` as `note`, shown in the browser alert. **Next step is to get that note from Randy after a real upload** - it is the only thing that can distinguish a signing/format rejection from a station-location mismatch from an upload that genuinely landed |
| 304 | **The web FT8/FT4 page had no band-preset picker - the Tab5 did** (Randy N4OPI, 2026-08-31) | ✅ **SHIPPED in v1.10.5** | Small | Device side: `ft8_preset_list(bool ft4, int *n)` in `ft8_screen_view.c` with a `_Static_assert` keeping `ft8_preset_t` layout-identical to `ft8_band_freq_t`, and `/api/status?presets=1` serving `ft8_presets`/`ft4_presets` **only on request** - the 1 Hz status poll must not carry ~300 B of a static list. Browser side: `<select id="ft8-preset">` plus `ft8LoadPresets()`/`ft8FillPresets()`/`ft8IsFt4()` and a change handler. ⭐ **It sends the EXISTING `set_ft8_mode` action rather than a new one** - `ft8_screen_view_request_preset()` was already wired there, and a second action would have been a second door onto the same room, free to disagree with the first |
| **🆕 2026-08-31 — QDX support** | | | |
| 303 | **Support the QDX (and QDX-M) as well as the QMX** (recurring user request; Travis AK6TB and others) | ❓ Needs a QDX on the bench before anything is built | Medium (RX) / Large (TX) | Full analysis: **`docs/qdx-vs-qmx-comparison.md`**, written from the vendor manuals + the QRP Labs changelog, cross-checked against our code. ⭐ **The case is STRONGER than for a QMX**: the QDX has *"only three connectors: USB, Power and RF"* and one status LED — **no display, no encoder, no buttons**. It cannot be operated without a PC at all, so a Tab5 would be the only face it has ever had. A QMX already has an LCD; this would turn a PC accessory into a standalone radio. **RX is ~1.5–2 days**: same 48 ksps 24-bit stereo I/Q (I expect *zero* changes in `audio.c`), same `Q9` IQ mode, same `Q3` VOX-off, same Kenwood basics. Needs a `radio_type_t` from `VN;` (QDX returns `VN1_10;` — **no "QMX" suffix**, a clean discriminator), per-radio `AG` scaling (**plain dB on QDX vs 0.25 dB steps on QMX — we would ask for 4x too much**), fixed `FW`=3200, RIT without `RC`, and UI gating for the absent `MM`/`PC`/`SW`/`TM`/`RG` and the missing 2nd CDC interface (no Radio-menus screen — QDX has one serial port and its terminal mode disables CAT). **TX is a separate project, NOT impossible** — ⚠ my first pass called it impossible on the strength of the missing `TA` and that was wrong: the Tab5 is the USB **host**, so it can stream synthesised PCM into the QDX's sound card exactly as a PC does. `uac_host_device_write()` is already in our patched UAC component, `audio.c` already receives (and ignores) `UAC_HOST_DRIVER_EVENT_TX_CONNECTED`, and `synth_gfsk_heap()` already generates FT8 audio on-device. Real costs: a hard-real-time PCM feeder (must go on **core 1** — core 0 is at 0–7% idle), the unmeasured output alt-setting, tone accuracy inherited from the QDX's zero-crossing measurement instead of exact `TA` synthesis, and **no `PC`/`SW`/`MM` means no measured power, no SWR and no PA guard** (think hard before enabling WSPR TX, which keys 110 s in every 120). ⛔ **TWO DECISIVE FACTS COME FROM THE CHANGELOG, NOT THE MANUAL, AND MUST BE CONFIRMED ON HARDWARE**: (1) *"12kHz IF offset is removed when you enable IQ Mode"* — if true our `n_bins/4` shift puts every signal 12 kHz off and tap-to-tune with it; (2) *"Transmit is disabled when you are in IQ mode"* — so `Q9` must be toggled around every burst, and per (1) that toggle also moves the LO 12 kHz, which may not settle inside an FT8 slot. **Next step: get a QDX** (they are cheap); both facts are an hour's work to settle with one attached, and everything else is speculation until then. ⚠ QDX firmware is **frozen** — last release `1_10_.zip`, Jul-2023 — so do not wait for `TA`. |
| 302 | **Frequency separator style as a setting - commas for thousands, point for the decimal** (Don N2VGU, 2026-08-30, #178024) | ❓ Needs the operator's decision | Small-Medium | *"I am used to reading/writing frequencies and numbers in general with commas separating kilo, mega, etc., and a decimal point after units; I guess it is a USA convention. Could this be a setup option? It is settable on many (other) instruments and my beloved HP RPN calculators."* We print `%lu.%03lu.%03lu` throughout - **14.074.000**, MHz.kHz.Hz with dots - where he would read **14,074.000**. ⚠ **Scope is the reason this is not trivial**: that format string appears **8 times in `ui.c` alone** (top bar, freq axis, the keypad, memory recall, the tune tooltip, the band-plan drag readouts) plus ~23 sites in `index.html`. Doing it by find-and-replace is how the formats drift apart; it wants ONE `format_freq()` helper that every caller uses, which is the same lesson as `pan_view` and the slot gates. **The helper is worth having even if the setting never ships.** ⚠ And a real question first: is a separator style worth an NVS setting and a drawer row, or should it follow something we already know? There is no locale setting on the device to hang it off. |
| 301 | 🔴 **Auto-work-pileup called a station who was mid-QSO with someone else** (Gyula HA3HZ, 2026-08-30, v1.10.3) | 🟢 **Fixed - NOT verified on air** | Small | His words: *"I did not call and did not mark YL3PK before, but the software called him."* ⭐ **He HAD been called** - `decoded: 'HA3HZ YL3PK KO27'` at uptime 819394, three times - so YL3PK entered the pileup legitimately. But the drain reached him at 948678, **129 s later**, by which time he was working ER1SKI/P, and we then called him **five times over**. ⛔ **The information was there and was used for the wrong thing**: the drain reads their last message to choose the message FORMAT, logging `auto-pileup YL3PK: last heard 'ER1SKI/P YL3PK R KO27' is not to us - report-first`, and never asks whether that means it should call at all. `partner_busy_with()` already existed and was consulted once a QSO was RUNNING, never at the moment of picking. Added to `try_start_pileup_pounce()`. ⚠ **The deeper cause is a broken premise:** `ft8_pileup.h` documents having no expiry and justifies it as *"Deliberately NOT automatic: nothing in this module ever arms a TX"* - sound while a human chose from it and could see the station was busy. **Auto-work-pileup was later built on top and broke that premise without revisiting it.** A staleness cutoff for the AUTOMATIC picker only (the manual viewer should still show old callers) is the other half and is NOT done - it needs an operating judgement about how old is too old. |
| 300 | 🔴 **FT4 reports a FABRICATED 5.0 W - the live path fell into the simulation placeholder** (Gyula HA3HZ, 2026-08-30, v1.10.3) | 🟢 **Fixed - NOT verified on air** | Small | His words: *"on Tab5 the max. power is always 5.0W - while QMX indicates higher. This only happens with FT4."* ⭐ **Exactly predicted by the code.** The mid-burst sampler is gated `if (!sim && !is_ft4)` - correct, FT4's 48 ms symbols cannot fit a CDC query - but its `else` is therefore `sim || is_ft4`, so **a LIVE FT4 burst wrote `FT8_TX_SIM_POWER_W` (5.0) and `FT8_TX_SIM_SWR` (1.20) into the live `s_last_power_w`/`s_last_swr`.** His log proves it: **zero** `SIMULATION - radio not keyed` lines, FT8 bursts logging real `live TX power=5.3..5.5W`, FT4 bursts logging `sim TX power=5.0W (placeholder, not measured)` on a radio genuinely on the air. Fixed by gating the placeholder on `sim` alone. **Same rule as never writing 599 into an ADIF record: a number nobody measured must not be shown as a measurement.** ⚠ **FT4 now has NO valid power reading, and that is the honest state** - the post-burst query is not a substitute: it runs AFTER `TA<keyup>;` has dropped the envelope, which is why his post-burst FT4 figures read 0.1 / 1.9 / 2.7-2.9 W against a real 5.4. Measuring FT4 properly means sampling before the envelope drops, which risks smearing the final symbol - a separate decision, not a quick fix. Until then the UI should say so rather than show a stale or invented number. |
| 299 | 🔴 **The bench CANNOT exercise the FT8/FT4 slot-loop gating without a live radio, and it passes silently** (found 2026-08-30 while checking Gyula HA3HZ's follow-up) | 🟡 **Harness DONE 2026-08-30, 9/9 mutations caught. Not yet WIRED into the slot loop** | Medium | ⛔ **My v1.10.3 'verified on hardware' claim for the FT4 fix was WORTHLESS and I stated it in the release notes and the commit.** The capture read `RX 0 pairs/s` throughout: the QMX was wedged by the flash that installed the very build under test. With no audio the capture fails, **no decode job is ever queued**, `s_decode_jobs_queued` stays 0, so `hold_for_fresh` can never be true - **the same test passes on the BROKEN build**. It exercised nothing. ⭐ **The gate is a pure decision** - protocol, `jobs_done != jobs_queued`, `ft8_qso_is_busy()`, `ft8_tx_slot_would_run()`, `into_slot_ms` - and it belongs in a portable function with a host harness, the same pattern as `db_gridlines.c`, `net_guard.c`, `spot_sig.c` and now `pan_view.c`. A table over protocol x counters x busy x due would have caught this in seconds, with no radio and no flash. **Second-best**: make sim mode queue a decode job even when there is no audio, so the slot loop's gating runs on a bench with a wedged radio. Today it quietly skips the whole branch (`no audio (sim) - running QSO advance`). ⚠ **Until one of those exists, no FT8/FT4 timing change may be called verified without a STREAMING radio** - and the radio is wedged by every flash, which is exactly the trap. | ⭐ **`main/ft8_slot_gate.c/.h` + `test/ft8_slot_gate_harness.c`.** The two gates now share ONE `ft8_gate_late_fire_enabled()`, and the harness's first test is the invariant that was violated: **a protocol that can be HELD must also be able to LATE-FIRE**, checked exhaustively over all 8 boundary states. Mutating the module back into the v1.10.2 shape - hold on for FT4, fire off - **is caught**. So is restoring the 2800 ms window. ⭐ **The arithmetic is now derived, not tabulated**, and it settles the question the v1.10.3 release note muddled: FT8 has 15000-12640 = **2360 ms** of room, FT4 has 7500-5040 = **2460 ms**. **FT4 has MORE room, not less.** The real defect was the shared `FT8_REPLY_TX_WINDOW_MS` of **2800 ms**, which is looser than EITHER protocol's room - a latent overrun for FT8 too, which only rarely bit because decodes usually land about a second in. Per-protocol window is now `slot - burst - 100 ms`: **2260 ms FT8, 2360 ms FT4**. ⚠ **Nothing is wired yet** - `ft8_test.c` still carries the inline gate and its own constants. Wiring it is what gives Gyula his fast FT4 replies back (#295), and it is now a change whose correctness is provable on the host before it ever reaches a radio. |
| 298 | 🔵 **A STILL spectrum/waterfall with the VFO moving over it (FlexRadio model)** (operator, 2026-08-30) | 📋 **Analysed, not started** - full plan in `docs/panadapter-still-spectrum-plan.md` | Large, staged | His words: *"tune the vfo up and down over the spectrum and wf that do not move unless you tune beyond the limits"* - how his FlexRadio behaves. ⭐ **The arithmetic decides the feature, so read it before designing anything:** the QMX's LO is rigidly `dial-12 kHz` and moves WITH the dial, so the capture window `[dial-36k, dial+12k]` slides 1:1 with tuning. A still viewport of width `W` therefore has **`play = 48 - W` kHz** of tuning before it is FORCED to move - and requiring the cursor to stay visible confines it further. **x1: play = 0, stillness is impossible** (the viewport IS the capture window, cursor pinned at 75%). **x2: cursor confined to the TOP HALF.** **x4 and above: full freedom.** A Flex can do this because it digitises megahertz and shows a slice; we digitise 48 kHz and show all of it. ⭐ **Much smaller than it sounds, though:** above x1 the code already keeps `s_pan_offset_bins` and already draws an arbitrary window - what makes the display dial-locked is `recompute_zoom_pan()` re-deriving the pan from the passband centre on EVERY tune. **The feature is essentially deleting that auto-recentre and clamping instead.** ⚠ **Biggest pitfalls:** the forced clamp fires per-tune at an edge and makes a "still" display shimmer (hysteresis is mandatory, not polish); the waterfall canvas is 2.1 MB so a horizontal shift is ~20 ms on a core already at 0-7% idle (use the same wider-canvas + view-offset trick the vertical scroll uses); and drag-to-pan would REPLACE the liked drag-to-stroll gesture, which is the operator's call. ⭐ **The real prize is not aesthetics** - a still waterfall is frequency-ALIGNED history, so a signal's past sits above its present. **#297 is phase 0 of this and is worth doing either way.** | ⭐ **DECIDED 2026-08-30 from an interactive simulation of the Tab5 screen** (`scratchpad/still-spectrum.html`, also published as an artifact): edge behaviour is **JUMP A WINDOW, not push** - his words, *"i like the jumping better"*. ⛔ **RETRACTED 2026-08-30: I claimed paging downward was impossible below x4 and blamed the hardware. Wrong, and the operator pushed back correctly** - *"it absolutely makes no sense... this is just like moving the band-plan slider"*. It is exactly that: the viewport can slide anywhere inside the 48 kHz, and at x2 a 24 kHz window has 24 kHz of room. **The blocker was my TRIGGER, not the geometry** - I paged when the cursor hit 5% of the view, but the ceiling stops the cursor short of the left edge, so below x4 that threshold is unreachable and the clamp dragged the view down continuously instead. ⭐ **The rule is: page when the cursor reaches the limit of where it can GO** (that limit is `max(0,(W-12)/W)` of the view), landing it at the far right and letting the clamp trim. Paging then works BOTH ways at x2/x3/x4 with ~11 kHz of still display between pages in every case, cursor always on screen. **x1 is the only true exception**, because there the view already IS the whole capture window. The cursor's confinement to the right of the view is still real and still worth drawing - it is why a page lands right and walks left - but it never prevented paging. **Lesson: a limit I derived from my own policy is not a hardware limit, and I presented one as the other.** ⭐ **EDGE BEHAVIOUR SETTLED 2026-08-30 (operator's design): DEAD BAND -> SOFT PUSH -> PAGE WITH OVERLAP.** A bare page is too abrupt for the commonest case, working a signal that sits near the edge: *"make it just a tad pushing so that it will not jump when you just wanted to tune a signal on the edge... THEN if tuning further out of screen then it jumps"*, and *"the jump should include a little overlap of the previous screen so you are not disoriented and can see where you came from"*. Three stages - (1) nothing moves in the middle ~80%; (2) past 0.90 the view nudges along to keep the cursor visible, bounded to `PUSHMAX` of a view; (3) beyond that it pages, landing the cursor `OVERLAP` from the far side and showing the carried-over slice briefly with the seam drawn. Starting values `EDGE=0.90 PUSHMAX=0.10 OVERLAP=0.28` - at x4 that is ~8.6 kHz still, 1.2 kHz of nudge, then a page. **Both are live sliders in the simulator so he can settle them by feel.** ⚠ **The page target must be clamped into the cursor's reachable range first**, or the capture clamp overrides it and a deliberate page reports as a FORCED move - bites at x2 tuning up (28% landing is under the 50% floor). Found by running the state machine over a sweep, not by reading it. ⭐ **THREE DECISIONS, 2026-08-30, and the first overturns my recommendation.** (1) **The display may go BLANK** - *"Let it go blank - or it is not a moving vfo."* He is right and I had it backwards: clamping the viewport to keep every pixel real is exactly what drags the view along with the dial, so keeping the screen full and having a still display are the same trade. **This DELETES most of the complexity** - no forced re-frame, no cursor-reachability limit, no up/down asymmetry; every column just asks whether its frequency is inside `[dial-36k, dial+12k]` and draws the bin or nothing, **which is also exactly the #297 fix**. Cost: blank screen where the radio cannot hear (up to 75% at x1, 50% at x2, none at x4), and it must LOOK empty rather than like a flat noise floor. (2) **Gestures: swipe moves the waterfall, press-hold-then-drag tunes** - navigating and tuning become different gestures instead of one gesture with a mode; reuse `ROW_HOLD_SELECT_MS` (250 ms) rather than inventing a second dwell. Retires drag-to-stroll. (3) **EDGE 0.90, PUSHMAX 0.10, OVERLAP 0.10**, explicitly provisional. ⭐ **ON A BRANCH FROM 2026-08-30: `feat/still-panadapter`** (operator's call - *"otherwise i cannot keep the daily maintenance of the main track"*). `main` keeps the #297 fix, which stands on its own; the exploratory still-display work lives on the branch. Plain branch, NOT a worktree: the two differ in three files so switching is an incremental build, and there are already four trees under the one-build-at-a-time rule. ⭐ **First bench impression: *"that was a new refreshing feeling!"*** ⛔ **And the first fault it found**: the pan was stored in FFT BINS (46.875 Hz quantum) and each tuning STEP was rounded, so a 10 Hz step rounded to zero and the display simply followed the dial - *"the spectrum moving slightly in the same direction as I am tuning"*, visible only zoomed because 100 Hz of drift is 2.7 px at x1 and 21 px at x8. The pan is now accumulated in **Hz** and only rounded where the DSP demands it. ⚠ **Residual, not yet fixed**: at zoom >= 2 `dsp_set_zoom()` still takes its centre in whole bins, so up to half a bin (~23 Hz, ~5 px at x8) can remain - as a step, never a drift. Sub-bin zoom centring is the next piece. `/api/cmd {"action":"still_view","on":false}` restores the old behaviour without a flash.
| 297 | 🔴 **The right-hand quarter of the ×1 spectrum is a WRAP, and the frequency axis labels it as real** (found by the operator 2026-08-30, reading a groups.io thread about SDR++) | ✅ **FIXED AND VERIFIED ON AIR 2026-08-30. On `main`, unreleased** | Medium-Large | **The claim is correct.** The QMX's LO sits 12 kHz BELOW the dial (that is why the VFO lands at +12 kHz in baseband), so the 48 kHz of complex spectrum covers **dial−36 kHz … dial+12 kHz** - all real, but only 12 kHz of it above the dial. A display centred on the dial asks for ±24 kHz, and the top 12 kHz simply does not exist. ⛔ **We draw it anyway.** `ui.c`'s column mapping is `bin = ((b % N) + N) % N`, so at ×1 (`window_bins = N = 1024`, and the IF shift is **exactly 256 bins** since 12000/46.875 = 256) `bin_start = -256` and the columns run b = -256 ... +767. **x 0-319 = dial-24.00 ... dial-12.05, x 320-959 = dial-12.00 ... dial+11.95** - both correct and continuous - then **x 960-1279 = dial-36.00 ... dial-24.05**, i.e. the last 320 px (25%) plotted where the axis claims dial+12 ... dial+24. It is real data in the wrong place, discontinuous with its neighbour, not noise. At x2 (`bin_start = 0`, b runs 0..511) there is **no wrap** - the earlier claim of a 650 Hz sliver came from my own arithmetic slip and is withdrawn. Only x1 wraps. ⭐ **The axis is the harmful half**: `update_freq_axis_labels()` computes ticks linearly as `center_hz ± span/2`, so it LABELS the wrap as dial+12…+24; `spots_lane_set_view()` is handed the same window, and tap-to-tune uses the same mapping - so **tapping a signal in that quarter tunes ~48 kHz away from where it actually is**. That is the operational fault, not the duplicate trace. **Options, none chosen:** (a) blank/grey the invalid region and keep the dial centred - honest, costs a quarter of the ×1 view; (b) centre on the LO instead, giving a correct axis across the whole 48 kHz with the dial marker ~75% across rather than centred - more usable spectrum, but the dial stops being mid-screen; (c) clamp `bin_start` so the window never leaves the valid range. ⚠ **Possible relation to #118 (phantom CW), flagged as a HYPOTHESIS not a finding**: a strong signal at, say, dial−30 kHz would appear in that quarter where nothing should be - a genuine phantom. But this wrap is a TRANSLATED copy, not a mirrored one, so if Roy KI0ER's phantom is truly mirror-imaged this is a different fault. ⭐ **PARTLY CONFIRMED ON AIR 2026-08-30, and it CORRECTED me.** The operator tuned ~12 kHz below 14.074 at x1 and reported the FT8 signals simply **disappear**. That is the `dial+12 kHz` CEILING behaving exactly as the geometry predicts - at dial 14.062 the capture top IS 14.074, so the sub-band is at and above the edge and genuinely absent from the data. **First empirical confirmation of the capture window on our own hardware.** ⚠ But it does NOT test the wrap, and **the falsifying test I wrote had the direction wrong**: a signal reaches the wrapped quarter only when it lies in `dial-36k .. dial-24k`, i.e. when you tune **ABOVE** it by 24-36 kHz. **Correct test: sit at about 14.100 MHz and the 14.074 FT8 cluster should appear in the right-hand quarter, where the axis claims ~14.112-14.124.** Untested as of this writing. | ⭐ **THE FIX THE OPERATOR CHOSE, and the right shape for it.** He asked for "centre on the LO". Do NOT implement that as a special case: LO-centred at x1 and dial-centred at x2 makes the trace JUMP the instant you zoom. **One rule covers both with no discontinuity - keep the window centred on the dial, then CLAMP `bin_start` to the valid bin range.** At x1 the clamp lands on exactly the LO-centred window (it is the only 48 kHz window that fits), at x2 it shaves the ~650 Hz sliver, and at x4+ it changes nothing. The window then SLIDES as you zoom instead of jumping, and the dial marker walks from ~75% across back to centre. ⭐ **And the axis must be DERIVED from the same `bin_start`/`window_bins` the trace uses.** It currently computes its own `center_hz +/- span/2`, which is a second mapping - exactly the drift that produced the v1.8.1 tap-to-tune bug, and the reason `spur_marks_update()` already carries a comment calling bin_start/window_bins "the authoritative bin->x mapping". ⛔ **IMPACT SWEEP (2026-08-30) - every site that assumes THE DIAL IS AT SCREEN CENTRE.** Device trace/axis: `ui.c` center_bin (~6506) and the column map (~6547); **`render_waterfall.c:222` `wf_center` must move identically or the two panes disagree**; `update_freq_axis_labels()` (~3753); `spots_lane_set_view()` (follows the axis for free); `spur_marks_update()` already correct. Overlays hard-coding `+ DISPLAY_H_RES/2`: passband edges (~6441/6442, ~6620), **VFO cursor (~6649)**, **RIT marker (~6675)**, **tap-to-tune (~7571)**, snap cursor / `s_target_x` (~7611), `hz_per_px = 48000/DISPLAY_H_RES` (~5600), band-plan visible-span window (~3394) and its drag maths (~7385/7440/7513). Web: **`webserver_ws.c` sends the browser bins ALREADY wrapped** (`((bin + if_shift) % N + N) % N`), so the browser inherits both faults - plus `index.html` freq->px (~1233), axis labels (~4309-4325), click-to-tune (~4410, ~4754) and passband edges (~5230). **~19 sites, 4 files + the browser.** ✅ **EXPLICITLY UNAFFECTED, so nobody 'fixes' them:** `dsp_get_peak_dbm_around_vfo(vfo_bin, ...)` in `render.c:147`, `webserver.c:473` and `:3649` - those locate the VFO BIN, which does not move, so the S-meter and `signal_dbm` are correct as they stand. Zoom >= x2 via `dsp_set_zoom()` already mixes the pan target to DC with `center_bin = 0`. And the FT8/WSPR decode paths consume AUDIO, not the display mapping - decoding has never been affected by this. ⚠ **The right first move is a test, not an edit**: one authoritative `x <-> Hz` helper used by the trace, the axis, the cursor and the tune path, so they cannot drift again. ⭐ **PHASE 0 STARTED 2026-08-30: `main/util/pan_view.c/.h`** - one portable, stateless mapping between screen column, absolute frequency and FFT bin, with `test/pan_view_harness.c` linking the REAL functions. It owns the capture window (`dial-36k .. dial+12k`, derived from `if_offset_hz` so CW pitch and RIT come along), clamps any requested viewport into it, and returns **`PAN_VIEW_NO_DATA` instead of wrapping** - which is the fix. Also exports `pan_view_cursor_range()`, so #298's per-zoom table is derived rather than hardcoded. **8 of 8 mutations caught, including restoring the modulo wrap itself**, so the regression is now permanently guarded. Compiles for the device as well as the host. ⚠ **Deliberately NOT yet called from anywhere** - wiring the ~19 sites is the next step and is where the risk actually is; the arithmetic is settled first so that pass is mechanical. One thing the harness decided on its own: bin `n/2` is Nyquist and is a REAL bin, so it is accepted rather than rejected (which left the last column of a x1 view dead) or clamped (which would have told a small lie). ⭐ **DONE.** `pan_view` is wired into the spectrum and the waterfall: a column outside `dial-36k..dial+12k` returns `PAN_VIEW_NO_DATA` and is hatched (9-px diagonal, dark slate deliberately outside the waterfall LUT so it cannot read as a signal level; the stroke phase advances per row so it leans rather than forming false carriers). **Verified on air at 14.092 and 14.100**: the hatch starts at exactly dial+12 kHz and the FT8 cluster that used to appear in that quarter is gone. Two details that would have re-admitted the wrap through the side door: the flat-mode five-tap smoother had to skip no-data neighbours (with a guarded divisor, since at the seam every neighbour can be one), and only the BASE path is touched - at zoom >= 2 the zoom FFT has already mixed the pan target to DC and cannot wrap. ⚠ **The BROWSER still shows the old wrap** - `webserver_ws.c` sends pre-wrapped bins. Deliberately parked: *"leave out the web until we have it settled on the tab5"*. |

The right move is another bench A/B with the operator reading supply current, not a constant edit. Also worth exposing what we already know: `/api/wspr` reports `advised_dbm` from a real `PC;` measurement, so the number the operator declares is derived rather than guessed |
| 295 | 🟢 **FT4 replies went out a cycle late, so exchanges took twice as long** (Gyula HA3HZ) | ⛔ **REVERTED 2026-08-30 - the fix BROKE FT4 TX ENTIRELY in v1.10.2. Back to open** | Small | His logs show it plainly - six consecutive `LA3WP HA3HZ R-05` bursts before the partner's RR73 arrived. The **hold-for-decode gate** (v0.21.0, the fix for exactly this shape in FT8) was gated `!is_ft4` and so had never applied to FT4 at all: when a TX is due but the previous slot's decode is still in flight, the boundary must NOT fire the stale armed request. Gate removed, so FT4 now holds for the fresh reply the same way FT8 does. ⚠ **FT4's slot is 7.5 s, half FT8's**, and decode measures ~2.3 s - so the margin is real but thinner; `FT8_TX_HOLD_DEADLINE_MS` still fires whatever is armed as the backstop, meaning the worst case is exactly the old behaviour, never a skipped slot. Watch a field log for the deadline path firing constantly, which would mean decode has outgrown the FT4 slot | ⛔ **WHAT SHIPPED WAS HALF THE CHANGE, AND IT STOPPED FT4 TRANSMITTING.** I removed `!is_ft4` from the hold gate but the **late-fire path 180 lines below kept its own `!is_ft4`** - so an FT4 burst was held at the boundary and then nothing was permitted to fire it. FT4 never keyed, on CQ or on a reply, with the slot countdown running normally. `ft8_qso_is_busy()` is true throughout a CQ run, which is why calling CQ was hit too. Reported by **Gyula HA3HZ within hours of release** - the same operator the fix was for. **Fixed by making one flag (`late_fire_ok`) drive BOTH gates**, so the pair cannot be half-removed again; holding a burst that nothing may fire is the entire bug. FT4 is back to v1.10.1 behaviour (fires at the boundary, replies can be a cycle late) and FT8 keeps the hold. **Verified on hardware in sim: FT4 two bursts at 5046 ms firing at the boundary with no hold line, FT8 `holding for fresh reply` then firing 400 ms later.** ⭐ **To do it properly:** the late-fire window `FT8_REPLY_TX_WINDOW_MS` is 2800 ms, and an FT4 burst is 5040 ms in a 7500 ms slot, so firing at +2800 would **overrun the slot by 340 ms**. FT4 needs its own window at or under ~2400 ms before that path can be opened to it. That is the missing piece, and it is why the half-change could never have worked as written. ⭐ **Gyula's follow-up 2026-08-30 12:41 CONFIRMS the diagnosis rather than contradicting it**: *"When I went from FT8 to FT4, it switched to transmission the first time, but not after that."* That is v1.10.2's mechanism exactly. `hold_for_fresh` needs a decode IN FLIGHT, and the counters only move when a capture completes - so straight after the mode switch nothing is in flight and the **first** due burst fires; that slot is spent transmitting so nothing is captured; the next RX slot queues a decode, and from then on **every** TX-due boundary has the preceding slot's decode in flight, is held, and in v1.10.2 nothing was allowed to fire it. **Impossible in v1.10.3**, where `hold_for_fresh` is hardwired false for FT4. ⭐ **And the fix is proven by CODE EQUIVALENCE, which is stronger than the bench test I wrongly claimed**: for FT4, v1.10.1 evaluates `hold_for_fresh = !is_ft4 && ...` -> false and gates late-fire on `!is_ft4`; v1.10.3 evaluates `late_fire_ok = !is_ft4` then `hold_for_fresh = late_fire_ok && ...` -> false and gates late-fire on `late_fire_ok`. **Term for term identical to the version he was running successfully.** ⚠ Not fully settled: he did not name a version, so ask him which build - it is one line and it is the only thing that could overturn this.

| 292 | 🔴 **A logged QSO can be missing a signal report - the ADIF viewer shows a dash** (Gyula HA3HZ, reported 2026-08-26 and AGAIN on v1.9.6/v1.10.0) | ✅ **FIXED and VERIFIED IN THE SIMULATOR 2026-08-30** (not yet field-confirmed) | Medium | **Root cause: the report was thrown away by the branch that had just read it.** When a partner's message already reports us (`HA3HZ UN6GO -02`), `ft8_qso_build_manual_reply()` answers `R-06` and `ft8_qso_start()` enters at `WAIT_RR73` - #234's own shortcut, because *"the partner's OWN last message already reported us"*. That skips `WAIT_RPT`, the state that normally records their report; if they then jump straight to `RR73`, `WAIT_ROGER` is skipped too, and the QSO logs `RST_SENT` with an empty `RST_RCVD`. Fixed by carrying the heard report (tagged with the callsign it came from, so a preview build for another row cannot hand over the wrong number) and recording it as `RST_RCVD` at QSO start. **Confirmed against his logs**: 2 of 24 logged QSOs in the sample were missing it - UN6GO (`-02`) and LA3WP (`-11`) - both on this exact path, both FT4, both ending in a partner `RR73`. The entry is at `WAIT_RR73` (`qso_state=4`), so `WAIT_ROGER` **cannot** run on this path - which is what makes the value attributable to the fix and nothing else. ⛔ **The FIRST apparent pass was a false one and nearly went in the notes:** an N5XYZ record read `(-02/-09)` and looked right, but `grep -c 'R-report'` returned **0** - it had taken the report-first fallback and the `-09` came from the ordinary `WAIT_ROGER` handler. **Every phantom rogers with -09, so the field's VALUE proves nothing; only the entry path does.** Check the path, not the number. |

Not closed: the remaining headroom is gated on core-0, which is a different problem — see #285** | Medium-Large | The operator's framing is the right one: *"what would happen when we implement the CW page? Then we can as well say to our users: you won't get more features, all resources are eaten up."* The SD card unmounting is only the first symptom - FatFs cannot get a sector buffer, `fopen` returns EIO, and the archive unmounts a healthy card. ⭐ **MEASURED with `util/dma_owners.c` (#283), by owning task**: app_main's (self-deleted) handle 146 KB, `ipc0` 56 KB, `sys_evt` 30 KB, `wifi` 8.7 KB - 232 KB of 253 KB in three buckets. Bluetooth is **1,128 B**, so it is the straw and not the load. ⭐ **AND BY BLOCK, which is what makes this tractable**: the top 30 blocks are 184,964 B = **73%** of the whole region, and nearly half of that is TWO REPEATED FIXED SIZES - **17 x 4,352 B = 73,984 B** (owners app_main + `sys_evt`) and **7 x 5,376 B = 37,632 B** (owners **all `ipc0`**), plus 3 x 8,704 B and one 32,772 B. **Repeated identical allocations are BUFFER POOLS, and pools have counts.** That is the same shape as the two tunings this codebase has already done and documented - mDNS (interfaces 3->1, services 10->2, recovered 13 KB) and NimBLE (connections 3->1, ACL 24->6, dma 943 B -> 26,415 B). `ipc0` owning every 5,376 B block points at the esp_hosted/WiFi path; the generalisation in CLAUDE.md's mDNS note applies directly: *a managed component's defaults are sized for a generic device - check its Kconfig for interface/buffer counts and cut them to what we actually have BEFORE assuming the cost is intrinsic.* **ELIMINATED by measurement, do not re-investigate**: `.bss` creep (154,290 B in libmain vs the 153,906 already recorded - no drift); the NimBLE tuning going missing (present in BOTH `sdkconfig` and `sdkconfig.defaults`, under the `TRANSPORT_*` key names); Bluetooth as the consumer (a one-variable A/B with a reboot: 8.0 KB free with it off, 4.7 KB with it on - and the card survives at 8.0 and dies at 4.7); task stacks (25+ `psram_task_create` call sites already move them to PSRAM). ⚠ **Why it "worked for a long time and suddenly stopped"**: nothing single changed. The pool held 40-41 KB free after the #65 fix on 2026-08-05; since then `dxcluster`, `mdns_svc`, `psk_rx`, `wsprnet`, `cloudlog_upload`, `ota_update`, `qmx_term` and the whole WSPR subsystem landed, each taking its share, until BLE's 1 KB became the difference. ⛔ **Next step is a deliberate tuning pass, not more instrumenting**: identify the two pools (raise `MAX_BLOCKS` in dma_owners.c past 900 first - the block scan reported TRUNCATED), find their Kconfig counts, cut to what this board has, and re-measure. ⚠ Measure on a build WITHOUT `CONFIG_HEAP_TASK_TRACKING`: it adds a handle per block header, so free figures on the instrumented build read low ⭐⭐ **SESSION OF 2026-08-28 — what the pool actually is, and what was DONE.** **It is TASK STACKS**: `dma_owners.c` gained an exact `pxStackBase`/`xHandle` match (containment, not equality — the tracking build's 4-byte owner header means a stack pointer can never equal a block address, and the first version reported *no* stacks at all) plus a size histogram. Result: **24 of the top 34 blocks, 115 KB, are task stacks**. The two 'pools' from the previous handoff are named — **6 × 5,376 B** are esp_hosted's SDIO/RPC tasks (`os_wrapper.h` hardcodes `DFLT_TASK_STACK_SIZE`/`RPC_TASK_STACK_SIZE` as `(5*1024)` and **ignores** `CONFIG_ESP_HOSTED_DFLT_TASK_STACK=3072`; measured peaks 716–3,044 B, so ~32 KB is reclaimable but it edits the SDIO link and becomes a 7th standing patch) and **16 × 4,352 B** are simply every 4,096-byte stack in the system. **SHIPPED (kept, in the tree, uncommitted):** httpd 12288→10240, cat_link 8192→5120, ws_push 4096→3072, usb_lib 4096→2560 (each sized from a measured high-water mark, recorded at the call site), rigctld's listener **and per-connection client tasks moved to PSRAM stacks** with park/reap — that one matters most, since 4 clients × 8,704 B was 34.8 KB against a pool with 1.9 KB free, i.e. **rigctld could not have accepted a single client**; hardware-verified over 6 connect/disconnect cycles (`f`/`m`/`v` all correct, count back to 0, psram flat at 15,365 KB, no leak) — and `PSRAM_TASK_MAX` 8→16 in the same change because rigctld now needs 5 slots and WSPR holds 3, i.e. the table was exactly full. ⛔ **THE BIG LEVER, FOUND AND THEN REJECTED:** `CONFIG_CACHE_L2_CACHE_256KB` → `_128KB` returns **exactly 128 KB** to the pool (the L2 cache is carved out of the same L2MEM; heap top was exactly `0x4ffc0000 − 256 KB`). Measured `dma free` 10,071 B → ~101,000 B, internal free 39 KB (min 2 KB) → 144 KB (min 68 KB). **Reverted**: code and rodata are fetched through that cache (`SPIRAM_FETCH_INSTRUCTIONS`/`RODATA`/`XIP` all on) and panadapter core-0 idle collapsed 6.9–7.6 % → 0.1–1.5 % with a visibly jerky waterfall. ⛔ **THREE OF MY OWN THEORIES FALSIFIED ON HARDWARE THE SAME DAY — do not re-take them**, reasons at the call sites: shrinking the rotation working set (`buffer_size` 36→12) made core-0 idle **worse**; moving taskLVGL to core 1 **broke the device** (starved `fft_task`, `DROPPED=48000 (ring full)` every second, WiFi down — #51 by another route); and `tab5_kb` 4096→2048, sized from a peak measured with the keyboard *attached*, crashed the device at 18 min with 52 bytes of stack left. ⚠ **The SD symptom was WRONGLY attributed to this item.** With the card mounted and then failing, the log reads `SDFAIL[slowopen] err=0x5` — **errno 5, EIO** — with **135 KB of DMA free and a 65 KB largest block**. That is the WiFi/GDMA contention already documented in CLAUDE.md's SD section, not starvation. A successful mount must not be credited to memory headroom either. **REMAINING, and why this stays open:** ~32 KB in esp_hosted's stacks (fragile), and the 128 KB behind the cache — which only becomes available if core 0 ever gets headroom. That is **#285**, and it is now the gating item for every future feature, not memory. |

| 281 | 🔴 **The FT8 per-slot log walked the heap with interrupts off - the documented cyan-flash cause, on the hottest periodic path there is** | 🟢 Fixed `pending` - operator SAW the flash, cause found by reading code, not guessed | Small | The operator got a full-screen cyan flash during an FT4 simulation run on 2026-08-28. `ft8_test.c`'s per-slot diagnostic called **`heap_caps_get_largest_free_block(MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL)` unconditionally, once per slot** - every 15 s in FT8 and **every 7.5 s in FT4**. That walk runs inside the heap spinlock with interrupts off, which is precisely what the 2026-07-13 investigation removed from `cpu_stats.c` and `audio.c` to stop this exact symptom: the ints-off window delays the core-0 MIPI-DSI frame-restart ISR past vertical blanking and the panel blanks for a frame. ⛔ **`audio.c` carries the rule in a comment** - *"must NEVER go on this periodic path (that caused the FT4 cyan flash)"* - while this call sat two files away doing it, at twice the rate in FT4. It was simply MISSED when the other two were fixed, and survived for over a year. **It was worse on the day**: internal free was 17-18 KB, below `audio.c`'s own 24 KB emergency threshold, so BOTH walkers were running - its 10 s one and this 7.5 s one. **Fixed** the same way `audio.c` does it: the walk happens only below `FT8_SLOT_LBLK_EMERGENCY_KB` (24), and `lblk` prints 0 otherwise. ⭐ **The whole class was swept**, per the standing rule: the only other `largest_free_block` / `uxTaskGetSystemState` / `heap_caps_get_info` calls left in `main/` are one-shot on the OTA path and capped at 3 calls on the rare SD-failure path, both of which the rule already permits. ⚠ NOT yet flashed, so not confirmed absent on hardware - and a cyan flash is rare enough that its absence is weak evidence anyway. The fix is correct-by-construction against a documented rule, which is the honest claim |

| 279 | ✅ **Every FT8 exit leaked ~218 KB of PSRAM, and every WSPR visit another 64 KB - a task freeing neither its stack nor its TCB** | ✅ **FIXED and measured flat 2026-08-28** | Medium | `ft8_task` (64 KB), `ft8_dec` (64 KB) and `ft8_dec0` (32 KB) are all created with `xTaskCreatePinnedToCoreWithCaps` and all end with a plain **`vTaskDelete(NULL)`**. IDF's own source says exactly what that costs: *"the idle task will not free the task TCB and stack memories we created statically during xTaskCreateWithCaps()... Therefore, it will leak memory."* (`idf_additions.c`, above `vTaskDeleteWithCaps`). **Measured on the dev bench, three Panadapter/WSPR round trips, monotonic: PSRAM 15153 -> 14946 -> 14540 -> 14498 KB**, i.e. ~218 KB per FT8 exit - the 160 KB of stacks plus the 38 KB shared FFT scratch. At that rate ~70 mode toggles in a session exhaust free PSRAM, and the symptom would be FT8 quietly failing to start after a lot of switching. **NOT fixed with the 2026-08-28 WSPR mode fix on purpose**: `main` has the identical pattern (5 WithCaps creates, 15 plain deletes), so this is not a WSPR regression, and CLAUDE.md is emphatic that FT8 task lifecycle is where the v0.18.1 double-spawn and v0.19.5 worker-join crashes both live - it wants its own change and a soak, not a seventh edit on a branch about to merge. ⚠ The sanctioned fix (`vTaskDeleteWithCaps(NULL)`) is not free either: for a SELF-deleting task IDF spawns a temporary cleanup task from **internal** RAM to do the freeing, which is the resource this board is shortest of. Check what happens when that spawn fails before adopting it  ⭐ **VERIFIED FLAT ON HARDWARE**: eight Panadapter/WSPR round trips on the merged main build gave PSRAM **15159 KB with a delta of exactly 0 across seven consecutive trips**, against **-218 KB/trip monotonic** before (15153 -> 14946 -> 14540 -> 14498). 33 reap events, 0 reap-slot errors, 0 not-reapable warnings, 0 respawn-watchdog firings. ⚠ An INTERMEDIATE version of this fix measured as no fix at all and looked correct while doing so - registering every `psram_task_create()` filled the 12-slot table with the ~24 permanent tasks, so the few that actually park found no slot and silently fell back to `vTaskDelete()`. Registration is opt-in (`psram_task_create_reapable()`) for exactly that reason, and both failure modes now log. The reap prints the PSRAM it recovers, which is the only reason the residual was caught rather than read as fragmentation - the #189 lesson, applied. |

This is also what defeated the reverted 2026-08-12 attempt. (2) ⭐ **BLE never puts the report ID on the wire** - unlike USB HID, each report has its own characteristic and the ID lives only in the Report Reference descriptor, so routing by ID cannot work; reports are matched by **declared payload size** instead. (3) That same fact then broke the MOUSE, because the long read made its descriptor parse for the first time and the code began expecting an ID byte that is never sent - found in the log in four lines after the operator asked *"why dont you look what happens in the log?"*, having watched me fix on hypothesis twice. **Two devices at once** needed `CONFIG_BT_NIMBLE_MAX_CONNECTIONS=2` and, more importantly, moving the report-map buffer and both layouts into a **per-link struct in PSRAM** - they were file statics, and I had written that they would never contend; hardware falsified it within minutes (the mouse froze the moment the keyboard connected). Scanning also has to RESUME after a connect while a slot is free, or the second device can never be found. ⭐ **The long-unexplained 30 s BLE drop is explained**: reason 531 = HCI 0x13 *Remote User Terminated* - the peripheral idles out and hangs up, which is normal Logitech behaviour, not a fault; the old code comment had the reasoning backwards. ⚠ **US layout only** - a HID keyboard sends POSITIONS, so Danish and other national punctuation is wrong; letters, digits, Enter and the arrows are layout-independent so shortcuts and numeric fields are fine. Needs a per-language table and a setting before this reaches users - **see #294**. His power question (should BLE time out when nothing is paired) is still unanswered, see #270 | ⭐ **CONFIRMED ON DON'S OWN Rii i4, 2026-08-30 (#178025)** - the second device and the one the request came from: *"letters, upper/lower case, back, delete, enter and the mouse functions are all working... The clip-on and BT touchpads and keyboards are all useable while all are connected and interact smoothly with the TAB 5 touch screen. The 4-way up-down-left-right cursor and its OK button function as expected."* He also confirms the sleep/reconnect behaviour reads correctly (*"times out, 5 seconds or so... and the link picks back up as expected, correctly reporting its state"*). So the snap-on keyboard, a BT keyboard, a BT touchpad and the touchscreen all coexist - which nothing on this bench could have shown, since it has one of each at most. He did not exercise Alt/Fn/AltGr, which is where the US-layout limit (#294) will bite first. |

The out-of-band slider is a good lead - it is a RELATIVE control that writes on every drag tick, so three sweeps is a burst of CAT writes plus repaints |

Is it possible, you are doing too much in the background such that communications to a PuTTY-like display on the web-page gets corrupted?" **Not corruption:** `term_json_row()` serialises all 80 columns (only trailing blanks are trimmed), so the browser receives the whole screen. The `<pre id="term-screen">` sits in a `max-width:960px` box with `overflow-x:auto`, so on a narrower window the right of an 80-column line is simply scrolled out of sight - and nothing says it can be scrolled. Fix by making it FIT: size the monospace font from the box width (80 ch) rather than fixing it at 14 px, so the screen scales down instead of overflowing. The Tab5's own view already solves this with a font chosen for 80x24  ⭐ **Done:** `termFit()` in `index.html` sizes the monospace font so 80 columns fit the box, re-run on every render and on window resize. The character width is MEASURED with a probe span that inherits the pre's own font rather than assumed at 0.6 em - which fallback font the browser picked decides it, so an assumed ratio would be wrong on some machines and silently wrong. Font size is reset to the 15 px base before each measurement so repeated calls cannot ratchet downward, and `overflow-x` stays as the backstop below the 6 px floor. **Verified in a real browser against the real function** (pulled out of `index.html` by the harness so it cannot drift): 1400 px -> 15 px, 820 px -> 15 px, 536 px -> 10.6 px, 375 px iPhone -> 7 px, and `scrollWidth == clientWidth` at every one of them, with the column-80 marker visible on screen |
| 276 | **In CW, tuning is followed by one or two automatic corrections** (Samuel W7STF) | ❓ Cause STILL NOT established - waiting on his before/after numbers. The dead `snap_to_peak` setting it was NOT is now removed | Small | "when tuning a CW signal, the tuning is set, then there are one or two automatic corrections to it? Is that some form of intentional CW Tune, or snap function? If so, how do I turn that behavior on/off?" ⛔ **Checked before answering: snap-to-signal is DEAD CODE.** `dsp_find_peak_hz_around()` has no caller anywhere in `main/`, and the `snap_to_peak` setting is still stored and still defaults to true while doing nothing - so there is no switch to offer him and the honest answer is not "turn it off". ⭐ **The dead setting is now REMOVED** (operator's call, 2026-08-28): `snap_to_peak` is gone from `qmx_settings_t`, with its NVS key `snap_peak`, dirty bit 39 and both flush/load lines, and `dsp_find_peak_hz_around()` and its declaration went with it. Nothing read the field - there was no setter, no getter, no drawer row, no web row and no config-export entry, so the flush branch was unreachable and the stored value could never do anything. Existing units keep an orphaned `snap_peak` key in NVS, which is harmless. The historical rows in `docs/mkdocs/releases.md` are left alone: they describe what shipped at the time and are still true of those releases. Real candidates: the optimistic display value being replaced by the radio's own readback on the next `FA;` poll (a few Hz), or the CW offset compensation arriving late (`cw_offset_refresh()` re-reads it every 5 s in CW - hundreds of Hz). **The size of the correction tells the two apart**, so ask him for the before and after numbers |
| 269 | 🔴 **After a band change something retunes the radio BACK to the old band ~11 s later** (Markus DL8MBY, v1.9.6, QMX+) | 🟠 **Mechanism PROVEN from his log; caller unidentified. Reply sent 2026-08-28** - awaiting: was the FT8 preset list open, and does it happen when changing band on the Tab5 rather than the browser | Medium | "I switched to 15m and the FW programmed the QMX+ properly to the 15m FT8 frequency but in the Tab5 and in my web browser the frequency remain still at the 20m... typing 21074000 the frequency switch for a short period of an half second to the 21.074 MHz but immediately after this period the old 20m band frequency appear in the Tab5 as well as in the Browser. (not in the QMX+ display)" **What has been ruled out already:** `cat.c` is UNCHANGED in v1.9.6 (`git diff v1.9.5..v1.9.6`), and the three frequency writers the release did add are all one-shot - the Radio-menus dial restore (#261), the API preset fallback (#255) and wheel-to-tune - so none of them can revert a display twice a second. **The shape says the FA poll is winning with a stale value**: ~0.5 s is the poll's own cadence, and the radio itself stays right. Candidates, undiscriminated: (a) the radio is receiving on **VFO B** or in **split** while `FA;` reports VFO A - `cat.c:966` already warns that this exact trap makes our handler take the wrong value as the dial, and Markus is the same operator the v1.8.7 "left receiving on VFO B" fix came from; (b) our write lands on VFO A while the radio operates on B, so the radio's display never changes and the poll reads back A; (c) something else re-asserting 14.074. ⛔ **Do not fix on a theory** - ask for the diagnostic download, which records every FA change and every write, plus how he changed band (QMX+ front panel, Tab5 band button, or web) and whether the QMX+ LCD shows ONE frequency or two ⭐ **SETTLED BY HIS DIAG LOG (2026-08-27): it is not a display bug - the Tab5 actively retunes the radio back.** The whole time, the CAT poll reports the CORRECT frequency (`poll heartbeat ... freq=21074000Hz` for minutes), so nothing is stale on the read side. Then: `I (2429999) cat: Sent: FA00021074000;` (his band change, good), and **11.5 s later `I (2441501) cat: Sent: FA00014074000;`** - the old band, written by us. Both writes are followed by `ft8_screen: decode list cleared` and by a settings flush of the `ft8_freq_hz` dirty bit, which together identify the caller as **`apply_freq_preset()`** - it is the only path that retunes, clears the decode list AND persists the frequency. **Ruled out:** the CAT poll (it never disagreed), and the API/web drain (v1.9.6 logs `API: switching to ...` before calling, and that line appears nowhere in the log - so despite "all my freq settings were done from the browser", the write did not come through the API). **That leaves the two Tab5 preset-row callbacks** (`ft8_freq_preset_cb` / `ft4_freq_preset_cb`, ft8_screen_view.c:2258/2284) - something is firing one of them with 14074000 about 11 s after the band change. ⛔ **Next step is an instrument, not a theory:** make `apply_freq_preset()` log WHO called it (a source string at each of the three call sites). One line, and his next log names the culprit outright. Also ask him whether the FT8 frequency popup was open on the Tab5 screen at the time. ⚠ Note his own report says the QMX+ display did not follow the reversion - the log says the radio did go back, so that part of his description is mistaken and should not be used as a constraint |
| 266 | 🔴 **An automatic update downloaded, rebooted the Tab5 by itself, and came back on the OLD version - twice** (Steve N9SZ, on v1.9.3) | 🟡 **Mitigated `pending`; the cause is a HYPOTHESIS until his log arrives** | Medium | "When booting my TAB5 it began to automatically update from 1.9.3 to 1.9.5 - it appeared to download 1.9.5 fully and then soft rebooted (blue screen?) and came back in 1.9.3... After the second soft reboot, I tapped the version number and performed the update with the modal window. After this download (the third) it appeared to do a hard reboot and came up in 1.9.5." ⭐ **The shape is explained by where `esp_https_ota_finish()` sits**: it verifies the image AND sets the boot partition, so a reset DURING the verify loses the download and leaves the old image bootable - exactly "it downloaded and I am still on 1.9.3". The verify is also the memory-hungry end of the job; `ota_update.c`'s own instrumentation records it dying inside segment 0 with the internal heap at 10 KB free / 4 KB largest. And the automatic download fired **30 s after boot** (`FIRST_DELAY_MS`), the worst moment on this board, with **no heap guard and no uptime guard at all**. **Mitigation shipped:** an AUTOMATIC download now waits for 5 minutes of uptime and 32 KB of free internal RAM, logging which guard held it; the MANUAL path keeps neither, since the operator is watching and that path is the one that worked for him. **Ask Steve for the diagnostic download** - the crash record (#117) says whether it was a watchdog and where. ⚠ Until then this is a well-supported hypothesis, not a diagnosis |

OFF still checks and still ANNOUNCES a new version - which is what all three asked to keep - it just does not spend the 3.3 MB until asked. Applying remains a deliberate press either way |

| 265 | **Every phantom in the simulator is +9/+10 dB, always** (operator, 2026-08-26) | ✅ **Fixed in the WSPR merge and DEVICE-VERIFIED 2026-08-28** - the row saying "not started" was stale | Small | His observation, and it blocks testing: the sim synthesises each message at one fixed amplitude, so the REAL decoder measures the same SNR every time. Two consequences. The decode list looks like no band that has ever existed - every station identical, no weak ones. And anything SNR-dependent cannot be exercised at all: #264's report refresh only rebuilds when the number CHANGES, so a 9/10 flip is the entire dynamic range available to test it with. Fix: give each phantom its own signal level and let it drift slowly, a few dB per slot with the occasional deep fade, spread over roughly -18..+12 dB so some are genuinely marginal. Cleanest at the source - scale the synthesised waveform's amplitude in `ft8_synth_and_decode()` so the decoder genuinely measures it - rather than adding an offset to the number afterwards, which would make the sim lie about its own measurement. ⚠ That function is shared with the boot self-tests, so any new argument needs a default that leaves them byte-identical. **This is the second time a sim limitation has blocked verification** - see #256, where phantoms never engage in FT4  ⭐ **Already implemented**: each phantom carries its own `level_db`, seeded across `SIM_LEVEL_MIN_DB`..`SIM_LEVEL_MAX_DB` (-18..+12) and drifting -3..+3 dB per slot with a 1-in-32 deeper fade, and the level is passed to `ft8_synth_and_decode_at()` so the SNR is one the decoder MEASURED rather than one the sim announced. **Verified on the bench 2026-08-28**: injected SNRs in one FT4 run were +2, -9, -4, +2 and the logged QSO carried `RST_SENT=-02 / RST_RCVD=-09` - two different numbers, from a path that used to produce +9/+10 every time |
| 264 | **A re-sent report keeps the SNR from when we first called** (operator, 2026-08-26) | ✅ **Fixed and OPERATOR-VERIFIED on hardware**: "snr now updating per TX cycle" | ✅ **Fixed and OPERATOR-VERIFIED**: "snr now updating per TX cycle" | "Answering a CQ'ing station sends the SNR report alright - but if the station does not reply immediately then I should call with a new fresh (and often different) SNR report. Now it stays on the original." A report is a MEASUREMENT, and re-sending a stale one every 15 s means the number that finally gets through, and into both logs, describes a signal from minutes ago. `refresh_our_report()` already existed but fired only when the partner answered us and repeated themselves - the case that does NOT persist. The one that does is a station working down a pileup: heard every slot, addressing us in none of them. New `refresh_report_from_heard()` reads that slot's decode out of the heard table (`ft8_screen_find_call`) and is called from the no-progress path of **WAIT_ROGER** (the reported case: skip-TX1 answers a CQ and starts there), **WAIT_RR73** (our R<report>) and **WAIT_RPT**. ⛔ Gated on `s_rst_sent[0]` - our message must ALREADY carry a report, or a plain pounce TX1 (our grid) would silently turn into a report |

POTA/SOTA will not add a dry run, but nothing stops the Tab5 checking its own file: every record has the fields POTA needs (CALL, QSO_DATE, TIME_ON, BAND, MODE, STATION_CALLSIGN, MY_SIG/MY_SIG_INFO), the activation count deduplicated by callsign (which `adif_log_count_activation()` already does), and any reference that does not look like a reference. Report it in the web log editor and the Tab5's Activation modal. **The parts already exist** - the count, `spot_sig_for_ref()`'s shape rule, and `adif_log_extract_field()`; this is a pass over the file plus somewhere to show the result. ⚠ It must never read as "POTA will accept this" - it can only say the file is not obviously incomplete |

In Safari that property makes the scroller a containing block for `position:fixed` descendants and gives it its own stacking context — which is exactly the two symptoms, one report each (clipped away entirely / rendered behind the page). **Blink ignores the property outright**, which is why desktop Chrome at 1280×720 AND emulated 375×812 portrait both place the menu correctly with `elementFromPoint` returning its own item — measured, not assumed. Two changes: the deprecated property is gone (iOS 13+ scrolls with momentum natively), and every `.bot-menu` is **moved to `<body>` at wiring time** so no bar styling can ever reach them again. I picked the function that answers *"would a click here act on a control?"* when the question was *"what would LVGL hand the press to?"* - two different questions that had one implementation. Mode goes through `cat_request_mode()` (the poll task owns the pipe; the close can run on an HTTP worker, the idle watchdog or the LVGL task). ⚠ **Accepted cost, stated in the code:** someone who went into Band Config to change band DELIBERATELY has it undone. | 262 | **Install the panadapter from the M5Stack launcher (bmorcelli) instead of a flasher** (Mark Hogan N5OBC) | ⚠️ **Assessed against the real numbers — needs the operator's decision, and it costs a feature** | Medium | ⭐ **The launcher already supports the Tab5** (`boards/m5stack-tab5` in `bmorcelli/Launcher`, `-DOTA_TAG="tab5"`), so the question is purely the flash map. **Its Tab5 table gives an app slot of 0x180000 = 1.5 MB** (`support_files/custom_16Mb_p4.csv`) and has no SPIFFS partition at all; **our binary is 3.3 MB and needs the `storage` partition** for the QSO log and the LoTW certificate + private key. So the released image cannot be installed into its layout as-is — this is a layout conversation, not a packaging one. **The hard constraint:** `user_nvs` (0x810000) and `storage` (0x820000) MUST NOT MOVE or every field unit loses its log and its LoTW key. **What that leaves, and it is more than it sounds:** `ota_1` sits ABOVE storage (0x930000-0xD30000), so **dropping it frees 4 MB without moving one byte of user data** — with the ~2.1 MB already unused at the top that is ~6 MB for a launcher plus other apps. The cost is the third app slot: `factory` (cable-flashed known-good) + `ota_0` (OTA target) still update over the air, so OTA survives, but the escape hatch narrows. ⚠ **Second unknown, needs checking before promising anything:** the Tab5 launcher build sets `-DBOOT_LOGIC_ON_NVS=1`, i.e. it chooses what to boot from NVS, while our OTA writes `otadata` — those two mechanisms have to be shown not to fight each other. Cheap first experiment: ask Mark to install the current release `.bin` through the launcher's SD method and send the diag log; the SPIFFS mount line answers half of it immediately |

Change is: `SIG_INFO` added to that endpoint's allowlist (reports were the only editable fields), reference validated and uppercased with a **required dash** so `US1241` is refused rather than uploaded as a park claim, `SIG` **derived** from the reference's shape via `spot_sig_for_ref()` (never typed, so the two cannot disagree; cleared with it), and a **P2P ref** column in the web viewer. `set_field()` also got the verify-before-commit fix that `delete_record()` got in v1.9.5 — the same latent bug, and this path is now used far more |
| 252 | **FT4 must be `MODE=MFSK` + `SUBMODE=FT4`, not `MODE=FT4`** (Don WB0LQW) | 🟢 Done `pending`; **LoTW side reasoned + host-tested, NOT re-verified against the live service** | Medium | ADIF defines FT8 as a mode but FT4 only as a submode of MFSK; **ADIFMaster refuses to load a file that declares FT4 as a mode at all**, which is what he hit while editing his log. POTA accepts either. ⭐ **The check the operator asked for, answered:** ARRL's own announcement says TQSL config 11.8 "includes FT4 as a submode of MFSK", i.e. `MFSK`+`FT4` is the canonical accepted form (it is what WSJT-X writes), and eQSL since 2026-03-01 explicitly remaps a mode that is really a submode — so the receiving ends already prefer this. **But LoTW has no MFSK mode of its own**, and we sign our own TQ8s rather than handing an ADIF to TQSL, so `lotw_mode_from_adif()` (portable, in `lotw_tq8.c`, 8 cases in `test/lotw_harness.c`, mutation-tested) collapses the pair back to `FT4` before signing: **the TQ8 bytes are unchanged from what LoTW already accepts**. Also scoped the legacy duplicate-SUBMODE migration to the `MODE==SUBMODE` pairing only — a blind sweep would have stripped the new, correct submode at the next boot. ⚠ **Old records keep `MODE=FT4`**; a one-time rewrite is possible but touches the operator's log, so it is a decision, not something to do quietly. Today-only export (#170) means a fresh activation's file is all new-format |

But QRZ, eQSL, LoTW and the operator's own record all use them, so trimming for one of four upload targets costs the other three. If it is wanted, it belongs behind the activation-scoped download (`/api/adif?activation=`), not in the log itself |

| 255 | **`set_ft8_mode` with no `freq_hz` silently does nothing when CAT is down** (found while verifying #252, 2026-08-26) | ✅ **Already fixed - this row was stale** (found 2026-08-30 while looking for easy work) | Small | `ft8_screen_view.c`'s drain now falls back to the stored `ft8_freq_hz` when `cat_get_frequency()` answers 0, with the reasoning in the code comment: it is what the FT8 screen would have tuned to anyway. ⚠ **Third stale row found in one pass** (with #256 and #265) - all three were fixed in the WSPR merge and never marked. `scratchpad/open_items.txt` is generated from this file, so it repeated all three as "not started" |

Bench-only impact, but the sim is how FT4 changes get verified without a radio, so it costs every future FT4 test  ⭐ **Both halves were already done** - `sim_cur_slot()`/`sim_next_slot()` work in ms on `ft8_op_mode_slot_ms()`'s grid and truncate the way the engine does.
