That is the ADIF-correct MODE/SUBMODE pair from v1.9.6 confirmed **on hardware** for the first time. ⚠ One partner (VK3ABC) stalled at the roger and timed out into the grey-list during the same run - normal QSO-timeout behaviour on the face of it, but nobody has checked whether FT4 times out more readily than FT8, so do not read this run as proving the exchange is robust, only that it can complete |

| 239b | **The band plan was "super difficult to land on"** (operator) | ✅ **Done + operator-verified**: "band-plan is much easier to hit now" | — | 22 px with the bottom bar hard against it below and the waterfall hard above, each doing something different with a tap - land high and you get a cyan tune line, land low and you get the update window. `BP_CATCH_PX` (50) adds an INVISIBLE catcher above the strip, so its touch area is 72 px while it still draws as 22; tap-to-tune gives up the same 50 px, which a 370 px waterfall can spare. So: does FILTER break after a plain `ota_reboot`? **Yes → this is a WARM-RESET bug and OTA is incidental** (cold boot via the power button is the only thing that fixes it, which fits — the PI4IO expander and the ST7121/ST7123 touch controller both retain state across an ESP32 reset, and CLAUDE.md already warns that a second touch probe leaves the I2C bus dirty). **No → it is specific to the OTA path** (running from `ota_0`, or post-download heap state). Run it with the **QMX switched OFF** — the Filter modal is UI-only, and a warm reset with the radio attached is the documented #74 trigger. | **🆕 2026-08-21 — the morning-after-v1.8.9 batch (Randy N4OPI ×5, Don N2VGU)** — all FIXED locally, none verified on hardware yet | | | | |
| 229 | 🔴 **The web UI took ~95 SECONDS to load — it read as "unreachable"** (operator, and me) | 🟢 **FIXED + hw-verified: 95 s → 4.0 s cold, 0.25 s on reload** | — | Two different PCs could not open the page while `/api/status` answered instantly - the signature of a bandwidth problem, not a reachability one. MEASURED: the page was **263 KB served UNCOMPRESSED** with `Cache-Control: no-store`, so every visit re-downloaded all of it; the live spectrum WebSocket holds **1026 B × 10 fps = 10.0 KB/s** continuously; and the whole esp_hosted/SDIO link measured **~12.7 KB/s**. The page therefore got 2.7 KB/s → **95 s**. ⚠ **A RATCHET, not a constant**: the page grows every release (260,945 → 263,150 bytes from one morning's edits), so this has been getting quietly worse for a long time and fits the shape of Samuel W7STF's "worse every release" - #193 was the same congestion seen from the WebSocket side. Three fixes, each measured: **gzip at build time** (263 KB → 83 KB, 3.2× - `tools/gzip_asset.py` + a CMake rule, GENERATED not committed, because `manual.bin` is the cautionary tale of a committed derived file shipping stale); **pause the WS while the page is in flight** (the page then gets the whole link - same courtesy the QRZ/eQSL/LoTW uploads already pay, and a browser fetching the page has no WebSocket open yet so nothing is lost); and an **ETag = the running firmware's ELF hash**, so a reload of unchanged firmware is a 304 and an update always re-fetches. Bonus: the binary shrank 175 KB (headroom 18% → 22%). All three paths verified over the wire - 200/3.98 s cold, 304/0 bytes/0.25 s matching, 200 re-fetch on a stale tag - and the WS resumed at 10.0 fps with **0 partial writes** afterwards, because it is no longer fighting a page download |
| 223 | **Web FT8: the TX power/SWR readout is hidden by the exchange status block** (Randy N4OPI) | ✅ **Done + operator-verified** (took four rounds - see the CSS comment block; ended as a wrapping row of boxes, not a pinned overlay) | — | He asked for the readout to MOVE; the cause is that it does not need to. `#ft8-tx` was sticky at `top:0` and `#ft8-webr` sticky at `top:3.4rem`, each offset BY HAND, while `#ft8-pwr` was left in the normal flow with no offset at all - so on any scroll the opaque `#ft8-webr` floated over it. His screenshot shows exactly that: "Last TX:" and "min ago)" peeking out either side of an opaque block. Fixed by wrapping all three lines in ONE `#ft8-status` sticky block so the browser does the arithmetic - the same "renderer does the arithmetic" rule the manual diagrams follow, and it removes a hand-maintained offset that could fall out of step with a line's height. **Compounded by #225**: a status string that never cleared parked permanently on top of the readout |
| 224 | **Web TX-tone picker selects a slot 1-2 to the left of the click** (Randy N4OPI) | ✅ **Done + operator-verified on hardware** | — | Arithmetic, not opinion: the draw reserves 34 px for the E/O row letters (`bw = (W-34)/n`) while the click handler mapped the pointer across the FULL width. Error = `f × 52 × (1000/966 − 1)` → **0 at the left edge, 0.9 mid-strip, 1.83 slots at the right**, matching his "off between 1 and 2 slots" precisely. Both callers now go through one `toneGeom()`, and the pointer is converted to INTERNAL canvas pixels first (the canvas is 1000 px wide and CSS-stretched). Keep any future cell maths in that one function |
| 225 | **"Busy: working KA3PMW" / "QSO cancelled" never clear** (Randy N4OPI) | ✅ **Done + hardware-verified**: fired a refusal over the API and watched it expire - present at +3 s and +12 s, gone by +20 s | — | `s_web_reply_result` is the OUTCOME OF THE LAST BROWSER COMMAND but the page renders it as CURRENT STATE, and it was only ever overwritten by the next command - so a refusal from ten minutes ago still described a station already in the log. Every string there answers "what happened when I clicked that", so it now expires with the click: `WEB_RESULT_TTL_MS` 20 s, one `web_result_set()` (printf-checked) that no call site can bypass, and the getter REPORTS expiry rather than clearing the buffer, because it runs on the HTTP task while taskLVGL owns the string. Live conditions already have their own fields in `/api/status` and do not need mirroring in a sentence |
| 226 | **A logged station stays green in the web decode list until the next QSO starts** (Randy N4OPI) | ✅ **Done + hardware-verified through a REAL QSO** (DK6WS, ADIF #43): pinned at 221331, `released DK6WS from the top` at 361039 - **84 ms after the ADIF write**, with no new QSO | — | Two sibling accessors asked DIFFERENT questions: `ft8_qso_get_working_target()` tested the QSO state (so it released at completion) while `ft8_qso_get_pinned_call()` tested `s_target[0]` - which is deliberately NOT cleared at completion, because `final_resend_if_still_asked()` reads it afterwards. The browser's colouring used the second. Both now go through one `qso_state_is_live()` predicate; `s_target` is untouched, so the v1.3.4 completed-QSO final re-send is unaffected. **Do not "fix" this by clearing `s_target`** |
| 227 | **"Who is hearing me" loses older reports — silently** (Randy N4OPI) | ✅ **REPRODUCED AND FIXED ON HARDWARE** - the operator's own device hit it hours later: `hit the 128-report cap` + `128 report(s) ... [PARTIAL]`, i.e. the old build would have shown 64 of them in silence | Needs a report | He saw dozens of spots on pskreporter.info and "no one has heard me in 24 hrs" here, with recent contacts present and older ones gone. TWO stacked truncations, neither logged: `on_data()` dropped everything past `RESP_CAP` (16 KB ≈ 40-55 reports) with a bare `return ESP_OK`, and the parse loop stopped at `PSK_RX_MAX` (64). RESP_CAP → 64 KB, PSK_RX_MAX → 128 (~10 KB PSRAM), both now WARN when they bite, and `psk_rx_is_truncated()` reaches `/api/psk_rx` so the page says "showing N - the collector had more" instead of presenting a cut list as the whole picture. The window (24 h) is now stated by the firmware rather than hardcoded in the prose, answering his "not sure how long the history window is". ⚠ **NOT reproduced**: N4OPI had no reports in the last hour when queried and the bench legitimately has none (it never transmits), so the ORDERING the collector returns - which decides whether truncation ate the old or the new - is still unknown. The fix is right either way, because a silent cut is wrong either way. Note `rronly=1` is load-bearing: without it the response is **1 MB / 4,997 activeReceiver elements** |
| 228 | **Tab5 snap-on keyboard in the Radio Menus screen** (Don N2VGU) | ✅ **DONE + hardware-verified end to end 2026-08-21** (digits, backspace, del, all four arrows, Enter, and unmapped keys correctly refused) | — | "Would it be possible ... to enable use of the Tab 5 hardware keyboard while in this screen? Or better, globally? Or at least the navigation keys". The terminal is the natural home for a real keyboard - it is the one screen that IS a keyboard interface, and the radio's numeric fields are typed. Needed no new plumbing: `qmx_term_view_key()` maps the keyboard bridge's spelled-out tokens onto the SAME `post(CMD_KEY, ...)` the on-screen keys use, so the port is still touched only by the worker, never from taskLVGL. `kbd_text_cb` claims it first, exactly like the frequency keypad (another full-screen view with no textarea). Both `backspace` and `del` send 0x7F, the byte measured to work. **"Globally" deliberately NOT done**: outside modals there is no focus model for a keyboard to drive, so it would need one designed first - answer him yes-to-the-terminal, not-yet-globally. ⚠ **Esc is a dead key IN THE RADIO, not in our mapping** - the capture shows `kbd: "esc" -> esc` firing four times, so the byte went out and the QMX ignored it, consistent with the measured record for that same numeric field where left/right and Enter do nothing either. Mapping Esc to our own `qmx_term_view_close()` (matching what Esc does in every modal in the app) is the obvious improvement and is NOT done - note a bare ctrl-q would be wrong, since closing needs the orderly screen-reading exit walk, not a raw keystroke |

QMX has no XIT (its own CAT manual: "XIT status: always 0"), so this is SPLIT - VFO B held at A+offset, `cw_split_maintain()` in cat.c's poll task so the offset follows every tuning source incl. the radio's own knob. CW/CW-R only; only clears split if WE set it; re-asserts every 30 s. Drawer CW section + web + config export. **FB/FR/FT/SP all exist in 1_03 - no firmware gate needed.** Needs a real CW contact to verify |

Present in 1_03 as well as 1_04. Re-seeds the flat-mode floor, since the noise floor just moved |

The QMX's own menu and its Terminal Applications share this CDC pipe with our 50 ms poll. `cat_user_pause_set()` - a SEPARATE flag from the FT8-TX `s_poll_paused`, which is cleared at the end of every burst. Also stands down the dead-stream and stuck-decode watchdogs, which would otherwise read a deliberate menu visit as a fault and power-cycle USB under the operator's hands. Blue banner + drawer button + web button; resume re-runs the IQ handshake |

New 30 s step re-asserts `Q9 1;` before the 60 s soft reset and the 120 s replug. `iq_mode_handshake()` extracted from link_task so both callers share it |

Stan independently asked for exactly the pause button #91 ships. **Ties Roy's blank-decode-list to the same mechanism**: watchdog reset -> QMX reboots -> `Q9` IQ mode (session state) wiped -> USB link survives -> CAT answers, no audio. Prediction to test: the new 30 s IQ re-assert (#92) should now recover Roy's case automatically. Samuel's *reason* for being in that menu was RF gain - which #90 now removes |

| 109 | ✅ **SOLVED - the BLE mouse scan was flooding the SDIO link it shares with WiFi** (fixed + hw-verified `dfa15e4`) | ✅ Done + hw-verified | — | Operator: the web top-right has flapped for TWO DAYS, on v1.6.0, i.e. this PREDATES Bluetooth. Captured chain on 2026-08-09: five `H_SDIO_DRV: SDIO RX oversize ... draining to recover` in 100973-109566 ms, then `httpd_ws: Failed to send WS header` at 113571/115564/116240, then `ws: Client connected, fd=56` at 116244 - the browser reconnecting, which is what the operator sees. Recovered to 15.7 fps by 121303. **13 recoveries in 134 s.** CLAUDE.md's own note on the v0.20.0 recovery patch says: *"if a longer soak shows recoveries accelerating/clustering, that's drift and warrants a prevention (counter-accounting) fix on top; if they stay rare and isolated, the recovery alone suffices"* - that condition is now MET. Each recovery DISCARDS a frame by design, which is the plausible source of the WS send failures. ⚠ Causal chain is INDICATED, not proven (one sample, 4 s gap). Next: (a) A/B with `bt_mouse_en=0` to see whether BT raises the rate - not the cause, but possibly a multiplier; (b) the counter-accounting prevention fix, noting `host_cnt` was observed WRAPPING mid-burst (978357 -> 10560). Do NOT widen MAX_FAIL_STREAK to paper over it. **⚠ CORRECTION, same evening, on the operator's evidence:** he has been on HOTEL WIFI for three weeks, and **none of ~25 field testers has reported WiFi instability**. That reframes this from "drift, needs a prevention fix" to **load**: the oversize delta is PENDING BYTES AT THE C6, so a congested lossy AP means more retransmits, burstier inbound traffic, larger coalesced deltas, and the 1536-byte RX_NONE buffer exceeded more often. Clustering under load is what CLAUDE.md's candidate **(a) occasional coalesced/oversized frame** predicts, NOT (b) counter drift - and the `host_cnt` wrap I cited as drift evidence is just the modulo counter wrapping normally. So the recovery patch is arguably PASSING a brutal robustness test rather than failing one: 13 recoveries, zero RPC timeouts, WS back to 15.7 fps in ~5 s. **Do not "fix" this from a hotel-network sample.** Reproduce on a normal LAN first; if the rate is low there, the correct outcome is a note in the manual, not a driver change | **⚠ UPDATE 2026-08-10, on the operator's OWN office LAN ('Aillio') - the hotel-network explanation is DEAD.** Same signature, and it now MEETS this file's own escalation criterion: recoveries per minute went 1-3 (isolated) -> **10-13**, with oversize deltas growing 1649 -> **6231** against a 1536-byte buffer, i.e. accelerating AND clustering. **BLE was tested and EXONERATED by measurement**: with the BT mouse scanning flat out (4,400-4,900 dropped ADV reports/min, minutes 38-47) there were **ZERO** SDIO recoveries - because WiFi was down. Recoveries start the minute WiFi reconnects and the spot feeds resume, and the ADV-drop rate then FALLS as recoveries rise, so the drops look like a symptom of a saturated link rather than its cause. I was one step from implementing a BLE scan-whitelist fix for something BLE does not cause - measure first, again. Concurrent load at the time: RBN (persistent TCP) + DX cluster (persistent TCP) + POTA (14 KB HTTPS every ~70 s). Next: controlled run with RBN and the cluster OFF, POTA only. **FIX (hardware-verified 2026-08-10):** `start_scan()` in `bt_hid_mouse.c` was FOREVER + ACTIVE + no whitelist + no duty cycle, so in a BLE-dense office the C6 forwarded every advertiser to the host (~3,000 dropped ADV reports/min) and the pending-byte delta kept overrunning the 1536-byte SDIO RX buffer. Now 20% duty cycle, and while bonded: passive + `BLE_HCI_SCAN_FILT_USE_WL`, alternating 60 s filtered / 15 s open so an RPA-advertising mouse can never be filtered out permanently and pairing a new one still works. **BT on, same conditions: 5.0 recoveries/min -> 0, rpc timeouts 4-8/min -> 0, ADV drops ~3000/min -> ~29/min.** Mouse still reconnects by itself (5.5 s into a FILTERED stretch) and works while connected.

| 113 | **RIT (and the RIT/XIT split of roles)** | 🟢 **Built — tap-to-RIT, bench-measured; the display SIGN still needs a carrier.** Operator picked option 1 (tap-to-RIT) with clear-on-retune. Shipped: `cat_request_rit_hz()`/`cat_get_rit_hz()` (`RC;` ALWAYS first, then one `RU`/`RD` — RU/RD are absolute *or* relative depending on the QMX menu setting "CAT RU and RD", which we cannot read, so never send them bare); clear-on-retune inside `cat_set_frequency()` so every path is covered; the compensation in `ui_get_if_offset_hz()`; a RIT pill at the spectrum's top-right (the top bar has no gap wider than ~45 px — measured, not guessed) that arms the mode and shows the live offset; the tap intercept in `touch_event_cb`'s RELEASED branch, which sets the offset and leaves the dial alone; a dashed magenta marker in the spectrum plus a matching overlay line down the waterfall; and the passband window shifted onto where you are listening. Tap-to-RIT forces a 10 Hz snap regardless of mode — the mode grid is there to land the DIAL tidily, and SSB's 250 Hz / DiGi's 500 Hz would have left five and three usable offsets inside ±500. **Measured on hardware at zoom x8 (4.6875 Hz/px), RIT +300 via a throwaway hook since a tap needs a finger:** gold dial line unmoved at x=262 before AND after (transmit stays put — Bill Carver's hard requirement), marker at x=326 = +64 px = +300.0 Hz on the high side, both passband edges moved the same +64 px, pill reads `RIT +300` in magenta, `cat: RIT -> +300 Hz (ok)` in the log, a retune logged `retune -> clearing RIT (was +300 Hz)` and the marker vanished, and the pill stands down for the open drawer. **⚠ NOT verified, both need a human:** the display sign (the falsifying test is one steady carrier — trace stands still and the marker sits 300 Hz up = right; trace jumps 600 Hz = flip the one operator in `ui_get_if_offset_hz()`; two FT8-window snapshots cannot answer it, the signals change between frames), and the tap gesture itself including the out-of-range clamp toast | Medium | groups.io thread 2026-08-10/11, three operators converging on the same shape. **Roy KI0ER**: while ACTIVATING (running) he engages RIT to pull in a caller sitting just outside his passband - "a Sasquatch tone below my centre frequency or a mosquito above me" - and it must be **at his fingertips during operation, not in a settings drawer**, because it changes per caller and gets turned off again immediately. **Michael KZ4LY** frames the division cleanly: **XIT while hunting, RIT while running** (XIT is #89, shipped as split since the QMX has no XIT). His UI idea: a "tap to RIT" mode while running, where a tap on a zoomed waterfall engages RIT onto that caller and a second tap clears it and returns to the run frequency. **Bill Carver** adds the display requirement, and it is the interesting part: with RIT on, the spectrum must **keep signals where they are** and show a separate RIT marker at the offset, rather than sliding the whole display - the QMX streams IQ centred on the RX frequency, so the fix is to offset where the spectrum is PLOTTED and mark the RIT position, not to recompute anything. He also warns against the obvious mistake: RIT must never move TX ("I do NOT want my TX moving around as other people transmit"). Note the QMX DOES have RIT over CAT (`RT`/`RU`/`RD`, and `RC` clear in 1_04) unlike XIT - check `docs/qmx-1_04-cat-comparison.md` before designing |

The Tab5 therefore draws `bin - mean(bins)` and `sample - median(bins)`, never a per-bin floor, and **the "Adaptive floor (%)" setting does nothing on either screen**. | 117 | **Capture crashes ON-DEVICE — route the panic handler's output into the diag ring before it reboots** | ✅ **DONE + hw-verified end to end (2026-08-20, `4c6b31b`)** | Done | Found 2026-08-14 while chasing two `usb_dwc_hal.c:504` asserts overnight: **no on-device log carries a crash at all.** Verified across four sources — the serial capture held both asserts and both register dumps, while `/api/log/saved` (0.43 MB), SD `qmx-log.txt` (0.52 MB) and SD `qmx-log.1.txt` (5 MB, back to 26 July) held **zero** asserts and **zero** register dumps between them. The cause is that panics print straight to UART through the ROM/panic path and never enter `diag_log`'s vprintf hook, so the assert text, the register dump and the backtrace exist only if a serial capture happened to be running at that second. Everything on-device sees is the NEXT boot's `reset_reason=panic/exception` — which CLAUDE.md already records as ambiguous (a forced power-off and every `idf.py flash` reset read identically), so it cannot even be counted as a crash. **Why this matters:** a field crash is currently undiagnosable. The operator is asked for a diag download, and it contains everything except the one thing needed. Roy's, Don's and Samuel's reports all hit this. **The design that would work:** hook `esp_system` panic output (`esp_rom_install_channel_putc` or a custom `panic_print` sink) so the panic text also lands in the PSRAM ring, then force the flash-persist path to `fsync` before the reboot completes — a panic handler runs with interrupts off and cannot take a mutex, so it must write the ring directly and flush without the normal task. **Two known-hard parts:** the ring's spinlock must not be taken from panic context, and `diag_log_persist`'s 30 s task will not run again, so the flush has to be synchronous inside the handler. ⚠ Note the SD mirror is ALSO parked whenever WiFi is on (`park_snapshot()`, one boot snapshot then stop — deliberate, the SDMMC/WiFi contention is documented), so on a WiFi-connected unit the SD path adds nothing here even once this is fixed; the flash log is the one to target. **DONE 2026-08-20 — but NOT by the design sketched above, deliberately.** Flushing to flash from the handler is the dangerous route: a panic handler runs with interrupts off, the other core halted and possibly no cache, so a SPIFFS write there can **hang instead of rebooting**, turning a diagnosable crash into a dead device — strictly worse than the bug. Instead `util/panic_hook.c` wraps `esp_panic_handler` (`-Wl,--wrap=`) and only memcpy's a compact record into **RTC no-init RAM**, which survives a warm reset — and every panic reset is warm. The NEXT boot logs it through the ordinary path from `app_main`, so it reaches the ring, `/api/log` **and** `/spiffs` via the normal persist task, with **no flash access from panic context at all**. Record carries: reason, the assert/abort text, **task name**, uptime at death, MEPC/MTVAL/MCAUSE/RA/SP, and a ready-made `addr2line` line. **Bonus:** a valid magic is POSITIVE proof the reset was a real crash, which `reset_reason` can never be here — `panic/exception` with no record means a forced power-off or a flash. **VERIFIED ON HARDWARE, whole chain:** the wrap was confirmed in the **disassembly** (`jal <__wrap_esp_panic_handler>` from IDF, `j <esp_panic_handler>` back out) rather than trusted, because a `--wrap` that fails to resolve does nothing *silently* — the #189 trap; new dev action `{"action":"panic_test"}` crashes on demand so the mechanism is **falsifiable** rather than merely uncontradicted; triggering it produced `task httpd, core 0, after 18.334 s, abort() was called at PC 0x4806acfd`, and that PC decodes to **webserver.c:848**, the exact `abort()` line — so the captured data is *useful*, not just present; confirmed in both `/api/log` and `/api/log/saved`. ⚠ **Deliberate limitation:** RTC RAM does not survive a full power cycle, so a crash then a battery pull is still lost — the serial capture stays the record for that and for anything that hangs without panicking. ⚠ `panic_test` is **radio-OFF only** (a panic reboot is a warm reset, i.e. the #74 trigger) |
| 118 | **Phantom CW: a mirror copy of a real signal** (Roy KI0ER, Samuel W7STF) | 🟠 **REPRODUCIBLE AT WILL 2026-08-15 — and the frame-misalign fix is probably NOT the cause** | Small | ⭐ **Roy can now cause it on demand, and the recipe points somewhere else entirely.** He changed **CW Sidetone volume from the QMX's own front-panel menu** mid-session and the phantom appeared immediately, complete with the passband haze; selecting "Let me use the QMX menus" and then "Done" cleared it, with **no restart of either box**. His 5 MB log (`KI0ER 2026-08-15`) shows the menu visit leaves **no trace at all** while `audio: RX ~47000 pairs/s` continues to the last line - so the stream is alive and merely not I/Q. **Hypothesis, matching every observation: the front-panel menu visit drops `Q9` (IQ mode is explicitly session state), the audio folds both sidebands, and every signal grows a mirror twin to the left.** Resume-from-pause re-runs the IQ handshake, which is exactly why Roy's cure worked. The dead-stream watchdog can never catch this because the stream is not dead. **Falsifying test (set up, awaiting the operator's front panel): read `Q9;` before and after the menu change - `Q90;` confirms it.** If confirmed the fix is a periodic `Q9;` check + re-assert, which also closes Roy's earlier blank-decode report. ⚠ The committed frame-misalign fix `539b769` stays (it is a real defect), but `FRAME-MISALIGN` has still **never** fired, so it should no longer be presented as the cause of the phantom. Prior reasoning: | `process_rx()` in `audio/audio.c` did `pairs = bytes_read / 6` and **discarded the remainder, with no re-sync anywhere in the path**. One read ending mid-frame shifts every later sample by 1-5 bytes for the whole UAC session; a 3-byte shift swaps I and Q outright. Image rejection collapses, so a real signal grows a mirror twin. The state is in OUR buffers and the UAC session survives a QMX power cycle (v0.15.5 note), so only a Tab5 reboot clears it - **which is exactly what Roy measured: restarting the radio changed nothing, restarting the Tab5 fixed it.** Two unguarded sources of a partial frame: `_ring_buffer_pop()` returns whatever it holds at timeout with no frame alignment, and the driver pushes `actual_num_bytes` per ISO packet, which its own comment says may be less than requested (CLAUDE.md already records truncated UAC chunks as real here). Roy's report that it began when he enabled the BLE mouse fits load as the trigger - unproven. ⚠ **The remainder is now carried, and every occurrence is counted and printed on the existing 1 Hz line as `FRAME-MISALIGN=n ev / n B`. THAT COUNTER IS THE WHOLE EXPERIMENT** - if it stays 0 with the radio streaming, partial frames are not happening and the phantoms have another cause. Do not report this as fixed until a log says otherwise |

Computed from the shipped 31-tap Hamming design: edge attenuation **-16.6 / -10.3 / -8.0 dB** at zoom x2 / x4 / x8, with the rolled-off width **1846 / 1237 / 928 Hz per side**. Samuel estimated "about 1000 Hz" at x4 and was reading it correctly. Behaviour dates from when zoom shipped (v0.16.0) - nothing regressed. Fix is a **longer FIR** (31 taps is short). ⛔ **Do NOT raise the cutoff toward 0.5/D instead** - that trades the dark edge for aliased FALSE SIGNALS, which is worse. Told Samuel it is queued |

| 121 | **CQ preset with a doubled space transmitted a DIFFERENT message** (Don WB0LQW) | ✅ Fixed `8d8b5c4` + flashed; on-device wiring NOT exercised | — | His preset was stored as `CQ  POTA WB0LQW` (two spaces). Whitespace is syntax in a 77-bit message: the encoder stopped seeing a CQ and encoded a signal report to a hashed callsign, so the radio keyed for the full 12.6 s at 3.2 W and receivers saw `CQ  <...> +00`. With a grid appended it returned rc=5 and never keyed - the "identity modal appeared" symptom. All three reproduced from that one character by `test/ft8_cq_encode_harness.c`. Fixed in two layers: `ft8_msg_normalize()` collapses interior runs (both call sites plus the editor's display path, so a bad preset repairs itself visibly), and `ft8_msg_roundtrip_ok()` decodes the payload back and refuses to key if the meaning changed. ⛔ **Never tighten that guard to a byte comparison** - the protocol legitimately drops tokens it has no room for (`CQ POTA PJ4/K1ABC` goes out as `CQ PJ4/K1ABC`); the invariant is that a CQ stays a CQ and the callsign survives. Next: open the CQ editor on hardware and confirm a doubled-space preset now shows one space |

| 201 | 🔴 **Auto-answer picks an OCCUPIED offset even with fresh EVEN+ODD maps showing green slots** (Roy KI0ER, v1.8.7 / QMX 1.04.007, TX Hold unchecked) | 🟢 **FIXED `667ee11` — TX1 re-picks its tone; NOT yet seen firing on air** | Medium | He sees `⚠ FREQ BUSY` during auto-answer while both parity strips are freshly mapped with several green slots. Sometimes it DOES relocate automatically, which is the interesting half - `relocate_cq_tone_if_clashing()` exists for the CQ path. Check whether the auto-answer/pounce path calls `ft8_find_clear_tone_hz_near()` at all, or inherits a tone chosen before the map existed. ⚠ Do NOT fix from the description - get his logs first ⭐ **ROOT-CAUSED 2026-08-20 (not yet fixed).** The warning is CORRECT and the *picker* is what fails him. `ft8_tx_is_clashing()` is sound: it does filter by parity, and `use_parity` is always true for a REPLY, so `parity_known` holds and opposite-window stations are properly skipped. The gap is that **`relocate_cq_tone_if_clashing()` is CQ-ONLY** — its own comment says *"never mid-exchange, where the partner is tracking our specific tone"*. So on a pounce the tone is chosen once at arm time via `ft8_find_clear_tone_hz()`, and if the band fills in the ~15 s before the burst **nothing moves it**; it only raises `FREQ BUSY`. That is exactly Roy's "chooses occupied frequency with TX HOLD unchecked". **The design reasoning is right mid-QSO and WRONG AT TX1:** on the first transmission of a pounce nobody is tracking our tone yet, so relocating is safe there and only becomes unsafe once the partner has heard us. **Proposed fix (deliberately NOT done at release time, needs a soak):** allow relocation while the exchange has not yet had an outgoing transmission — i.e. TX1 only — and keep the existing lock from the moment the partner could be tracking us. Honour `ft8_tx_get_tone_hold()` exactly as the CQ path does. ⚠ Also note his screenshot is **v1.8.7**, so it predates both the `FREQ BUSY` log line and the #202 gate fix **FIXED 2026-08-20 (`667ee11`).** `relocate_cq_tone_if_clashing()` became `relocate_tone_if_clashing(bool is_cq)` and a pounce now calls it from the WAIT_RPT no-progress path, gated on a new `s_tx_sent` — false when an exchange begins (`ft8_qso_start` and `ft8_qso_start_cq`), true from the first completed burst. So the tone can still be re-picked right up until we key, and is locked from then on because the partner may be tracking it. Kept deliberately: `ft8_tx_get_tone_hold()` still wins; a burst already `FT8_TX_ACTIVE` is never moved (disarm/re-arm cannot recall a keyed transmission); and `s_cq_saved` is only rewritten for a real CQ, or a later resume-CQ would return on a tone picked for someone else's exchange. Logs `TX1 tone %d Hz is busy - moving to %d Hz`. ⚠ **NOT verified on air** — it needs a real pounce onto a tone that fills up between arming and keying. Shipping after v1.8.8, so it is in the next release |
| 202 | 🔴 **Auto-answer waits ~30 s instead of replying in the very next slot** (Roy KI0ER) | ✅ **FIXED `aa5b6e3`, flashed** — our own TX blinded the occupancy gate | Medium | With filters POTA-only + CQ-only, a station heard on EVEN is not answered in the next ODD window; it waits a full extra cycle, by which time a POTA activator has moved on. **Not every time** - he has seen prompt replies too. Suspects to check: `FT8_REPLY_TX_WINDOW_MS` (2800) and the hold-for-decode gate, `ft8_robot_occupancy_ready()` (#142 requires BOTH parities heard within 4 slot periods before the first TX - a lost map would hold exactly like this), and the early-decode toggle |
| 203 | 🔴🔴 **ROOT-CAUSED: a ~2-minute AUDIO RING OVERFLOW starved FT8 completely** (Roy KI0ER) | ✅ **FIXED as standing patch #9 `pending`** — the httpd spin now closes the socket | Medium-Large | No TX, no active QSO, no operator action. ODD went grey, then EVEN, then after 2 more cycles mapping resumed. ⭐ **HYPOTHESIS, NOT A CONCLUSION: 201, 202 and 203 may be one fault.** An occupancy map that intermittently empties would explain all three - a blank map means no clear slot can be chosen (201), and `ft8_robot_occupancy_ready()` holding until both parities are mapped again is exactly a one-cycle delay (202). `build_tone_occupancy()` filters by slot PARITY and needs `ft8_screen` rows; note the **`ft8_screen: record_decode: mutex timeout, dropping ...`** seen on the bench the same day, which DROPS decodes and would thin the map. Test the hypothesis against his logs before touching code . **✅ TESTED AGAINST HIS LOG - and the hypothesis was HALF right.** 201/202 are one fault (the occupancy gate blinded by our own TX, fixed `aa5b6e3`); 203 is a SEPARATE and more serious one. **The strips were telling the truth: there was no data.** Decodes run 96-198/min for 26 minutes, then **minutes 27 and 28 are entirely empty**, then min 29 recovers - matching his "after 2 more all-grey cycles the mapping resumed" exactly. The decode list ages at `FT8_ROW_STALE_SEC` (60 s), so a 2-minute gap empties the occupancy map and both strips go grey. **Cause: the audio ring overflowed.** `W audio: RX 49081 pairs/s ... DROPPED=49936 (ring full)` - the QMX kept delivering ~49k pairs/s throughout while essentially every sample was thrown away, because nothing was draining the ring. `dsp: FT8 cap: ... active=0` confirms capture was not running. **Slot completions pin it:** slot 102 at 1584921, slot 103 at 1608743 (24 s, already stretched from the normal ~15), then **slot 104 at 1725908 - 117 SECONDS later**. `fft_task`, the ring's sole consumer, stopped for two minutes. **Bounded, not chronic:** 125 `DROPPED` lines in the whole session, and they are all in minutes 26-28 (20/59/45) plus one benign line at startup. ⚠ **WHY fft_task stalled is NOT established.** Correlates in the window, none of them proven causal: internal heap sliding 33 -> 27 -> **22 KB with `lblk=12KB LOW!`**; dense HTTPS feed activity (`esp-x509-crt-bundle: Certificate validated` several times, a 33 KB POTA fetch, a PSK Reporter send, update_check); a WS client connected; and two SDIO oversize recoveries. ⭐ **A candidate worth testing first:** device TLS runs on SOFTWARE crypto because hardware AES cannot get DMA descriptors (see the TLS/DMA note in CLAUDE.md), so concurrent HTTPS handshakes are unusually expensive on a board where `fft_task` is priority 4 on core 1. The CW-audio task at priority 6 caused exactly this class of starvation before. **This is the #51 signature - audio lost, decodes collapse - arriving through ring overflow rather than the USB wire, on a released build with a user watching.** Reproduce by driving the feeds hard during FT8 and watching for `DROPPED=`  ⭐⭐ **CAUSE FOUND 2026-08-19 (late): the httpd worker BUSY-LOOPS on a dead socket and starves the audio consumer.** `W httpd_txrx: httpd_sock_err: error in recv : 128` (ENOTCONN) repeating every ~6 ms - **19,773 of them at a steady 158/s spanning 125.7 s**, from 1599.4 s to 1725.1 s. The FT8 blackout is 1608->1725 s. **The two windows match almost to the second**, and the first sustained `DROPPED=` line falls INSIDE the storm. So the chain is: a stale socket -> httpd `recv` spins flat out -> `fft_task` (pri 4) is denied the core -> the audio ring fills -> every sample dropped -> no decodes -> the 60 s decode list ages out -> both occupancy strips go grey. Every symptom Roy reported from one root cause. ⚠ **Two hypotheses were TESTED AND FALSIFIED first**: feed tasks preempting `fft_task` (they are priority 2-3, BELOW it) and `dsp_set_transfer_quiet()` (which does stop FT8 exactly this way - but no upload ran; the only upload lines in his log are at boot). **Mechanism:** a half-closed socket stays readable forever, so select wakes, recv returns ENOTCONN, and nothing closes the fd. IDF maps ENOTCONN to `HTTPD_SOCK_ERR_FAIL` via `httpd_sock_err()`'s `default:` - which SHOULD end the session, so why it does not is the next thing to establish. **NOT FIXED** - it wants either a 9th standing patch (close the session after N consecutive FAILs) or a socket watchdog of our own, and that is not a midnight change. ⭐ **This is almost certainly #59 as well** ("web server wedged after ~35 min, WiFi up, HTTP dead", open since July, never investigated) and the unresponsive httpd I hit on the bench the same afternoon, which I had recorded as probably self-inflicted. Related to #193 but NOT covered by it: that fix stopped us CORRUPTING the stream; nothing stops the worker spinning once a socket dies underneath it  **FIX: standing patch #9, `tools/patches/apply_httpd_ws_dead_socket_close.ps1`.** `httpd_ws_get_frame_type()` cannot read the header byte from a half-closed socket and its own comment says *"closing socket now"* - but it returned **ESP_OK**, so `httpd_req_new()` marked `sd->ws_close` and `httpd_sess_process()` succeeded, and `httpd_main.c` never reached its `httpd_sess_delete()`. One changed return value (ESP_FAIL) turns the endless spin into the close it always claimed to be doing. ⚠ **Why nothing of ours could rescue it:** the httpd worker is priority **5**, above `fft_task` (4), `ws_push` (3) and the feeds (2) - everything below it on that core was starved, INCLUDING the push task that would have closed the socket. In Roy's log the dead fd was only freed 125 s later when a new browser connected and our takeover logic closed the stale one. Registered in `check_patches.py` (mutation-tested: removing the marker fails the build). ⚠ NOT yet observed fixing it on hardware - needs a browser that vanishes mid-stream. |

| 214 | 🔴 **Mode change CW -> LSB left the BW label at 50 Hz** (Samuel W7STF, v1.8.7 / QMX 1.04.007) | ✅ **FIXED + hw-verified 2026-08-20 (`8bdc4dc`)** | Done | On 40m watching a CW signal, switched to LSB, and the bandwidth stayed showing **50 Hz** - a CW filter width, in an SSB mode. Suspect the `FW;` suppression: `cat.c` drops `FW;` from the poll rotation while `s_ssb_bw_pinned` is set and the mode is USB/LSB, and CLAUDE.md claims polling resumes "in CW and on mode change". If the pin survives the CW->LSB transition, nothing ever refreshes the label and it keeps the last CW value. **CAUSE:** `s_ssb_bw_pinned` is set in exactly ONE place and **never cleared anywhere**. The poll drops `FW;` whenever a width is pinned AND the mode is USB/LSB, because reading the filter back makes the QMX revert it. In CW `in_ssb` is false so the suppression is off and the label tracks the CW passband; switch CW -> LSB and it turns straight back on from a pin left earlier in the session, and **no code path repaints the label**. So CLAUDE.md's "`FW;` resumes in CW and on mode change" was only half right - it resumes in CW, but coming BACK into SSB it is suppressed again immediately. **FIX:** repaint from the pin on the CW->SSB transition. The pin is the correct value to show - setting it wrote BOTH `MMSSB|Filter RX` (committed) and `MMSSB|Bandwidth` (live), so it IS the radio's SSB filter. Deliberately NOT fixed by clearing the pin and re-reading: a bare `FW;` there would make the radio revert the live filter, which is the whole reason the pin exists. **HARDWARE-VERIFIED**, and the log discriminates the two possible sources: LSB pinned 2700 -> CW shows **300** after a real `RX<- FW0300;` -> back to LSB shows **2700** with **no `FW;` response at all**, the update landing in the SAME millisecond as `Mode = LSB`. That proves the repaint fired and that `FW;` is still correctly suppressed in SSB. ⚠ Minor: `ui_update_passband_width()` always logs the suffix `(CAT FW)`, including on this repaint where the value did NOT come from a CAT response - harmless but mildly misleading in a future diagnosis |
| 215 | **QMX Diagnostics screen: colour and button animation not reproduced** (Samuel W7STF) | 🟡 **Made MEASURABLE `b78d54c`; the fix needs one reading from a radio** | Blocked on a measurement | The Radio Menus colourisation is "vastly improved", but the **Diagnostics** screen is not faithful on either UI: the QMX+ uses **red**, and the pressed-button feedback (the words `Press` or `<<< >>>` in **green**) does not animate. `util/ansi_term.c` models reverse video (load-bearing - it is the only marker of the selected menu item) **Checked end to end first, and the obvious diagnosis is WRONG — colour is not missing.** `ansi_term.c` has carried a per-cell `fg` since the first version, `/api/term` already emits `col` runs, and **`ef1ada6` ("Radio Menus show the radio's colours on both screens") shipped IN v1.8.7**, so both renderers paint it. **The real gap is narrower:** `apply_sgr()` implements only `0`, `7`, `27` and `30-37`. Every byte ever captured from the **menu** screens uses only 0/7/27/33/37 — all handled — so the parser's own comment (*"the radio does not send it, and guessing at unmeasured behaviour is how a parser grows bugs nobody can reproduce"*) was correct at the time. Sam's report is evidence the **Diagnostics** screen sends more, and a silently discarded parameter left no way to learn what. **DONE:** unhandled SGR parameters are now recorded (distinct codes, capped at `ANSI_UNK_MAX`, plus a total count) and reported by `/api/term` as `unk`/`unk_n` — the same anti-unfalsifiability move as the #189 USB counters. Harness extended and **mutation-tested** (never-dedup → 2 failures, no-cap → 2, record-a-handled-colour → 5, telemetry-eats-the-text → 5, clean → 0). **DELIBERATELY NOT GUESSED:** bright `90-97`, bold `1` and backgrounds `40-47` are the likely candidates and are still unimplemented, because "likely" is exactly what this project says not to ship. ⛔ **BLOCKED ON ONE READING:** the bench QMX has a single serial port configured, so `qmx_term` refuses to open. **To finish this:** on a radio with `System config > GPS & Ser. ports > USB serial ports = 2` (then power-cycle), open Radio Menus → **Diagnostics**, then `GET /api/term` and report the `unk` array. Those numbers are the fix. Sam's second symptom (the Press / `<<< >>>` labels not animating) is probably the same thing — they are described as *green lettering*, i.e. a colour we drop — but that also wants confirming from the same reading |
| 216 | **Web UI: the out-of-band tuning slider is missing** (Samuel W7STF) | ✅ **DONE + browser-verified 2026-08-20 (`17db931`)** | Done | Out of band the Tab5's band strip becomes a centre-detented coarse tuner (v1.8.3, knob springs back on release, full deflection = half the visible span). The web UI simply has no control there. Straight Tab5/web parity gap. **CAUSE:** `bandplan` is null exactly when the VFO is outside a band, and the web treated that as "hide the row" rather than as the OOB signal it is — so the one place you most want a way back to a band had no control at all. **FIX:** mirrors `ui.c`'s OOB branch rather than approximating it — knob mid-strip with `‹ ›` on the same 140/1280 proportion, **full deflection = half the visible span** (`48000 / zoom`) so consecutive drags overlap instead of leaving a gap, deflection measured **from where the pointer landed** (measuring from centre would deflect the control the instant the strip was pressed anywhere else), whole-kHz snap clamped 1–60 MHz, springs back on release and on `pointercancel`, and a plain **tap does nothing** because a tapped x has no frequency to mean without a band plan. **BROWSER-VERIFIED against the running device:** strip visible with `lastBandplan` null; colours match `ui.c` exactly (`53,59,66` = `BANDPLAN_OOB_COLOR`, knob `#4a525b`, border `#7d8894`), knob centred; at zoom 2 from 13.500 — half deflection → **13.506.000** (+6 kHz, quarter span), full → **13.512.000** (+12 kHz, half span), past the edge **clamps**, knob detents to 0; the drag committed and the radio read back **13512000**; a plain tap changed nothing. **In-band regression checked:** bandplan present, zero OOB pixels, tap at 75 % of 14.000–14.350 sent `set_freq 14263000` |
| 217 | **Web UI: residual PSD stalls, 3-6 s, after a point-and-shoot** (Samuel W7STF) | 🟢 **A REAL cause found + fixed `56ff56b`; whether it is HIS cause needs his reading** | Fixed, needs his confirmation | He confirms the common stall is "greatly improved" after the partial-write fix, but reports occasional **3-6 s** delays in PSD updating, sometimes following a point-n-shoot gesture, and is careful to say it may be his PC. **Do not assume it is the same fault as #193** - that one was a corrupt WS frame causing a reconnect, and its counter (`partial writes healed`) plus the teardown count are both directly observable now, so the first step is to ask whether they move during one of his stalls. ⭐ **A REAL CAUSE FOUND 2026-08-20, and it is not his PC: the TAKEOVER notice was a malformed websocket frame.** `ws_send_binary()` always used the `126` + 16-bit length form under the comment *"126 is valid for anything <= 65535"* — wrong: **RFC 6455 §5.2 requires the MINIMAL encoding**, 7-bit for 0–125. Spectrum frames are `2 + DSP_FFT_SIZE` so they were always legal; **exactly one frame was affected — the 1-byte takeover notice**. That notice is what lets a displaced browser stand down instead of retrying, and the comment where it is sent documents the alternative (340 takeovers in one session). Because it was illegal, every browser failed the connection instead of reading it, reconnected, and took the slot back — **the cure was re-creating the disease**. **MEASURED both sides with two tabs open:** before **19 sessions / 16 takeovers in ~10 s** (1.6/s); after **4 sessions / 2 takeovers in 25 s** (0.08/s, ~20× fewer), and the displaced tab now shows *"another browser took the live view - click to take it back"* with its socket CLOSED (readyState 3), which it never did before. So anyone leaving a second browser or a phone pointed at the device got a continuous takeover storm, each cycle costing a reconnect — which is what a 3–6 s stall looks like. **Also shipped, and how it was found:** WS health counters (`sessions`/`takeovers`/`closes`/`partial`) now in `/api/status`. Every number that would settle "device or my PC" previously lived only in a periodic log line. ⚠ **Still to confirm:** whether Sam had a second client open. Ask him to watch `/api/status` → `ws` during a stall — `takeovers`/`closes` moving means the device dropped him, and all four staying still means it is the network or the browser |
| 218 | **One-tap firmware update from the device (NOT automatic OTA)** | ✅ **SHIPPED in v1.8.9** — flasher + raw .bin assets both published; OTA URL verified HTTP 200 | Medium + a soak | Detection already exists: `net/update_check.c` polls GitHub Releases with a `tab5.lav.dk/latest.json` fallback and raises the banner in the Reader. **Only download-and-apply is missing.** **Space is not the obstacle.** Flash is **16 MB** and the partition table maps only **9 MB** — `factory` 8 MB at `0x010000` (binary 3.25 MB), `user_nvs` at `0x810000`, `storage` (SPIFFS) at `0x820000`, and **7 MB unused above `0x920000`**. OTA slots fit there without moving `user_nvs`/`storage`, which is essential: those hold the QSO log, the LoTW certificate **and private key**, and the flasher deliberately never touches them. Note the flasher DOES write the partition table at `0x8000`, so a layout change propagates on the next normal flash. Keeping `factory` as a permanent known-good image gives a fallback a two-slot design would not. ⛔ **AUTOMATIC IS REJECTED, and this is the whole point of the row.** An OTA reboot is a **Tab5 warm reset with the radio attached — the documented #74 trigger** — so an unattended update would wedge the QMX of an operator who was not watching, and they would report it as a radio fault (that inversion has already happened twice). This device also keys a transmitter: rebooting mid-burst or mid-QSO is not acceptable, and **#146** (a shutdown mid-burst can leave the QMX keyed) is still open. ⚠ **The download is the risky part ON THIS BOARD**, not the flash write: a 3.25 MB HTTPS transfer would be by far the largest network operation the device ever performs, landing on the weakest subsystem — esp_hosted over SDIO to the C6 (three standing patches, a documented wedge history), **TLS on software crypto** because hardware AES cannot get DMA descriptors, and `MALLOC_CAP_DMA` measured at ~22 KB free. **Proposed shape:** add "Install now" to the banner that already exists; write to an OTA slot with `factory` kept as permanent fallback and IDF app-rollback enabled; verify the image before switching the boot slot; **refuse while TX is armed or active, or a QSO is in progress**; and on success say plainly *"Updated — power-cycle your QMX"* because of #74. Same class of decision as **#192** (diag log partition): it changes the flash layout on shipped units, so it is the operator's call, not something to slip into a release **DONE 2026-08-20, both halves.** *Awareness* (`375ae0e`): the update check had run since v1.1 and announced itself ONLY inside the Reader — `update_check_available()` had **zero callers** — so it now marks the always-visible bottom-bar version label amber (`v1.8.8 -> v1.9.0`) and says the same in the browser footer, from a new `update` block in `/api/status`. Never a popup. *Install* (`55868d4`): three 4 MB app slots, `user_nvs`/`storage` at unchanged offsets, `factory` still the flasher's target and a permanent known-good fallback. Refuses while transmitting or mid-QSO; install and restart are separate so the operator picks when the radio is interrupted; source policy reuses `net_guard` (https anywhere, plain http only to a **numeric** address on our own subnet). **Hardware-verified from a LAN server**, deliberately publishing nothing to GitHub for the test: guard refuses a hostname and an off-subnet IP, allows our subnet, download runs to 100%, state `done`, reboot shows `Loaded app from partition at offset 0x410000` (ota_0) and `new firmware confirmed good ... rollback cancelled`, with settings, WiFi credentials and the ADIF log intact across BOTH the partition-table change and the update. **Two real faults found by testing:** a PSRAM stack on the OTA task panicked at 96% (writing flash disables the cache; `psram_task.h` now forbids PSRAM stacks for anything touching flash) — diagnosed straight from the #117 crash record; and GitHub's `/releases/download/` 302 needed `max_redirection_count`. ⚠ **The screen flickers cyan while writing** — cache-disabled bursts delay the MIPI-DSI frame ISR. Inherent, stated in the confirm dialog, and one more reason it must never run unattended. ⛔ **BEFORE IT CAN BE USED:** every release must publish `qmx_panadapter.bin` as an asset (the browser builds that URL; no release carries one yet), and existing units need **one more cable flash** to receive the new partition table **OPERATOR-TESTED 2026-08-20 with a finger on the glass**, which is the half I could not do: short tap showed `hold to confirm`, long press showed `release to confirm`, release restarted the device, and it booted from **ota_0** and confirmed itself good (`rollback cancelled`). Log trail: `update line long-press confirmed (x=424)` -> `operator confirmed restart` -> `Loaded app from partition at offset 0x410000`. Zero crashes. His verdict: *"I like it - good implementation! And the Cyan screen was gone! And I tried both short and long tap - super!"* ⚠ **ONE STATE STILL UNPROVEN END TO END: the `tap?` OFFER.** It requires `update_check_available()` to be true, i.e. a release NEWER than the running build, which cannot exist while the bench runs the newest. Its *rendering* is proven (all four states injected in a browser and read back), but "the check finds a newer version and builds the download URL from it" has only ever been exercised by calling `ota_install` directly. To demonstrate it: build with a temporary local `v1.8.7` tag, and the offer appears for real. ⛔ **STILL OWED BEFORE ANY USER CAN USE THIS:** every release must publish **`qmx_panadapter.bin`** as an asset (the URL is built from the tag and no release carries one), and existing units need **one cable flash** for the new partition table. Until the asset exists a real tap would end at `Failed - tap retries` |
| 219 | **Mid-QSO tone relocation: my stated reason for forbidding it was FALSE** (Roy KI0ER + Gyula HA3HZ, 2026-08-20) | ✅ **SHIPPED in v1.8.9** — nearest-slot only; not yet confirmed on air | Small-Medium | #201 allows a pounce to re-pick its tone only before TX1, justified by "once the partner has heard us they are tracking our specific tone". **Roy KI0ER: that is not true** — *"The other station's software is not and should not be tracking my offset tone. It does not matter where in the waterfall I show up; their software is decoding and matching the TEXT of my message to their callsign."* He is right: FT8 decoders work the whole passband and match on callsign, so each message in a QSO may ride a different tone, and operators move deliberately in WSJT-X to escape QRM or QSB mid-QSO. The claim came from a pre-existing comment which I repeated and endorsed; both comments in `ft8_qso.c` are now corrected. ⚠ **But "move freely" is also wrong, from Gyula HA3HZ in the same thread:** a partner who has NARROWED their receive bandwidth (MSHV does this so decode finishes inside the reply window) may not hear a station that jumped far. His conclusion: *"it is not possible to say clearly that we should change our transmit frequency during a QSO."* **Proposed synthesis (not implemented):** allow relocation mid-QSO but only to the NEAREST clear slot — which is already what `ft8_find_clear_tone_hz_near()` does — so we escape a clash while staying inside a narrowed window. Bound it (do not move every slot), keep honouring TX hold, and keep the one rule both agree on: **never change tone during a burst**. Needs the operator's call because it changes on-air behaviour **DONE 2026-08-20.** Moving mid-QSO is allowed now, but only to the **nearest** clear slot: capped at `QSO_MIDQSO_MOVE_MAX_HZ` **250 Hz** (five 50 Hz slots) and at `QSO_MIDQSO_MOVE_LIMIT` **3** moves per exchange. If the nearest clear slot is further than the cap we stay put and keep showing `FREQ BUSY` — **being heard on a busy frequency beats being unheard on a clear one**, which is Gyula's point. Neither limit applies before the first burst or to a CQ, because nobody is listening for us yet. Now runs from all three stuck states (`WAIT_RPT`, `WAIT_ROGER`, `WAIT_RR73`), which are the no-progress paths — so it fires exactly when we are being stepped on and getting nowhere, i.e. when Roy says he moves by hand. Never during a burst; TX hold still wins. Each refusal logs its reason (too far / already moved N times) so the bounds show up in a diagnostic download instead of being silent. ⚠ **NOT verified on air** — needs a real QSO with someone landing on our tone mid-exchange. The two macros are the knobs if field reports disagree |
| 220 | **The flasher download is 44 MB when it should be 2.9 MB** (Gyula HA3HZ, 2026-08-20) | ✅ **SHIPPED in v1.8.9** — flasher asset 2.9 MB, packer scripted | Small | Gyula: *"The flasher has become larger because it contains the previous ones compressed."* **Exactly right, and it is a release-process bug of mine:** the v1.8.8 asset was built with `Compress-Archive -Path "tools\QMX-Panadapter flasher\*"`, and that directory accumulates every historical flasher zip — **27 of them, 40 MB**. | 222 | **Spur suppression PARKED — it only ever worked at zoom ×1** (operator, 2026-08-20) | 🟡 **Parked, and DOCUMENTED as withdrawn in v1.8.9** | Medium to revive | **Root cause, read from the code not guessed:** `dsp_get_zoom_spectrum()` returns the zoom-FFT buffer whenever `s_zoom_decim > 1`, and both `ui.c` and `render_waterfall.c` prefer it. That buffer is built straight from magnitude to dB and **never calls `spur_map_apply()`** — so at ×2 and above, on and off are identical. Anyone inspecting spurs zooms in, which is exactly where it stopped working. That is why almost nobody reported on it (only Samuel W7STF) and why the operator could see no difference at all. **Severity is also lower than the bench work implied.** Measured on the operator's own antenna: strongest tooth **22.7 dB** over the floor, against **38.6 dB** with the BNC open in August. Same 8015.6 Hz comb, 33 bins still detected — the spurs are unchanged, band noise simply came up ~16 dB and buried the weaker ones. The original characterisation was done with no antenna, which flattered it. **PARKED, not removed:** the setting, the config export, `/api/settings`, `dsp/spur_map.c` and the teal marks all stay; only the drawer row is gone, because a control that cannot change what you are looking at is worse than a missing one. Same precedent as the parked *Adaptive floor* slider. Waterfall section reflowed 404 → 300 (height and `y +=` together — the documented trap); **operator confirmed the row is gone from the drawer**. **TO REVIVE:** apply the map to the zoom spectrum as well. ⚠ Entries are **BASE-FFT bin indices** and the zoom FFT is finer, so one base bin spans several zoom bins — a straight index translation would notch a sliver and leave the spur standing. Translate via each entry's Hz offset to a RANGE of zoom bins and re-derive the interpolation references there. Then **measure it with an antenna**, not an open BNC |

| 200 | **Option to tap a caller rather than auto-answer while running CQ** (Eric K3FNB, GitHub #11) | ✅ **Shipped in v1.8.7 as "Pick callers myself", reply posted.** Sim-verified both ways; NOT used on the air | Small | *"When I am activating a park, I sometimes like to be a bit more engaged ... could you have an option where I have to tap on a caller/hunter in order to initiate the exchange? I don't mind the firmware automating the rest."* Gates ONLY the choice of who to work: `cqrun_answer()` is skipped and the caller waits in the pile-up until tapped, after which the exchange runs itself. Keeps calling CQ while the operator decides (an activator wants the pile-up building, and going silent reads as a fault), and overrides `auto_pileup` since both decide who is next. Appended to `ft8_filters_t` so old NVS blobs read 0 = off. **A/B verified in sim on the same rig**: with it on, `CQ: manual pick is on - K9ZZ is waiting in the pile-up`; with it off, `cqrun_answer: JA1XYZ`. ⚠ The pair is what makes it meaningful - my first run looked like a pass but the CQ had simply hit its 3-call auto-stop before any phantom answered, and I nearly recorded that silence as the feature working |
| 199 | 🔴 **The FT8 monitor pool can be built TWICE with no teardown, and the second build double-frees the shared FFT scratch** | 🟢 **ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED `3f348fc`; NOT yet reproduced-and-disproved on hardware** | Fixed, needs radio | Serial-captured 2026-08-19 while switching into FT8 on v1.8.6-27-g87a0098, and it is almost certainly the **"one unreproduced `tlsf_free` double-free reboot"** that has been open since v1.3.0 - it now has a backtrace and a mechanism. `ft8_task -> build_monitor_pool (ft8_test.c:535) -> free()`, `assert failed: tlsf_free ... block already marked as free`. **Mechanism:** all monitors share ONE FFT scratch buffer. The build loop freed `s_mon_pool[i]->fft_work` unconditionally before repointing it, so on a rebuild that skipped the teardown every monitor still pointed at the OLD shared buffer and the same block was freed `FT8_NUM_BUFFERS` (2) times. The two TEARDOWN paths already guard exactly this (`if (fft_work == s_shared_fft_work) fft_work = NULL;`) - only the build path was missing it. **Trigger, from the log one second earlier:** `W ft8_view: t_clock_cb: FT8 view visible but no ft8_task alive - respawning` — the respawn watchdog raced the normal FT8-entry path and both built the pool (4 `Block size` lines where one build emits 2). **DONE:** the build loop now frees only a buffer the monitor owns, and an unreferenced old shared buffer is **leaked once** rather than freed, because a decode may still be reading it on the other core - a use-after-free would be just as fatal and harder to find. It logs a LOUD warning when that happens, so the double build stays visible instead of being silently survived. **NOT DONE, and this is the actual bug:** the pool should never be built twice. That means the respawn watchdog (`ft8_task_is_alive`, added v0.20.0) and the FT8-entry path agreeing on who starts `ft8_task` - task lifecycle, which this project's history says is the fragile part (the v0.18.1 double-spawn crash, the v0.19.5 worker-join crash). Deliberately not attempted mid-release. **ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED 2026-08-20 (`3f348fc`) — it was the meaning of the alive flag.** `s_ft8_task_alive` was set as `ft8_task`'s FIRST STATEMENT, so it meant "an ft8_task has begun **RUNNING**". Both readers need it to mean "an ft8_task **EXISTS**": `ft8_self_test()`'s single-instance guard and the 1 Hz respawn watchdog. Between spawn and first run sits the whole scheduling delay — and `ft8_task` is created at `tskIDLE_PRIORITY + 1`, the **LOWEST priority on the board**, with the capture task, `fft_task` (4), taskLVGL, the feeds (2) and httpd (5) all above it. Taking over a second to get its first slice is ordinary here, not exceptional: the same starvation was measured the same day in `record_decode`, where the mutex holder went **~190 ms** without CPU. So the entry path spawned the task, the watchdog looked a second later, saw a flag still false, logged `respawning` and spawned a **second** one — both built the pool, and the second double-freed the shared FFT scratch. **Fix:** claim the slot in `ft8_self_test()` **before** `xTaskCreate`, and release it if creation fails (nothing else would ever clear it, and FT8 would be dead for the session with the watchdog refusing to retry). Chosen over a watchdog grace period deliberately — that only makes a race rarer, and this project's history is emphatic that **timing changes the odds of a race, not the race** (see the two falsified attempts against the `cdc_acm` close abort). ⭐ **EXERCISED ON HARDWARE 2026-08-20 with the radio on:** 7 FT8 entries → 2 ft8_task spawns, **1 legitimate respawn**, **5 correct guard refusals**, and **exactly one pool build per spawn**; zero `rebuilt with no teardown`, zero double-frees, zero crashes, device alive throughout. The respawn was immediately followed by a refusal, which is the flag going true at once — the guard is demonstrably live, not merely unexercised. Read as **not contradicted rather than proven**: reproducing the original race needs the watchdog to look inside the spawn-to-first-run window and that cannot be forced on demand. The v1.8.6 loud warning stays as the field detector. ⚠ An earlier note here claimed this could not be tested with the radio off; that was based on toggles that used the wrong action name (`screen` instead of **`set_screen`**) and silently did nothing — see #38 |
| 193 | 🔴 **The web UI panadapter stalls for seconds at a time, and it is getting worse every release** (Samuel W7STF #177196) | ✅ **ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED `73f46fe`, FLASHED, server side VERIFIED on hardware** (0 teardowns, 0 ECONNRESET, 8 partial writes healed in 9.3 min). Wants a VISIBLE browser to close out | Medium | His words: *"Does it seem to you that the Web-UI panadapter hangs from time to time, and then several seconds later it begins to animate again? It never used to be this bad. Is there too much background processing going on? Every release, it seems to stall more and more, and for longer periods of time."* ⚠ **Treat as the most serious open item** — it is a regression, it affects every web user, and he has watched it degrade across releases, which is a much stronger signal than a single observation. **Do NOT guess**: every "obvious cause" in CLAUDE.md has been wrong when measured. Three mechanisms to DISCRIMINATE BETWEEN, not to assume: **(1)** the background feeds (POTA/SOTA/RBN/DX cluster/PSK Reporter/`update_check`) each fetch periodically, and an HTTPS fetch on this weak link is already known to starve the WS stream — the LoTW forge fetch once wedged httpd outright, which is why `pauseWs()` exists. This fits "worse every release" best, since feeds have been ADDED over time and nothing paces them against each other. **(2)** the SDIO recoveries — the 2026-08-18 soak logged **592 in 9 h (~1/min)** plus 8 `0x126` timeouts, and **each recovery discards a frame**. **(3)** genuine accumulation of background CPU work (note core 0 sits at 0-5% idle in panadapter mode and CLAUDE.md records ~14-35% historically — unexplained since v1.8.3). **The measurement:** run a serial capture with the web UI open and correlate the WS gaps he sees against feed-fetch timestamps and `draining to recover` lines in the same capture. His own guess ("too much background processing") is plausible but is still a guess until the timestamps line up . **✅ MEASURED AND ROOT-CAUSED 2026-08-19 — and his guess was right about the trigger but the fault is elsewhere.** Mined the running 9.6 h capture before touching code: **545 session teardowns, median 14.4 s apart**, each costing the browser a **median 2.2 s (p90 4.5 s)** blackout while it reconnected — precisely the symptom he describes — with the stream perfectly healthy in between (median **10.0 fps**, the target). Enrichment in the 2 s before a teardown, against an 8.1 % base rate: **RBN 8.4x, DX cluster 8.6x**, TLS 5.7x, SDIO recovery 4.6x. **Mechanism (2) and socket exhaustion both TESTED AND FALSIFIED** (SDIO is enriched but present before only 11 % of closes; 0 accept/ENFILE errors) — do not go looking there again. **The actual fault is in IDF, verified by READING the pinned v5.4.4 source rather than inferred:** `httpd_ws_send_frame_async()` sends header and payload with two bare `sess->send_fn()` calls and checks only `< 0`, but `httpd_default_send()` is a single `send()` returning a BYTE COUNT — so **a partial TCP write is reported to us as `ESP_OK`**. The ordinary HTTP path is fine; it uses `httpd_send_all()`, which loops. The WS path never got the loop. It bites hardest here because `ws_uri_handler()` sets `SO_SNDTIMEO` to 400 ms so a stuck send cannot freeze the single httpd worker (the 2026-07-14 freeze fix), and a send timeout on a socket with bytes already queued is exactly how a short count arises — so **the freeze fix and this bug are the same line of code**. The result is not a dropped frame but a **corrupt stream**: the browser is told to expect `WS_FRAME_LEN` payload bytes, gets fewer, and consumes the next frame's header as this frame's tail, so every later frame is misparsed until it sees a protocol violation and closes the socket. That is why the symptom is a multi-second freeze-and-return rather than one glitchy frame, and why "worse every release" fits — feeds have been ADDED over time, so there is more congestion to trigger it. **Fixed entirely in our own code, no 9th standing IDF patch needed**, since `httpd_socket_send()` is public and returns the byte count: `ws_send_all()` loops until every byte is out; the WS header is built in front of the payload so the two cannot be split; a frame we are committed to mid-way must be finished or the session closed, because abandoning it IS the corruption; EAGAIN with nothing sent yet is free to drop since the stream is still in sync, so congestion no longer costs the session at all; and `HTTPD_SOCK_ERR_INVALID` (httpd has already removed the session) closes immediately instead of burning 15 attempts ≈ 800 ms on a socket that no longer exists. **Counted, not silent:** `partial writes healed` on the fps line — before the fix these were indistinguishable from healthy sends, the same unverifiability trap #189 documents. ⚠ **NOT YET FLASHED.** The confirmation to look for: teardowns collapsing from ~545/9.6 h toward zero while `partial` climbs |

NOT REPRODUCED** (this bench unit has a pack fitted, so Randy's failing path cannot be exercised here) | Small | He runs the Tab5 from USB-C with **no NP-F550 fitted**, and the bottom-left readout toggles between the two extremes. Start at `util/battery.c` / `util/ina226.c` and the bottom-bar readout in `ui/ui_clock.c`. ⚠ **First hypothesis TO TEST, NOT TO ASSUME:** 8.4 V and 4.2 V are exactly 2:1, which looks like a 2S-vs-1S interpretation flipping between samples — but a floating input with no cell attached can read anything, so confirm what the raw INA226 sample actually does before believing the arithmetic is at fault. Low priority by his own assessment; worth doing because a wrong battery readout is the kind of thing that gets reported again by someone who DOES care |
| 195 | ⭐ **"What are the long-term plans for the FT8 web access?"** (Randy N4OPI) | ✅ **DECIDED 2026-08-19 by the operator: NO spectrum/waterfall on the web — reply owed** | — | His full message: *"What are the long term plans for the FT8 web access? I have quite a list of requests, but if you're planning to eventually mirror the screen on the Tab5, I'll wait till that comes out."* So this is not a technical question and cannot be answered by reading code — he is deliberately holding back a list until he knows whether to send it. **Answering it costs nothing and unblocks him; not answering it silently loses the list.** Two coherent answers exist and they lead to very different work: (a) the web UI stays a SEPARATE surface with its own strengths, in which case send the list; or (b) the Tab5 screen eventually gets mirrored, in which case per-feature web requests are largely wasted effort. Standing guidance in `memory/project_web_tab5_parity.md`: diff **SURFACES, not commit titles**, and ask *"would this be needed at a POTA site with no laptop?"* — that question is also the honest test for whether mirroring is the right goal at all . **✅ DECIDED 2026-08-19 (operator): there will be NO spectrum/waterfall on the web in FT8 mode — *"Randy cannot have the spec/WF on the web due to lack of resources in Tab5 - a decision taken long time ago"*.** It is a hardware limit, not a missing feature: the FT8 decode pipeline saturates core 0, which is precisely why the Tier-1 render gate turns the spectrum stream off in FT8 mode, so mirroring would be paid for in decode yield. **The reply must say the mirror is NOT planned and why**, so he stops waiting, and must ask for the list — the four control gaps (filters/auto-answer, CQ presets, mid-QSO overrides, time sync) are exactly the kind of thing that can be added. Do not leave it sounding merely deferred. | 196 | **Radio Menus render all white; PuTTY shows the same screens in red/green** (Samuel W7STF) | ✅ **Fixed `ef1ada6`, flashed, and HARDWARE-VERIFIED ON BOTH SCREENS 2026-08-19** | Small | `util/ansi_term.c` **already parses and stores a per-cell `fg`** (SGR 30-37) alongside `reverse` — the capture confirmed `ESC[3xm` colour in the stream. **Both renderers simply discard it:** `ui/qmx_term_view.c` and the web one read only `reverse`. If it recurs, get the diag log at the moment it fires: the warning is raised from the second-serial-port probe, so the interesting question is whether the probe genuinely failed (a real re-enumeration race) or whether the warning is raised on a path that cannot know yet . **ROOT-CAUSED 2026-08-19: the warning was misattributing a transient to a settings mistake.** `qmx_term.c` opened interface 5 exactly ONCE and produced that wording on ANY failure — so a radio still finishing an enumeration (precisely what a cable swap causes) was reported as a configuration error, sending the operator into the radio's menus to change something already correct. The two cases differ in one observable way: a one-port radio has no interface 5 and fails identically forever, while a device mid-enumeration fails now and succeeds shortly after — so the open is now retried 4x at 200 ms (a genuine one-port radio still gives up in ~0.6 s). And the setting is only NAMED when `cat_is_ready()`, since CAT on interface 0 of the same device proves it enumerated; otherwise the log says the radio is not connected and that this is NOT a serial-port problem. | 161 | 🔴 **Terminal: numeric fields cannot be edited — no cursor, no working delete, and multi-digit/table values do not increment** (Randy N4OPI #177010, Samuel W7STF #177019) | ✅ **DONE + operator-verified on the radio** — cursor, one BS key sending **0x7F**, and typing all work; the "fields that refuse to increment" were never an increment problem, they are backspace-and-retype | Medium | Randy's own pattern is the lead: *"numerical values in a table or those that are over 2 digits don't Incr-Decr properly. In a table the arrow buttons moves the cursor to the next column and in single value fields with more than 2 digits it does nothing."* His list: **Max PA Voltage** (the one `nn.n` field) won't adjust with L/R while every other Protection value will; **band config table** L/R moves column instead of incrementing; **CAT Config Timeout**, **System Config TCXO**, **Display/Controls dbl-click timeout**, and the same in **Virtual U3S** (Max PA Voltage, FSK Shift) all refuse to change. On the **web** side, typing appends instead of replacing (typing 1 into 8000 gives 80001) and **Backspace/Delete do nothing** — we send `\b` (0x08); the radio may want DEL (0x7F). **Also ours: the cursor is never drawn.** `ansi_term_t` tracks `cur_r`/`cur_c`/`cursor_visible` and the renderer ignores all three, so in Messages you cannot see where you are typing. Fix the cursor and DEL first (unambiguous), then determine on hardware which key the QMX wants for the fields that refuse — do NOT guess a key mapping into a release. **✅ ANSWERED BY RANDY 2026-08-17 (#177068), and it was not an increment key at all — the editing MODEL was the thing I had wrong.** *"In PuTTY landing in a numerical field puts the cursor at the right most digit. Backspace deletes leftward and then you can type in the desired values. Del does nothing."* So: **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-08-17 (same evening): the working byte is 0x7F, NOT 0x08.** I read "Backspace" as 0x08 and deleted 0x7F - but PuTTY's Backspace sends 0x7F by default, so Randy's Backspace WAS 0x7F, and his "Del does nothing" is PuTTY's Delete (ESC[3~). Measured on the radio in Protection -> Max. PA voltage with up/down as the control: **0x08, left, right and Enter all do NOTHING** (the radio does not even answer - `seq` unchanged), while **0x7F deletes leftward** (11.5 -> 11. -> 11) and typing then appends (11 -> 119). The key keeps the name "bksp" and the label BS - only the byte changed. Found because the operator tried it and said "It does not work - BS has no effect - try it your self?" — the two-key hedge shipped in v1.8.5 can collapse to one. These fields are not meant to increment; they are backspace-and-retype. That also explains my own probe result, which looked contradictory: BS moved the cursor but changed no text — I was testing in **Messages**, a TEXT field, not a numeric one. Retest in a numeric field. Three more facts from him: **1-2 digit numeric fields DO increment with the arrows as long as they are not in a table**; QMX **4.x.x changed table behaviour** so the arrows now move left/right to the next band rather than requiring a trip to the top of the column (except in a boolean enable/disable field); and **the same issues exist in the Messages and Presets tables**, which I had not covered. ⚠ Do not "fix" the arrows in tables — per Randy the arrows are *supposed* to move between columns/bands there |

| 165 | 🔴 **CW waterfall does not compensate the CW offset correctly — tap-to-tune lands off frequency** (Roy KI0ER #177031) | ✅ **Both parts now fixed** — the stale cache (v1.8.5) AND the residual (v1.8.6: a -60 default trim plus rounding the IF offset to a whole 46.88 Hz bin; the arithmetic predicts both of Roy's measurements). ⚠ v1.8.6 part not confirmed against a signal | Medium | v1.8.4 / QMX 1.04.007. The dial reading matches the radio, but the waterfall/spectrum position does not. His measurements, CW centre → error in where the signal appears: **650 → +30 Hz, 700 → +40 Hz, 800 → +140 Hz** — and he explicitly retracted his own formula because *"the evidence when using different CW offsets is not linear."* Consequence is not cosmetic: tapping a CW signal tunes ~30 Hz off, so he transmits off frequency as if XIT were on, and with his +60 Hz offset the far station hears him +90 Hz out; he also then hears 620 Hz instead of his preferred 650. Visible on any POTA CW spot — the peak sits right of the passband centreline. Start at `ui_get_if_offset_hz()` (CLAUDE.md calls it the one documented place defining which baseband frequency the dial maps to) and at how the stored `cw_pitch_hz` relates to what the radio reports; note v1.8.1 already found our boot-time push never reached the radio. ⚠ Do not fit a curve to three hand-read points — find the arithmetic. **ROOT CAUSE (2026-08-17): `s_cw_offset_hz` was written in exactly ONE place — the one-time link-up sequence in `link_task` — and never refreshed.** So the moment the operator changed CW offset or CW centre on the radio (front panel or terminal), our display compensation froze at the link-up value for the rest of the session. That is precisely what Roy was doing while measuring, and a stale CONSTANT is what "not linear in the value I asked for" looks like — it was never a scale error, which is why his own formula had to be retracted. **Fixed:** `cw_offset_refresh()` in `cat.c`'s `poll_task` re-reads `MMCW|CW offset;` every 5 s, CW/CW-R only (zero cost in any other mode), change-detected logging, and re-seeds `ui_seed_cw_pitch_hz()`. **Bench measurements that justified the approach (QMX 1_04_004, Auto-offset/tone=YES, passband 300):** setting `CW center=650` dragged BOTH `CW offset` and `Sidetone freq.` to 650 and restoring 700 took all three back — so `CW offset` is the right item and it tracks the centre; and four consecutive reads returned the same value with the passband untouched, i.e. reading has no `FW;`-style re-assert side effect, which is what makes polling it safe. **hw-VERIFIED:** with the radio changed under a running session the log shows `QMX CW offset changed on the radio: 700 -> 650 Hz` then `650 -> 800 Hz`, and `/api/status` `cw_pitch_hz` followed 700→650→800 (before the fix all three read 700). Radio restored to as-found afterwards. ⚠ **What is NOT settled:** whether the stale cache accounts for ALL of Roy's numbers. A single stale value X predicts error = requested − X, which his 30/40/140 would need X = 620/660/660 — not one constant, so either he re-seeded X with a Tab5 reboot between tests, or a second component remains. Candidate: his own IF-calibration trim (`if_cal_hz`, −60 on the bench unit here) adds a constant. **Ask him to retest on the fix rather than fitting anything** — and note the manual's table says all achievable filter centres are multiples of 25, so 620/660 cannot be centres and centre-snapping (worst case 50 Hz) can never produce +140. Also learned: `CW offset` is valid 600–800 only, a NARROWER range than `CW center`'s 500–950 25 Hz grid — two different parameters, and CLAUDE.md's CW-centre note is about the latter |
