| 172 | 🔴 **THIRD instance of manual-right/automatic-wrong: the pileup drain also answers a report with a report** | ✅ **Fixed + sim-verified; the sim run then found a flaw in the fix, also fixed** | Medium | Fixing #167 (`cqrun_answer`) prompted a grep for the same shape, and it is there a third time. `try_start_pileup_pounce()` always replies with a **bare** report (`fmt_report(pile[best].snr_db)`) and starts in WAIT_ROGER. Correct for a caller who sent a **grid** — wrong for Gyula HA3HZ's experienced operator who reported us instead, who should get `R<report>` and WAIT_RR73. ⚠ **It cannot be a one-line branch**: `ft8_pileup_entry_t` records only `call`, `snr_db`, `freq_hz` and `last_seen_utc` — **not what they sent** — so the drain has no way to tell the two apart. Two ways out: (a) add a flag to the pileup entry, set when the caller's third field was a report; or (b) ⭐ **the real fix — look the caller up in the heard table at drain time and build the reply with `ft8_qso_build_manual_reply()`**, which already derives all of this from `last_text` and has been correct in every one of these three cases. (b) removes the second message-building path instead of patching it again, which is what keeps producing this bug: #134 (pileup sent a grid), #167 (`cqrun_answer` sent a bare report), and now this. **Do (b), and verify in sim** — N5XYZ is now a `terse` phantom, so the case is reproducible on the bench |
| 173 | 🔴 **Clock reported `UTC(GPS)` on a QMX with no GPS — we were measuring our own time push** (operator's bench unit, 2026-08-17) | ✅ **Fixed + BOTH branches hw-verified in v1.8.5** | Done + follow-up | The GPS auto-detect confirms "GPS-disciplined" when the QMX's second-tick agrees with SNTP inside `QMX_GPS_CONFIRM_MS` (300 ms). Its comment argued a push-set RTC could not pass, being "only whole-second accurate". **That is wrong:** `cat_set_qmx_time()` sends `TM<hhmmss>;` at whatever instant the call happens and the radio starts its second when it parses, so the phase we induce is uniform in 0..1000 ms and clears 300 ms about a third of the time unaided. Measured agreement was **12 ms**. `qmx_sync_once()` does detect before pushing *within a boot*, but `s_qmx_detect_done` resets on a **Tab5** reboot while the clock we set lives in the **radio** — so reflashing with the QMX powered makes the "first" detection read our own previous push. Consequences: the label lies; `push_to_qmx()` then early-returns so we **stop maintaining the clock of a radio that has no other source** (a QMX's software RTC is explicitly not persisted across a power cycle); and `settings_set_qmx_gps()` **persists** it, so an offline POTA session inherits the false verdict and `apply_gps_tick()`'s offline branch starts accepting stray RTC ticks as authoritative — the same class as Don WB0LQW's v1.8.2 bug. **Fix:** new persisted `qmx_time_pushed` (NVS `qmx_tpush`, dirty bit 90) — set whenever a push succeeds, and while set a tight agreement is refused as evidence with a log line saying why; cleared when the radio's clock is more than `QMX_CLOCK_LOST_SEC` (60 s) off, which is the power-cycle-to-00:00 moment its clock becomes its own again. On upgrade any stored verdict is discarded and `qmx_time_pushed` assumed true (safe direction: a genuine GPS owner loses only the label while SNTP still keeps the clock right). **VERIFIED on hardware, both branches, zero reboots during the test:** clock-lost clear — `QMX tick 00:04:57 off SNTP by 22971174ms - not GPS-disciplined` then a push; and the refusal — `QMX tick 17:52:08 agrees to 154ms, but WE set this radio's clock - not treating that as GPS`, followed by a push, i.e. we keep maintaining the clock rather than abandoning it. 154 ms sits inside the 300 ms window the old code accepted, so that line IS the regression test. Migration verified too (fires once, verdict → "not present", `time_src` reads `NTP`). **Mechanism confirmed empirically:** successive pushes measured **12 ms**, **834 ms**, **154 ms** of induced phase — uniform in 0..1000 ms as predicted, so the old code lied or not purely on the draw. And the false verdict was *persistent* because the periodic passes are 300 s apart, an integer number of seconds, so a push **preserves** the prior phase (two consecutive pushes 301002 ms apart = a 2 ms shift): phase is set by the first push after a radio power-cycle and then sticks. **Test aids added:** dev action `{"action":"time_redetect"}` → `time_sync_force_redetect()` re-arms the once-per-boot detection with no reboot (a Tab5 reset with the radio attached is the #74 trigger, and recovering needs a QMX power cycle, which resets the radio's clock and destroys the precondition — warm resets wedged 2 for 2 that day); the tight phase was produced deliberately by sending `TM<hhmmss>;` through `cat_raw` timed ~220 ms ahead of a true UTC second boundary. ⚠ **Still only inferred:** that the new `qmx_tpush` NVS key survives a reboot. Its write path is identical to `qmx_gps`, whose persistence *was* observed (the one-shot migration line is correctly absent on later boots), but it was never directly seen — the flag was cleared and re-set inside a single boot. **The next Tab5 boot is that test:** had it not persisted, detection would falsely CONFIRM on that boot; look for the refusal line instead. ⚠ **Known limitation, deliberate:** a QMX+ we had already pushed to will not re-confirm GPS until its clock is next seen unset. The clean removal is to stop inferring and **ask the radio** — `GPS source` in its GPS & Ser. Ports menu reads `QMX+ Internal` for a permanently fitted GPS, and MM can Get it over CAT (confirmed today that `MMSystem config\|Real time clock;` answers `MMSoftware;`, and `...?;` returns the numeric path `MM5\|16`, so this menu is readable). See #174 |
| 180 | ✅ **RESOLVED same evening: the three esp_hosted/CDC patches were MISSING from `managed_components/`** (bench, 2026-08-17) | ✅ Root-caused + fixed + hw-verified. ⚠ My first hypothesis (BLE) was WRONG | Medium | Symptom: the web UI stopped answering within ~4 min of boot, twice, while the device stayed completely healthy — `boots: 1`, no panic, audio still streaming ~48k pairs/s, `idle1` 90%. **I first blamed BLE**, because the log showed `NimBLE: HCI wait for ack returned 19` / `BLE controller reset, reason=19` looping BEFORE the WiFi wedge, and BLE was scanning with no mouse paired. **Falsified by the operator in one step: he turned BLE off and WiFi died again.** The next boot then showed the real signature with BLE provably off (`btmouse: BLE mouse disabled in settings - stack not started`): `H_SDIO_DRV: sdio_get_len_from_slave: Len from slave[3246] exceeds max [1536]` followed by permanent `rpc_core 0x126` — i.e. the **documented** oversize pending-byte delta, with the recovery patch's own `draining to recover` line **absent**. Checked `managed_components/`: **all three patches were gone** — esp_hosted PSRAM transport, esp_hosted SDIO RX recovery, and cdc_acm close-tolerant. That directory is git-ignored, so nothing in the repo could show it. Re-applied all three, rebuilt, reflashed: **2 oversize events, both logged `draining to recover`, 0 rpc timeouts, WiFi up** — the same event that wedged the link all evening now self-heals, which is the patch working exactly as documented. **Lessons, both worth more than the fix:** (1) the DMA-starvation theory was also wrong and I nearly published it off three samples — the FULL `dma free=` series plateaus at ~11.5 KB rather than collapsing, which is the trap my own note about pulling the whole series describes; (2) **nothing checks that the standing patches are applied on a NORMAL build** — only the release process mentions re-applying them, and a silently-unpatched tree looks like a hardware fault. A build-time guard is worth adding (see #181). Also note every build flashed earlier this evening was unpatched, so any instability observed during that window is suspect |
| 182 | 🔴 **NEW USB assert reboots the device, and THAT is what wedges the QMX overnight** (bench soak of the released v1.8.5, 2026-08-18) | ✅ **CLOSED — shipped as standing patch #7 in v1.8.6** | Small | `assert failed: usb_dwc_hal_chan_decode_intr /IDF/components/hal/usb_dwc_hal.c:504 (chan_intrs & USB_DWC_LL_INTR_CHAN_CHHLTD)` at **1 h 57 m** of an otherwise healthy FT8 soak - radio streaming 46,806 pairs/s, FT8 mid-capture, heap 55 KB free. Aborts -> `SW_CPU_RESET`. **The overnight QMX wedge is a CONSEQUENCE, not a separate fault:** the abort warm-resets the Tab5 with the radio attached, which is the documented #74 trigger, and the radio then never re-enumerated for the remaining 5 h 26 m (4 ENUM failures, then `RX 0 pairs/s` all night). Fix the assert and the overnight wedge goes with it. **The code:** `if (chan_intrs & CHAN_INTRS_ERROR_MSK) { HAL_ASSERT(chan_intrs & USB_DWC_LL_INTR_CHAN_CHHLTD); }` with the comment *"An error should have halted the channel"* - an ASSUMPTION the hardware violated. A channel error interrupt arrived without the halt bit. **The graceful path is already there, immediately below**: it classifies the error (STALL/BBLEER/BNA/XCS_XACT), sets `chan_obj->flags.active = 0` and returns `USB_DWC_HAL_CHAN_EVENT_ERROR`, which the HCD handles - and patch #4 already made the bulk path tolerant of exactly that. So the fix is to drop/soften the assert and let the error be reported, which is the SAME shape as standing patches #4 (`hcd_dwc.c` bulk-error assert) and #5 (`hub.c` recover in `ESP_ERROR_CHECK`). This would be **standing patch #7**, in the pinned IDF tree, so it needs a script + an entry in `tools/check_patches.py` (whose table is the thing that makes it non-optional). ⚠ **v1.8.5 shipped WITHOUT this**, so users can hit the reboot; frequency unknown beyond once in ~2 h on this bench under FT8 - earlier 10 h+ soaks never showed it, so do not assume the rate. **Generalise:** three asserts now in IDF's USB stack on hardware states it treats as impossible but which occur on this board - when a new USB abort appears, look for a HAL_ASSERT/ESP_ERROR_CHECK guarding a "cannot happen" case with a working error path beside it | `tools/patches/apply_usb_dwc_hal_chan_error_tolerant.ps1` exists and `tools/check_patches.py` fails the build without it; the counter added by #189 reports it in `/api/status` as `usb_patch.chan_err_no_halt`. ⚠ Still never OBSERVED firing - which is a statement about the fault's rarity, not about the patch.
| 183 | 🔴 **v1.8.5 shipped with the whole web UI dead** (Randy N4OPI, Michael KZ4LY) | ✅ **Fixed in v1.8.6 + build guard added** | Small | One unterminated string literal in `index.html` stops the ENTIRE inline `<script>` parsing, so the page draws its controls and then does nothing: no spectrum, no waterfall, no working buttons, "disconnected" in red, both Chrome and Firefox. Not a typo - written by a script whose shell collapsed a backslash-n into a real newline before Python saw it, the SAME collapsing that put a NUL byte in `ft8_qso.c` and a raw DEL in `qmx_term.c` the same evening. The C compiler caught both of those instantly; nothing caught this, because the build had no reason to parse HTML. **Fix is `tools/check_web.py`**: extracts the inline script and runs `node --check` as a PRE_BUILD step, failing the build with the parser's own message. Mutation-tested by re-breaking the literal exactly as shipped. Skips (not fails) when node is absent |
| 184 | **Terminal: typing blind behind the on-screen keyboard past message 9** (Michael KZ4LY) | ✅ **Fixed in v1.8.6** | Small | He suggested a transparent keyboard; that is the worse answer, since two layers of text are readable only if you already know what they say. The grid now SCROLLS so the row the radio's cursor is on stays above the keyboard (`KB_VIS_H`, `s_scroll_px`), and the grid clips so rows slide away under the header rather than over it. Applies to all three positioned things - rows, cursor, reverse-video blocks |
| 185 | **Two-finger blank works fewer than one try in ten; the teal tune line steals the tap** (Michael KZ4LY) | ✅ **Fixed in v1.8.6** | Small | Two causes, one root. The tap only ended on `npts == 0`, but two fingers never leave the glass on the same poll, so a normal 2->1->0 lift measured the tap as lasting until the SECOND finger left and blew the 300 ms budget nearly every time. And that intermediate single finger was indistinguishable from a deliberate touch, reaching BOTH the pan/hold-to-tune path and LVGL's own `touch_event_cb` - which is the teal cursor he described. Now ends on `npts < 2`, plus a 400 ms `MULTI_LOCKOUT_MS` that suppresses the straggler in both paths. ⚠ Not verified by me - needs two real fingers |
| 186 | **Band config tables: E/D change a value and the arrows will not move column** (Stan Dye KC7XE) | ✅ **Documented in v1.8.6** (no code needed) | — | The Enable/Disable entries accept `E`/`D` as characters - the on-screen keyboard already types them - and because those fields use the arrows to change the VALUE, the arrows will not move between columns while you are on one; step onto a numeric column first. The radio's own behaviour, and it explains something that otherwise reads as broken arrows in tables. In `guide/radio-menus.md`. Stan also independently confirmed the 0x7F delete byte |
| 187 | **Auto-answer off after a restart** (Brian WA6JFK) | ✅ By design, no action — answered by Roy KI0ER in-thread | — | Roy: *"One would not expect their QMX to just start transmitting on its own simply by turning the Tab5 device on. The QMX may not even be connected to a tuned antenna yet ... which would likely destroy the finals."* Matches WSJT-X, which also requires arming TX per startup. Worth keeping only so it is not "fixed" later by someone reading it as a bug. **CLOSED 2026-08-19 — Brian replied accepting it and endorsing the behaviour**: *"I figured as much just after I posted, but I think that was changed recently and like you say for the better to prevent wild transmissions."* No reply owed |
| 188 | **A radio receiving on VFO B silently ignores everything the Tab5 does** (Markus DL8MBY) | ✅ **Shipped in v1.8.7, reply posted 2026-08-19.** Benign path hw-verified; the CORRECTING path still untested (needs a radio deliberately on VFO B) | Small | His report: band select worked and the Tab5 display looked right, so CAT was plainly connected - but the frequency never changed on the radio and the receive frequency did not move. **Cause: we poll `FA;` and write `FA<freq>;`, which are VFO A ONLY.** With the radio receiving on VFO B every frequency write went to a VFO he was not listening to. The failure is nasty because it is PARTIAL - enough works to convince you the link is fine. He diagnosed it himself and then had to ask whether it was his own mistake; it was not. His own words on why it is easy to miss: *"As the VFO indicator is very small in the QMX+ display this could be easy overseen especially with my old eyes."* **Fix:** at CAT link-up ask `FR;` (the QMX's three-state VFO Mode - its CAT manual: *"0, 1, 2 correspond to VFO A, VFO B or Split respectively"*), and if it is not A, send `FR0;`, read back, and TELL the operator via a toast naming what it was - we changed their radio, so we say so. The proven sequence was already in the tree from Roy KI0ER's dual-VFO work. ⚠ **`FR` only, deliberately not `FT`/`SP`**: an operator running their own split receives on A and transmits on B, which works fine with the panadapter, and clearing FT/SP would break that for nothing - the same restraint `cw_split_maintain()` already observes. ⚠ Untested: needs a radio put on VFO B on purpose |
| 189 | **Standing patch #7 can never be confirmed from a log** | ✅ **Done 2026-08-18, NOT yet hardware-observed firing** | Small | Patch #7 turns the `usb_dwc_hal` channel-error assert into a normal error report and deliberately adds NO logging, because that code runs in the USB interrupt path and CLAUDE.md's cyan-flash rule forbids long/blocking work there. The consequence, noticed during the first soak after it shipped: a clean log is ambiguous - either the fault did not occur, or it occurred and was handled, and **nothing distinguishes them.** So the patch cannot be positively verified, only failed-to-be-contradicted. **Fix: a counter, not a log.** An `uint32_t` increment in the ISR is safe where a log call is not; expose it the way the UAC fork already exposes its ringbuf-overflow count, and print it on the existing periodic diagnostic line. Then "patch #7 fired N times and the device stayed up" becomes a statement of fact rather than an inference from silence |
| 190 | 🔴 **A bare `abort()` killed a 7-hour soak of the RELEASED v1.8.6 — and the "wedged QMX" report was this** | ✅ **Root-caused + fixed as standing patch #8 (2026-08-18), NOT yet hardware-observed firing** | Small | `abort() at PC 0x480f3e31`, 7 h 06 m into a perfectly healthy FT8 receive session (26 decodes/slot, 53 KB internal free, `idle0` 61 %), with **no assert text**. Identified as `_buffer_parse_error()`'s `default:` in `hcd_dwc.c` — the branch under the comment *"HCD_PIPE_EVENT_URB_DONE and HCD_PIPE_EVENT_ERROR_URB_NOT_AVAIL should not occur here"*. **The FOURTH member of the same family** as patches #4/#5/#7: IDF's USB stack aborting on a state it treats as impossible. Note `addr2line` names line 2578, a *different* switch whose `default:` is unreachable (2-bit field, all 4 values cased); the two identical bare `abort()`s fold, and `_buffer_parse_error` is `static inline` into `_buffer_parse`. Assertions are at level 2, so a failing `assert()` would have printed text — nothing did, which is what proves it was a plain `abort()`. **The reboot was not the expensive part:** it is a warm reset with the radio attached (#74), so the QMX then failed to re-enumerate and stayed dead for the remaining 5 h 26 m. Fix follows patch #4's own precedent in the same file — report `USB_TRANSFER_STATUS_ERROR` (`actual_num_bytes` is already 0) instead of aborting. Rate unknown: once in 7 h on this bench |
| 191 | 🔴 **The flash-persisted diag log stops writing: SPIFFS hits ENOSPC with ~400 KB still free** | ✅ **Root-caused + fixed 2026-08-18, NOT yet hardware-verified** | Small | Observed on the operator's own unit: `W diag: flash-persist: write failed (No space left on device)`. **Not a capacity problem** — SPIFFS reported 536 KB used of 934 KB, and `qso.adi` is only 6 KB (37 QSOs). Measured mechanism: the diag log grows **~47 KB/min** in an FT8 session (dominated by legitimate per-decode `ft8_test`/`ft8_screen` lines), so `diag.log` reaches its 256 KB cap and **rotates roughly every 11 minutes**, deleting a 256 KB file each time; SPIFFS then returns ENOSPC as soon as GC cannot free a whole block inside `CONFIG_SPIFFS_GC_MAX_RUNS` (10, the IDF default). It self-heals — `diag_log.c` rotates early on ENOSPC — but only by **discarding a generation**, so the flash log silently loses history, and that copy is the only log that survives the reboot it exists to explain. Fixes: GC budget 10 → 32 (safe here specifically because `SPI_FLASH_ERASE_YIELD_DURATION_MS=20` + XIP-from-PSRAM mean GC cannot hold cache down long enough to trip the cyan flash); **deleted the reader page/TOC cache writes**, dead since 2026-08-06 (`reader_view.c` reads the embedded blob — up to ~30 KB written per page view that nothing read back), plus a one-time reclaim of the stale files at boot; and the boot log now **lists every `/spiffs` file with its size**, because "536 KB used" was not actionable and answering "which file" previously needed a rebuilt firmware. ⚠ The structural option — the diag log deserves its own partition, there is ~6.5 MB of unused flash after `storage` — is NOT done: it changes the flash layout on shipped units, so it needs a deliberate decision (see #192) |
| 192 | **Give the diag log its own flash partition** (~6.5 MB is unused after `storage`) | ❌ Not started — needs a decision, not just code | Medium | The 1 MB `storage` partition holds the QSO log, the LoTW certificate **and private key**, and the diag log, and #191 showed the diag pair alone wants 512 KB of it. The partition table ends at 0x920000 of a 16 MB flash, so ~6.5 MB is unused. Adding a new partition *after* `storage` leaves `storage`'s offset and size untouched, so its SPIFFS magic still matches and **user data is not reformatted** — and the flasher already writes the partition table at 0x8000. That would end the competition permanently and let the flash log cover a whole overnight soak instead of ~11 minutes. **Do not do this casually:** it is a flash-layout change on units in the field, it needs the flasher scripts checked (see the v0.18.5 bootloader-corruption history), and an extra SPIFFS mount costs some internal RAM on a board where that is the scarce resource |
| 181 | **Fail the build (or warn loudly) when a standing patch is missing from `managed_components/` or the IDF tree** | ✅ **Done + mutation-verified, shipped in v1.8.5** — `tools/check_patches.py` as a PRE_BUILD step, fails the build naming the script to run. ⚠ Known gap: PRE_BUILD only fires when the main component relinks, so a no-op build skips the check | Small | Tonight #180 cost an evening of misdiagnosis — two wrong hypotheses, BLE and DMA starvation — because three patches had silently vanished from a git-ignored directory and the symptom looks exactly like a hardware fault. There are now SIX standing patches, three of them in `managed_ements`-style locations wiped by `fullclean`/dependency refresh and three in the pinned IDF tree, and the ONLY thing that says "re-apply them" is the release process. A normal `idf.py build` happily produces a binary that will wedge WiFi within minutes. Cheapest useful version: a CMake pre-build step that greps each patched file for its marker (`draining to recover`, `H_SDIO_TX_FAIL_STREAK_MAX`, `MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM` in os_wrapper.h, the cdc_acm retry, the hcd_dwc transfer-error return, the hub tolerant recover) and FAILS with the name of the script to run. The markers already exist and were exactly how the loss was diagnosed. Do not make it a warning only — a warning in a long IDF build scrolls past |
| 175 | 🔴 **Leaving Radio menus leaves the waterfall "doing the phantom thing" — a manual pause/resume clears it** (Roy KI0ER #177058) | ✅ **Fixed + shipped in v1.8.5**; the fix fires correctly, but the CAUSE is UNPROVEN — Q9 read 1 before, during AND after a session here, so a terminal open/close alone did not drop IQ. Roy had *changed* a setting. The fix covers both candidates (dropped Q9, stale floor) | Small | v1.8.4 / QMX 1.04.007. *"when I exit the QMX menu on the Tab5 after changing my sidetone volume, I still need to select 'Let me use the QMX menus' followed immediately by 'Done - Tab5 takes over' in order to clear the waterfall back to its normal state because the waterfall starts doing the phantom thing."* His workaround is literally the operator-pause resume path — which is the tell, because that path does three things a terminal close does not: re-runs the **IQ handshake** (`cat_request_iq_reassert()`), re-seeds the **flat floor** (`ui_flat_mode_reset()`), and restarts the CAT poll. CLAUDE.md already records that **`Q9` IQ mode is SESSION state** and that a trip through the radio's own menus can drop it, with the exact symptom being audio that keeps flowing while no longer being I/Q. A terminal session IS a trip through those menus, on a second port. **So: on `qmx_term` close, do what resume-from-pause does.** Verify by reading `Q9;` immediately after a terminal close — if it reads 0, this is confirmed outright rather than inferred. ⚠ Do NOT reach for `MU;` (it drops IQ mode — see the dual-VFO quirk). Also worth noting Roy's phrase "the phantom thing" ties this to **#118**, which is still open; if a terminal close reproduces #118 on demand that is a repro path for the harder bug |
| 176 | **"Show only CQ callers" hides the live exchange of a QSO involving my own callsign** (Roy KI0ER #177065) | ✅ **Done + hw-verified in sim** | Small | *"When 'Show only CQ callers' is selected, it literally does just that. But if there is an active QSO occurring with my callsign, the message display should also show all relevant messages associated with that exchange."* He is right that the filter's intent is "hide other people's CQs", not "hide my own contact in progress". The exemption already exists in spirit — `rebuild_list()` hides other stations' `CQ ` rows during a CQ run so replies to us stand out, and the decode list already knows which rows are addressed to us (they render inverted) and who the QSO/manual partner is (`ft8_qso_get_pinned_call()`, added v1.5.0 for exactly this kind of "keep the relevant thing visible" problem). So the fix is an exemption in the `hide_cq` branch for rows whose callsign is the current partner. He also confirms the **QSO itself completes and logs correctly** while invisible, so this is display-only |

It continues to connect properly and scrolling works, but the mouse pointer moves unpredictably."* He also power-cycled BT and rebooted twice, so it is not a stale-pairing artefact. v1.8.4 shipped both the report-map parser AND a log line that prints the parsed bit offsets/widths — that line is the whole point and nobody has read it yet for this mouse. **Ask Kevin for a diagnostic download with the mouse connected** (Roy KI0ER already told the group this is what is needed, #177065). Scrolling working while X/Y misbehave says the wheel field is located correctly and the X/Y fields are not, which is consistent with a report the parser mis-walks rather than a missing descriptor — but that is a hypothesis and the log settles it. ⚠ Do not add a pointer-speed setting: CLAUDE.md records that was deliberately refused for #152 because a scale factor cannot make one axis wrap while another stands still, so it would hide the fault | Kept because it is where the REAL cause was recorded: his mouse is BLUETOOTH, so #152's fix to `usb_hid_mouse.c` never applied to him at all. | 178 | **Dual-boot the Tab5 between this firmware and Zhenxing's** (Roy Ashkenaz K2RMA #177057) | ✅ **ANSWERED: no** — verified `partitions.csv` has ONE factory app partition and no OTA slots; adding one is a layout change that risks user data. Reflashing already does this in ~15 s | Answer only | *"Is it possible to allow the M5Stack to dual boot? I think it would be nice if I could choose to run Steff's panadapter program OR Zhenxing's program during boot up."* Worth answering rather than logging silently. The honest obstacles: our app partition is **8 MB of a 16 MB flash** and the partition table also carries `storage` (SPIFFS: ADIF log + LoTW cert **and private key**) and `user_nvs`; two app slots means repartitioning, which erases user data on every existing unit, and the flasher deliberately writes only 0x2000/0x8000/0x10000 precisely so that data survives an update. It would also need the other project's binary, its own partition expectations, and a boot selector. ESP-IDF's OTA slot mechanism is the closest fit and is not free. Not a flat no — but it is a repartition-everyone release, not a feature |

Worth answering because it is the second-most-common way someone decides whether to build one, after the screenshots. If nothing exists, that is itself the answer, and a short screen recording would be cheap next to the effort already spent on the docs |
| 174 | Ask the radio whether it has GPS instead of inferring it from clock agreement | ✅ **Done + hw-verified** 2026-08-17 | Small | Follow-up to #173, which removes its documented limitation. `MM` can Get any menu item over CAT: `MMGPS & Ser. Ports\|GPS source;` should answer `Paddle port` (no permanent GPS — a plain QMX must not leave one connected, it forces practice mode) or `QMX+ Internal` (a QLG3 fitted inside a QMX+, i.e. permanent GPS). Confirmed today that the MM Get path works and returns bare values (`MMSoftware;`). Use it as a positive override so a QMX+ owner is detected correctly even on a radio we have previously pushed to, and keep the phase test only as the corroborating measurement. **Do not** use `Real time clock` for this — it selects software vs CR2032-backed hardware RTC, not GPS (checked against the 1_04_001 operation manual) |
| 171 | **Cloudlog / Wavelog QSO upload** (Mark G4MEM, GitHub issue) | ✅ **BUILT + FLASHED 2026-08-19 (`c672640`, `fbad3cb`). Subnet rule host+mutation-tested; NOT tested against a real server** | Medium | *"Specifically, the ability to upload QSO's into Cloudlog after, say a POTA / SOTA operation in the field w/no Internet."* **API, read from the wiki rather than memory:** `POST <base>/index.php/api/qso`, JSON body `{key, station_profile_id, type:"adif", string:<ADIF>}`. **The doc is explicit that dupe checking is server-side and more than one QSO can be sent per request** — which is a gift: batching works like eQSL, and a re-send is harmless, so the cursor can be conservative (advance only on clear success) with no cost to the user. Effort is genuinely medium: `eqsl_upload.c` is 235 lines and this follows it almost exactly, plus settings and a web setup row. Dirty bits: highest allocated is 89 of 128, so 38 spare. ⚠ **TWO REAL DIFFERENCES from QRZ/eQSL/LoTW, and the first needs Mark's answer before any code.** (1) **The base URL is user-supplied — Cloudlog and Wavelog are self-hosted.** Every existing uploader has a fixed endpoint. A self-hosted instance is very often **plain HTTP on a LAN**, or HTTPS with a **self-signed certificate** — and our mbedtls path uses the CA bundle, which rejects self-signed outright. So we must decide whether to allow plain HTTP, and whether to allow skipping verification (and if so, say so plainly in the UI, since the API key travels in the body). **Ask Mark what his instance actually is** before choosing. (2) **No response format is documented at all**, so success can only be judged from the HTTP status — weaker than the documented strings QRZ and eQSL give us, and a false success that advances the cursor loses QSOs silently. Mitigated by the server-side dupe checking in (1): be strict about advancing, and let a repeat be free. ⭐ **Worth telling him:** if his Cloudlog is on his home LAN, this is the only one of the four uploaders that needs **no internet at all** — back home on his own network is enough, which fits the POTA case better than QRZ/eQSL/LoTW do. Taking a base URL covers Wavelog for free (the wiki's own example now says `WAVELOG_URL`)  **✅ HIS ANSWER, 2026-08-19 — and it is the case the draft reply flagged as 'a different problem worth solving properly rather than papering over'.** He lists the three ways to run Cloudlog (Peter MM9SQL's subscription, self-hosted public-facing with Let's Encrypt, self-hosted local) and says *"of course I run the latter"*. His proposal: *"Could it be feasible to permit http:// if the connection is to the same subnet that the device is currently connected to (and also allow the https:// connection if the server is remote)?"* — and his use case is precisely ours: *"the ability for me to upload the logs to Cloudlog once I return from the field, i.e. when I reconnect to my home lan"*. **The same-subnet rule is sound and is the right answer**, because it is a CHECKABLE boundary rather than a 'don't verify the certificate' switch — the traffic never leaves the wire the operator owns. Feasible with what we already have: `esp_netif_get_ip_info()` (used in `wifi.c`) yields our IP and netmask, so resolve the configured host, then allow plain HTTP only when `(target & netmask) == (ours & netmask)`; anything else must be HTTPS with a CA-verifiable certificate, exactly as QRZ/eQSL/LoTW are. ⛔ **THE CHECK MUST RUN PER-UPLOAD AT CONNECT TIME, NEVER ONCE AT SAVE TIME** — and this user's own scenario is the reason: he configures at home and then operates from a field site, so a save-time verdict would let the device post his API key in the clear to whatever answers that hostname on a hotel or park LAN. Re-checking each time fails safe: away from home the upload simply refuses and waits. Note Cloudlog would be the FIRST user-supplied upload address (QRZ/eQSL/LoTW are all hardcoded), so this is also the first time the firmware sends credentials somewhere it does not already know |
| 160 | **Soak runs happen OVERNIGHT ONLY — the bench is for development in daylight** | 🔵 Standing practice, set 2026-08-17 | — | Operator's rule: *"we need to make those soak runs at night so that the Tab5/QMX is available for development runs during daytime."* A soak occupies the hardware and COM3 completely, so it cannot overlap bench work. **Practice:** propose the soak in the EVENING so he can prepare the station (power, cable, whether BLE is on, whether the antenna matters for that run); start it before he stops for the night; read it in the morning. Use `scratchpad/cap_serial_reboot.ps1` with **no `-Reset`** and a `-Seconds` long enough to outlast the night — an expired capture reads exactly like a quiet healthy device, so always check the process is alive AND the file's `LastWriteTime` is within seconds before trusting it. **Queue of runs wanted:** (a) **BLE OFF, 12 h** — the repeat that makes #130's pair two-deep; (b) a long run hunting the unexercised SDIO TX drop path (#131). Record the starting state (firmware, screen, band, BLE, spur/robot/sim, battery) at the top of every run or the result cannot be interpreted |

| 155 | ✅ **ADIF viewer: Close was RED, and brighter red than "Delete all"** (Gyula HA3HZ) | ✅ Fixed `pending` — Close is now neutral; **not yet seen on screen** | — | *"When I want to close the ADIF-log window, I am surprised that the 'Close' button is red (bright red here) as well as the 'Delete all' button."* Worse than he put it: Close was `0x962020` and Delete all `0x5a1f1f`, so the SAFE button was the brighter of the two. `0x962020` is the house Cancel colour and is fine everywhere else, because elsewhere the worst outcome is "nothing happened" - this is the one modal with a destructive sibling. Close is now the neutral drawer-button style; **in this panel red means exactly one thing, and anything added here must stay off red** |
| 156 | 🔴 **A QSO that could not be written was still reported as logged** (found while answering Gyula's "how much can the log hold?") | ✅ Fixed `pending`; **failure path NOT exercised** (needs a deliberately full SPIFFS) | Small | The write path did its `fprintf`s then `fclose()` and checked NEITHER, so a full or failing filesystem lost the contact in silence while the code incremented the count and logged `Logged QSO #N`. The operator is told it is safe and finds out at upload time. Now: `ferror()` + `fclose()` both checked, `fsync` before close, and on failure an `ESP_LOGE` + a toast naming the station, with the count NOT incremented and no SD mirror. **Real answer to Gyula:** the `storage` partition is **1 MB** (934 KB usable) shared between `qso.adi`, the 256 KB rolling diagnostic log *and its rotation*, and the LoTW certificate + key - so a few hundred KB for the log, order of a couple of thousand QSOs. Worth telling users to download and clear periodically |
| 157 | **Spur suppression "doesn't seem all that effective"** (Samuel W7STF) | ✅ **ANSWERED with the measured figures** — Erase -78% vs Subtract -28% on a 38 dB comb; ceiling is the spur's own 0.5 dB wobble, so better detection would not help. Asked which mode + a screenshot | Medium | He asks directly: *"I think the detection method might need to be a bit more sophisticated. It doesn't seem all that effective to me. Is it for you?"* Before changing anything, establish WHICH mode he used: **subtract** is documented as ~12-17 dB and deliberately cannot hide a real signal, while **interpolate** takes +38.6 dB down to ~+10 dB. If he ran subtract and expected the spur to vanish, the feature worked as designed and the gap is the wording, not the DSP. The known ceiling is the spur's own ~0.5 dB wobble - *a constant cannot cancel something that moves* - so "more sophisticated detection" would not help; the discriminator (25 Hz nudge, offset moves at 16-50x the dial) is physical and has an order of magnitude of margin. Ask for a before/after screenshot and which mode, and do NOT start redesigning the detector on the strength of an impression |
| 158 | **Pile-up: two stations answer the CQ, what happens to the second?** (Gyula HA3HZ) | ✅ **ANSWERED** — both behaviours are by design (second caller waits in the pile-up). The real issue is that the screen does not say so; kept as a UX item | Small-Medium | *"In Pileup operations it is not yet clear that two partners answer the CQ and it ends with the first one, so what should I do? If I do nothing, it calls CQ again, if I select the second one and it is not present, it reports x times and times out."* Both behaviours are as designed, but nothing on screen tells him that, and a station that answered 90 s ago may genuinely have gone. Candidates: age the pile-up list visibly, or say why a pick timed out. Related to #139/#140 (already fixed: hit targets + clear-all) |
| 159 | **Tab5 battery: discharges when "off", and will not charge below 6 V** (Tony Abbey) | ✅ **CLOSED — M5Stack hardware behaviour, answered on-list** | Small | Tony's pack reached 0% / 6.1 V and would not recover. Roy KI0ER and Samuel W7STF answered well on-list: the Tab5's power-off is a SOFT shutdown (no mechanical switch in the battery path), **charging only happens once the device is powered on and initialised**, and below ~6 V the pack enters protection needing an external charger. Nothing to fix in firmware, but the user manual should say: pull the NP-F550 for storage longer than a few days, and a flat pack needs an external charger. ⚠ Do NOT let this be blamed on the panadapter without evidence - check `charge_limit_en` is not implicated if a second report arrives |

| 154 | 🔴 **`xSemaphoreTake(NULL)` aborts the device — audit every module mutex for use-before-init** | ✅ **Instance + CLASS both done (2026-08-20)** | Done | Serial-captured 2026-08-16: a web-requested switch to the FT8 screen, while the "Waiting for QMX..." prompt was up (CAT was down), rebooted the device with `assert failed: xQueueSemaphoreTake queue.c:1709 ((pxQueue))`. addr2line put it in `ft8_status.c` with that very string half-formatted on the stack: `ft8_status_set()`/`_get()` took `s_lock` with no NULL check, and `ft8_status_init()` runs from `app_main` (main.c:357) - later than the UI path that reached it. **`xSemaphoreTake(NULL, t)` is not a failed take, it is an immediate abort**, and a timeout argument does not save you. Fixed there by doing the copy unlocked when `s_lock` is NULL (pre-init there is no concurrency to protect against, so dropping the text would be worse). **Same shape, NOT yet checked for reachability before init:** `adif/adif_log.c` (6 sites), `ft8_greylist.c` (5), `ft8_pileup.c` (the `lock()` inline), `ft8_hash.c`, `dsp/dsp.c`. Each needs its init order checked against every caller rather than a blanket guard - but a NULL guard can only ever replace an abort, so it is a safe default where the ordering is not obvious. **AUDIT DONE 2026-08-20 (`4c64826`): two of the five were real, three were already safe.** ⭐ **`dsp.c` was the worst and nobody had noticed:** six takes, no guards, and `main.c` runs `ui_init()` at line **202** but `dsp_init()` only at **312** — over a hundred lines in which the UI and its LVGL timers are live while `s_spectrum_mtx`/`s_zoom_cfg_mtx` do not exist. That is precisely the ft8_status.c shape. All six now go through a `dsp_take()` helper treating "not created yet" as "could not take". **`ft8_greylist.c` had two faults:** four of its five sites re-check `s_lock` after `ensure_lock()` and one did not (a create that failed under memory pressure aborted instead of skipping a note); and the lazy create was unprotected while the pickers run on **taskLVGL** and the timeout notes come from the **decode task**, so both could create a mutex with the second overwriting the first — two tasks each holding a *different* mutex, i.e. no mutual exclusion at all, plus a leak. Now atomic under a spinlock. **Verified safe, not assumed:** `adif_log.c` creates `s_lock` as the FIRST statement of its init, before anything that can fail, and that runs at main.c:102; `ft8_hash.c` gates both takes on `!s_tab`, which is only assigned once the mutex succeeded; `ft8_pileup.c` guards all five `lock()` calls individually and creates in `ft8_pileup_init()` at main.c:365 |
| 151 | 🔴 **A TX offset chosen DURING a QSO is ignored — the engine keeps using the one from the QSO's start** (Roy KI0ER) | ✅ **ALREADY FIXED — verified in code 2026-08-19** | Small | v1.8.3 / QMX 1.04.007. His rule, and it is correct: *"During an active QSO, there's no expectation for either station to appear on the same, or any particular frequency offset. The only expectation is that the other station stays in their time window."* So there is no reason to remember the old tone. Why it bites: if your offset gets hijacked mid-exchange the other station can no longer hear you, and today the only escape is to cancel the TX, get a fresh occupancy map, then pick a new offset. `ft8_qso_set_tx_tone_hz()` already exists and refuses only while ACTIVE, so the likely fault is a re-arm path reading a saved tone (`s_freq_hz`/`s_cur_req.audio_freq_hz`/`s_cq_saved`) instead of the current one. **The operator's choice must win**  `ft8_qso_set_tx_tone_hz()` no longer refuses mid-burst: it stores the choice in `s_pending_tone_hz`, and `on_tx_complete()` applies it BEFORE the re-arm so the very next transmission uses it. Cleared on QSO end so a queued tone cannot leak into the next contact. The comment in the function quotes Roy's report verbatim. The row was simply never marked. |
| 152 | **USB mouse: pointer flies horizontally and crawls vertically** (Kevin KW6E, Microsoft Surface Arc Mouse) | 🟢 Fixed `7a7f6da`; **NOT verified on his mouse** (I have no Surface Arc) — the new `report map: id=.. X @bit../..b` log line at connect will confirm or refute it from his diag log | Small-Medium | v1.8.3. Connects and scrolls fine, but "rapidly moving between multiple points", with apparent speed/acceleration VERY HIGH in X and VERY LOW in Y. That asymmetry is the signature of X and Y being read at the wrong **bit offsets/widths** in the HID report, not of a missing sensitivity setting - a scale factor would affect both axes alike. `hid_report_map.c` exists precisely because mice do not agree on a layout and was written for the BLE path; the **USB** path (`usb_hid_mouse.c`) still assumes the boot-protocol layout. Check whether this mouse reports in boot protocol at all, and parse its Report Map the same way. He also asks for a speed/accel setting - worth having, but do NOT ship it as the answer to this, it would paper over a parsing bug |
| 153 | 🟠 **The SD diagnostic log cannot contain a crash — it only holds the first ~5 s of every boot** (found while triaging Michael KZ4LY's report) | ✅ **FIXED `pending` — slow diag mirror keeps the card log alive; NOT soaked** | Small-Medium | Michael sent "the full log from the microSD" after a light-blue-screen-then-restart during an FT8/FT4 band change with the display flipped 180°. It is 17 boot headers ~150 lines apart, each ending at uptime ~4.8 s: the WiFi-aware gate (continuous with WiFi off, **one boot snapshot with WiFi on**) means the card gets the opening seconds of each boot and nothing else. So the file cannot show what happened during operation, by design, and no backtrace exists to decode. Two things follow: (a) tell users to send `qmx-log-saved.txt` (the flash copy, which keeps appending) or a serial capture, and (b) reconsider the gate - a crash log that stops before the crash is worse than none, because it looks like evidence. Reset reasons in his file: 13 × `other-watchdog`, 3 × `unknown`, 1 × `panic/exception`, **0 backtraces**. Related to #117 (route the panic handler into the diag ring)  ✅ **FIXED `pending` — the SD log now continues past the boot snapshot.** The parking rationale was *"on a WiFi unit the SD diag log loses little, the full log is available over the network at /api/log"*, and that fails in exactly the case the log exists for: after a crash the RAM ring is GONE and the flash copy is a rolling ~11-minute window. So Michael's card could never have held his crash. **Fix:** instead of stopping dead, the diag log keeps mirroring on a **30 s** cadence (`SLOW_LOG_MS`) while WiFi is on, and every other file stays parked as before. The slow path **opens, appends, fsyncs and CLOSES** each time rather than holding the handle - so exposure to the SD/WiFi contention is roughly a TENTH of the old continuous mode AND there is no held-open handle for a card pull or a crash to damage, which is strictly safer than the pre-park behaviour on that count. Three consecutive failures unmount, as before. ⚠ **NOT soaked.** This touches the SD-during-WiFi hazard that has its own long section in CLAUDE.md, so it wants a night with a card in before it is trusted. The knob to back it out is one constant. |

| 147 | **A QMX terminal emulator on the Tab5** (Randy N4OPI, seconded by Michael KZ4LY) | ✅ **DONE 2026-08-16 — working on BOTH screens, hardware-verified end to end** | Medium | Randy runs a **headless QMX+ with no control panel**, so the Tab5 is his only possible route to the radio's menu system. Michael wants a headless unit too. Two things make this more attractive than it first looks: the QMX's TERMINAL menu is served over serial and, per Stan KC7XE, is **well isolated from the CAT control channel** - unlike the front-panel menu, which is the one that reboots the radio (see the front-panel quirk in CLAUDE.md). And the mechanism already exists: "Release radio" (`cat_user_pause_set`) stands the CAT poll down precisely so the operator can use the radio's own menus. **Findings from the operation manual, 2026-08-16:** ① ⭐ **The QMX can expose THREE virtual COM ports** (firmware 1_02_000+, a System config parameter): *"This permits use of a terminal emulator session at the same time as a Digi mode software program ... using one COM port for CAT control."* So the terminal need NOT fight the CAT poll or borrow the pipe - it gets its own. That removes the whole design problem. ② Our `cdc_acm_host_open(VID, PID, **0**, ...)` already takes an interface index, so opening a second is a small change plus a second handle. ③ The interface is a fixed **80x24** grid; cursor keys navigate, Enter selects, **Ctrl-Q** backs out of a nested app. At montserrat_20 that is ~960x576 px - it FITS on this screen at a readable size. ④ ⚠ **THE EXIT TRAP, and it is the safety issue:** the manual is explicit that closing a terminal session without choosing "Exit terminal" *"will leave the QMX in terminal applications mode and it will not accept CAT commands"*. Any implementation MUST guarantee the exit - on modal close, on mode switch, on disconnect - or the operator is left with a radio that ignores the panadapter. ⑤ **MEASURED ON HARDWARE 2026-08-16 — and the second port did NOT appear.** The menu path was discovered over CAT rather than guessed (my first guess was wrong twice: `System config` has no trailing period, and it is `GPS & Ser. ports` lowercase): `MM10\|8\|3` = "USB serial ports", type 5 list 32, `ML32;` → `ML1\|2\|3;`. Set to 2 by name, read back `MM2;`, **persisted across a QMX power cycle**, radio re-enumerated (`QMX CDC opened` at a fresh uptime) - and `cdc_acm_host_open(VID,PID,1,...)` STILL returns `0x103` not-found. Two candidates, **undiscriminated**: (a) the QMX is not actually presenting the second port despite the setting, or (b) it is, and our CDC-ACM driver cannot open a second interface on a device it already holds open. ⚠ **The decisive test needs no code: plug the QMX straight into a PC and count the COM ports.** Two means it is our driver; one means it is the radio. Do that before writing any terminal code. ⑥ Still unmeasured after that: whether the stream is ANSI/VT100 escapes or plain lines - decides a small VT100 parser vs painting rows. **⑦ RESOLVED 2026-08-16, both questions.** The PC test said one COM port, which pointed at the radio - but the real answer was **our probe**: it assumed the CDC interfaces were contiguous and `break`ed at the first gap, so it never looked past interface 1. The audio function occupies 2-4 and **the second serial port is at INTERFACE 5**. Opened it, wrote a CR, and the radio answered with a full menu repaint - **while CAT kept polling on interface 0 throughout**, which is exactly the isolation this feature needs. The stream is **ANSI/VT100**: ESC[2J, ESC[H, ESC[r;cH, ESC[?25l, ESC[3xm colour, and ESC[7m reverse video - and reverse video is how the SELECTED menu item is marked, so a renderer that drops it leaves the operator unable to see where they are. **⑧ Built:** `main/util/ansi_term.c` (80x24 model, `dirty_seq` so a UI can skip redraws) with `test/ansi_term_harness.c` driven by the REAL 288 captured bytes and re-run at all 287 byte-split points; and `main/qmx_term.c` (open/close/key/screen), whose `qmx_term_close()` walks the radio's own "Exit terminal" item by READING THE SCREEN rather than sending a fixed key count - the exit trap in ④ is handled. **⑨ SHIPPED:** `/api/term` (GET screen incl. reverse-video runs, POST open/close/key/text), web UI **Radio ▸ Radio menus**, Tab5 **Settings ▸ Radio ▸ Radio menus** (`ui/qmx_term_view.c`), and a hidden dev action `{"action":"term_view","open":true}` so the screen can be driven for screenshots. Font is **JetBrains Mono (OFL) at size 25** - unscii_16 is the only monospace LVGL ships and it looks awful; the size is a correctness constraint, not taste, because the reverse-video highlight is drawn at `col*CELL_W` and only a multiple of 5 gives a whole-pixel advance (25 -> 15.0 px -> a 1200x648 grid). **⑩ Four faults found and fixed by a test plan that drives the real radio (`scratchpad/term_test.ps1`)**: the opening CR is sometimes lost and nothing re-sent it (2 of 6 opens blank - now retried, 8/8); every session opened with **"Exit terminal" already selected**, a trap of our own making since our close leaves it there and the radio remembers; the UI called into the port from taskLVGL and froze the display for ~2.4 s (now a worker task + queue); and `cdc_acm_host_close()` abort-rebooted the device, fixed by **standing patch #6** `tools/patches/apply_cdc_acm_close_tolerant.ps1`. ⚠ Two of my own fixes were falsified on hardware first - "fixed by timing" (declared off ONE successful close, aborted on the fourth) and "never close at all" (traded it for a worse abort in IDF's hub layer on a QMX power cycle). **Still owed:** a manual chapter, and the CLAUDE.md entry for patch #6 |

| 134 | **CQ-run: the SECOND pileup caller was sent our GRID instead of a signal report** (Roy KI0ER) | ✅ **FIXED `276d431` + SIM-VERIFIED on hardware 2026-08-15.** Sim run: `VK3ABC OZ1LAV +09`, `W1AW OZ1LAV +08`, `N5XYZ OZ1LAV +08` — all three drained callers got a REPORT in WAIT_ROGER, each on our CQ tone (1650 Hz), then `resuming CQ @ 1650 Hz`. ⚠ Not yet on air (needs a real pileup); the reports are the sim's synthetic SNR, so the message SHAPE and state are proven, not the measurement's provenance | Small | Roy: after a CQ-run QSO completes and a pileup exists, the first TX to the next caller is `HIM ME GRID` where it must be `HIM ME -nn`. But these stations **called US**: they already have our grid from the CQ, so this must take the **CQ-run answer** path — `<them> <me> <report>` from their SNR, into WAIT_ROGER. `pile[best].snr_db` is already in the struct and already used for ranking, so the report is available at the call site. ⚠ Fires on the normal POTA activation path whenever auto-work-pileup is on, i.e. exactly what Don and Roy were both doing |

| 132 | **A config upload that matches nothing reports "Config applied: 0 item(s)" — which reads as success** (Brian WA6JFK) | ✅ **ALREADY FIXED — verified in code 2026-08-19** | Small | He could not get his edited file to work, concluded "my file must have had issues", and resorted to an **erase install** — the second time he has reached for the big hammer. Verified in code: `config_io_import()` returns a count, unknown keys are silently skipped by design (merge semantics), and `index.html` alerts `"Config applied: N item(s)"`. So a typo'd key, a wrong file, or a file mangled by an editor all report **0 applied** in the same wording as success. Fix is small: treat `applied === 0` as a failure message naming the likely cause ("nothing in that file was recognised — is it a qmx-config.txt?"), and consider echoing the first unrecognised key. ⚠ Worth remembering an erase install wipes WiFi credentials, callsign/grid, memories **and the ADIF log** — a user reaching for it to fix a config typo is a UX failure, not a user error  `index.html` has an explicit `if (!j.applied)` branch that says nothing was recognised, names the likely causes (wrong file, not plain text, edited key names) and states that nothing on the Tab5 was touched. Only a non-zero count gets the 'Config applied: N' message. |
| 133 | **Flashing failed repeatedly until the Tab5 was rebooted** (Samuel W7STF) | 🟡 **Docs half DONE `3aff8f2`** (all four places: README, quick-start, reference/troubleshooting, and the flasher's own README.txt). Cause still not reproduced or measured | Small (docs) + ? | Win11 Device Manager still showed the COM port, but esptool kept failing; a Tab5 reboot fixed it immediately. He suggests "reboot the Tab5" as the first troubleshooting step for a flash failure, which is cheap and clearly right — **do that in the docs regardless of cause**. He hypothesises memory depletion; I would not endorse that without measurement. **A better-supported hypothesis, from our own open finding:** the Tab5's console is USB-Serial/JTAG implemented BY the P4 itself, and #TBD records **core 0 sitting at 0-5% idle in panadapter mode** (vs ~14-35% historically) — a pegged core is a plausible reason the download-mode handshake is not serviced in time. Note `usb_shutdown_graceful()` ("prepare for flashing") already exists in the tree; check whether it is reachable by a user and whether it would help here. His unit had also been running **7 hours continuously** before the attempt, which is a useful stability datum in its own right |

Everything normal right up to it — FT8 capturing, audio 47k pairs/s, idle0 60.9% / idle1 73.3%. The Tab5 rebooted itself and ran clean for the 1.9 h after. **Two consequences.** (1) **The parking reason is dead: the antenna was OFF and it fired anyway**, so the antenna was never the variable. (2) **This is the comparison the note was waiting for** — the earlier BLE-**off** soak reached ~10 h clean, this BLE-**on** run (confirmed `scanning (filtered to 3 bonded device(s), passive, 20% duty)`) asserted at 10.9 h. Same duration, opposite result, which points at BLE scanning ([[project_sdio_recoveries_ble_scan]], #109). ⚠ **n=1 per arm and both land at 10–11 h, so a time-dependent cause is NOT excluded.** Next step is to repeat with BLE off, not to conclude. Last night's failure was evidently rarer than one-a-night, so confirming it needs either a much longer soak or a provoked write failure. **The RX degradation is unchanged and continues:** 401 drains in 10.9 h (**36.7/h**) before the run's `usb_dwc_hal` assert (#130), then 4 in 1.9 h (2.1/h) after the reboot — all self-healed. ⚠ I first split those counts by uptime, which resets at a reboot, so the two sets overlapped and summed past the total; split by log position instead. It is a controlled `esp_restart()`, so there is **no panic dump** and `reset_reason` is `SW_CPU_RESET` - which is exactly how a user would fail to recognise it as a fault. Our `usb_shutdown` handler did run correctly on the way down. **Run stats:** 27 RX drains in 50 min, bursty rather than steadily worsening (per-minute rate by quarter: 0.34 / **2.38** / 0.72 / 0.47), 2 RPC timeouts, then the fatal TX failure. Two drains 652 ms apart is the clustering CLAUDE.md said would indicate drift and warrant a prevention fix on top of the recovery. BLE was ENABLED but not connected, i.e. scanning ([[project_sdio_recoveries_ble_scan]] / #109). **Consequences:** (a) our patch #2 fixes the RX livelock only - the TX path has no equivalent and probably needs one; (b) a field user sees an unexplained reboot with a clean log and nothing to report; (c) this may well be what is behind some "it just restarted" reports, including possibly Michael KZ4LY's (#153). Prior finding: | `project_sdio_recoveries_ble_scan` records that the scan fix `dfa15e4` took SDIO recoveries **to zero**. The fair statement is that **BLE scanning was one contributor, not the only one**. The rate is flat-to-fluctuating, **not** the "accelerating and clustering" pattern this repo treats as the escalation criterion, so it is not urgent. **Discriminating test: turn the spot feeds off one at a time with BLE still off** — the same elimination that found the BLE contribution. Audio is healthy throughout (~47-49k pairs/s, `0 badpkt`). Asking what the antenna was doing - the standing rule from the RF-gain saga - was the whole answer: it was disconnected. **The diagnostics said so and should be trusted next time:** `score max` in the low teens with `n15=0 n20=0` means NO SIGNAL IS PRESENT, and it is worth far more than the decode count for telling a dead band from a dead decoder. ⚠ I also flagged `cap≈13.25s` + `zero≈15,300` as possibly "1.3 s short of a window" - **that was wrong and is retracted**: `zero` counts waterfall cells of zero MAGNITUDE (`v == 0` in `ft8_test.c`), not samples, so a silent input produces it by construction; and `off=+0ms` proves the window IS boundary-anchored, with the v0.20.0 pre-ring backfilling the arm delay, so a sub-15 s wall-clock `cap` is the healthy case, not a short window. Do not chase either number on this evidence |

| 116 | **DOCS: a Bluetooth mouse must be BLE 4.0 or later** | ✅ Written in BOTH trees: README mouse paragraph, `guide/settings.md` (warning box in the Bluetooth mouse section) and `reference/troubleshooting.md` ("A Bluetooth mouse never connects") | Small | State it plainly wherever the BT mouse is described, in **BOTH** doc trees (`README.md` and `docs/mkdocs/**` - correctness in one does not propagate to the other). The reason is hardware, not software: the Tab5's Bluetooth comes from the ESP32-C6, whose `soc_caps.h` has `SOC_BLE_SUPPORTED` and **no** `SOC_BT_CLASSIC_SUPPORTED`, and the P4 has no Bluetooth radio at all. So a Bluetooth **Classic** (BR/EDR) mouse can never work and no firmware change can help - this is Roy KI0ER's two Microsoft mice. Include the practical tell for users: a Classic mouse never appears at all (we scan for the BLE HID service UUID 0x1812), whereas a mouse that pairs but moves the pointer badly is a different, fixable problem. Dual-mode mice work on their LE channel (Roy's MX Master does) |

Still owed: a real DXpedition, and a look at whether the 2-slot ignore/answer cadence matches a real Fox's |

| 75 | **Zombie-device wedge: QMX power-off mid-stream kills USB until Tab5 reboot** | ✅ **Root-caused + AUTO-RECOVERY shipped (cc73722), replug cure hw-proven live** | — | Second distinct wedge separated from #74's fog: QMX powered off while streaming → UAC teardown fails (`Suspend Interface Failed` + `EP command ESP_ERR_INVALID_STATE`) → device object never freed → zombie occupies the single root port → re-powered QMX invisible, CAT reopen spins on `usbh_devs_open ESP_ERR_INVALID_STATE` ~20 lines/s forever. Likely a big share of the historical "restart the QMX many times" pain (no QMX restart can help — the Tab5 is the stuck side). **`usb_replug()` cures it** — proven live over the wedged unit's own web API: port power cycle → "USB: All devices freed" → clean enumeration in ~1 s, no reboot. Detector task now separates the wedges by `num_devices` and acts: >0 with nothing opened 30 s = zombie → auto-replug; enum-failure tally = #74's QMX-side wedge → toast. Verification pending: the scripted zombie repro (QMX off-while-streaming → on → self-heal ≤45 s) |
| 74 | **QMX never re-enumerates after a Tab5 warm reboot** | 🟠 **ROOT-CAUSED (QMX-side); detector shipped, no cure possible host-side** | — | Wedge signature captured on hw with the QMX ON: the QMX answers every fresh enumeration with **8 of 16 device-descriptor bytes** (`ENUM CHECK_SHORT_DEV_DESC FAILED`), host frees the device, no retry, bus then looks empty. Persists through bus resets (in every enum attempt), DWC root-port power cycles, and USB5V_EN VBUS cuts of 2 s + 8 s (pullup stays up) → QMX-firmware-side; only a QMX power cycle clears it. Intermittent — 2026-08-02 warm reboots reconnected fine twice. **Shipped (ff71393):** stale-QMX detector — diag_log hook tallies the ENUM-failure lines (only reliable signal), toast "QMX USB is stuck - power-cycle the QMX to reconnect" every 5 min while unconnected (baseline logic avoids nagging after normal unplugs); hw-verified (failure 4.5 s → toast 19 s). Auto-replug REMOVED (can't cure, risks inducing); `usb_replug()` kept as hidden `/api/cmd` tool with corrected VBUS-first ordering. **Same-night confirmation (operator at bench):** reproduced on **QMX fw 1_04_004** (latest; no USB changes in any 1_04 changelog) AND survives **multiple physical cable replugs** (each = fresh attach + same 8/16 answer) — only a QMX power cycle (firmware restart) clears it. Detector hardened to 2 consecutive checks (545fc0b) after a false positive during a healthy power-up. **Remaining action: report the signature to Hans G0UPL** (draft in session notes / memory). See memory `project_qmx_reenumerate_after_reboot`  **⚠ RE-TESTED 2026-08-11 — SIX approaches falsified, recovery AND prevention; the QMX-side diagnosis now stands on evidence rather than assumption.** The operator refused the original conclusion (Stan and others unplug/replug freely on Windows/Linux/macOS), so it was re-tested against a really-wedged radio. Identical empty data stage every time (`Unexpected (8) device response length` = setup packet + ZERO descriptor bytes): reset hold 30/50/200 ms (200 = Linux HUB_LONG_RESET_TIME) with recovery to 100 ms; `ENUM_SHORT_DESC_REQ_LEN` 8 -> 64 (what Windows/Linux request); VBUS off 2 s + root-port cycle, twice; repeated retries (already happening - the zombie branch DOES fire, num_devices is non-zero after a failed enum); **orderly `usb_shutdown` before flashing** (never previously run, because esptool resets the chip without invoking the handler - its own comment says so); and **orderly shutdown + PHYSICAL VBUS removal held down through the reflash and the whole boot** (that function never cut 5 V - only usb_replug.c does). All sdkconfig knobs are back at IDF defaults and `sdkconfig.defaults` records the negative so nobody raises them hoping again; the VBUS cut is reverted (no gain, and it would leave USB dead until a reboot if the button were pressed and not followed by a flash). ESP-IDF's missing enum retry is real (issue **#17918**, open, same class on a HID joystick and USB sticks) and worth knowing but is NOT the cause. **Report to Hans drafted at `docs/qmx-usb-wedge-report-for-hans.md`** - it corrects the August "8 of 16 bytes" phrasing, which counted the host's own setup packet in both figures and would have sent him looking for a truncated descriptor instead of no descriptor at all. ⚠ Two claims of mine were wrong in opposite directions that evening; both corrected in CLAUDE.md, the memory and the code comments. Lesson: a persuasive argument from the operator is a reason to TEST, not a reason to conclude |

| 73 | **Dennis WN4FLA's crash (radio-on / band-change in FT8)** | ✅ **FIXED (4b2a273), bench-repro'd + torture-verified** | — | Reproduced FIRST TRY on the bench with the serial monitor (his exact recipe: FT8 waiting for radio, radio on): `assert xQueueGenericSend queue.c:936` in `ft8_decode_worker_task` — the FT8 worker's queue/semaphores were shared statics, and ft8_task's 10 s teardown wait was SHORTER than the decode task's own 12 s worker join, so a new session could recreate the statics and the old teardown deleted the new instance's handles under its live worker. Fix: per-instance heap `worker_ctx_t` (worker gets it as task arg, decode_slot as param), leak-on-join-timeout instead of free-under-live-worker, teardown wait 10→15 s, spawn refused while the previous decode task exists. Operator torture-tested many combinations: no crash. His earlier band-change crash is the same window (mode churn at connect). Reply to Dennis pending: "reproduced your exact steps, fixed, in the next release" |
| 67 | **Settings-drawer sliders/checkboxes intermittent** | ✅ **Fixed, local/unpushed** | — | Don WB0LQW. NOT a hit-target problem (checkboxes already carry `ext_click_area 28` = ~86 px). LVGL's `find_scroll_obj()` (`lv_indev_scroll.c:312`) walks up from the pressed object and only stops if something on the way lacks `LV_OBJ_FLAG_SCROLL_CHAIN_VER`; the drawer IS vertically scrollable, so once travel passes `indev->scroll_limit` (**10 px**, LVGL default, never overridden here) the drawer takes the gesture and the control gets `PRESS_LOST`. 10 px is nothing with a fingertip. Fix: clear `SCROLL_CHAIN_VER` on drawer sliders + checkboxes, and give sliders `ext_click_area 12` (lv_slider's HIT_TEST inflates `right_knob_area` by `ext_click_pad`, so the ADV_HITTEST knob-only grab widens 42->66 px without re-arming the track). **Cost, promised publicly:** a drag starting exactly on a knob/checkbox no longer scrolls the drawer. **HW-VERIFIED 2026-07-30** in two parts, which is why they are worth separating: the operator confirmed "touching a control (when you hit it right) stops the scrolling as it should" - so the scroll-steal half was right - but still asked for bigger touch areas, i.e. HITTING it was the remaining problem, not keeping it. `ext_click_area` 12 -> **28** (42 -> 98 px catch, sized against the closest slider spacing of 72 px minus the 14 px track = 29 px/side before neighbours fight; same value the checkboxes use). Operator: "much better now". **Known remaining limit:** with ADV_HITTEST set you must still be near the knob HORIZONTALLY - a tap at the far end of the 488 px track does nothing. Clearing ADV_HITTEST would make the whole row grab and jump the knob to the tap, at the cost of a stray tap being a large value change and of losing drawer scroll from a slider row. One line if wanted. Candidate follow-up: the drawer DROPDOWNS have the same scroll-steal exposure, untouched |

| **🆕 2026-07-26 session — PSK Reporter de-risking (LOCAL/UNPUSHED)** | | | | |
| 65 | **The real constraint is the MALLOC_CAP_DMA pool, not the internal heap** | 🟠 **Root cause identified, NOT fixed** | Needs design call | Four boots, serial-captured, SD-enabled vs `SD_ARCHIVE_DISABLED` A/B. **⚠️ Two earlier conclusions in this file were WRONG and are retracted:** (i) "dies 226 ms after WiFi gets an IP" — coincidence from n=1; deaths actually observed at 13.6 s / 58.7 s / 140 s, plus one boot that never mounted, with the IP always at 12.4–13.4 s → **no timing correlation with WiFi**; (ii) "card definitively exonerated" — too strong: one boot failed mount at **4.168 s with err=0x108 `ESP_ERR_INVALID_RESPONSE` while DMA memory was abundant** (112 KB free / 77 KB largest), i.e. a genuine card/bus-level init failure. The card is **intermittent at the protocol level** — some boots it mounts at 4.09 s and mirrors all five files, others it fails init outright. **What IS solid:** at every SD failure the *general* internal heap is healthy (35–40 KB free, **31 KB largest block**) — so every "internal heap starvation" framing of this bug class measures the wrong pool. The **`MALLOC_CAP_DMA` pool** is what collapses: 112 KB free / 77 KB lblk at 4.2 s → **703 B at 14 s → flat ~379–399 B from 44 s onward, forever**. WiFi bring-up consumes ~111 KB of it. So the `0x101 ESP_ERR_NO_MEM` remount failures are **secondary and masking** — they explain why recovery is impossible, not why the card died. **Wider consequence:** with ~400 B of DMA-capable memory after boot, *any* runtime DMA allocation should be expected to fail — consistent with the documented USB endpoint-alloc failure on a QMX reconnect once WiFi is up, suggesting one shared root constraint rather than two bugs. Note both standing esp_hosted patches (PSRAM transport, SDIO recovery) **are** applied, so they are not the consumer. **Options:** (a) find and relocate whatever takes the ~111 KB of DMA-capable RAM during WiFi bring-up — highest leverage, fixes SD *and* USB-reconnect; (b) reserve a DMA-capable buffer for the SD path at boot so remount can succeed; (c) investigate the intermittent `0x108` card init (SPI clock / pull-ups / marginal card); (d) bound the futile retry loop. **Not touched pending a decision** |
| 66 | Measured internal-RAM cost of an inserted SD card | ✅ **Measured** (supersedes the "correlation only" note) | — | Same A/B as #65. **SD disabled:** steady internal free ~44 KB, min watermark bottoms at **7 KB** by 44 s then flat for 3 min, **0** allocation failures. **SD enabled:** steady free ~37 KB, min watermark **1 KB**, **20** `ESP_ERR_NO_MEM`. The mount self-reports `internal -14228 B`. So an inserted card costs ~7 KB of steady internal free and takes the low-water mark from 7 KB to 1 KB. No unbounded leak in either arm (free is flat). This makes #59's lone `SD SPI init ESP_ERR_NO_MEM` **not incidental but the reproducible steady state** of this device with a card in — a concrete candidate mechanism for that wedge |
| 62 | PSK Reporter datagram violated RFC 7011 padding rule | ✅ **Fixed + bench-verified** (3e06360) | — | The receiver Data Set padded to 4 bytes unconditionally, but its shortest possible record is only 3 octets (3 var-length fields), so a 3-byte pad was byte-identical to a record of three empty strings. RFC 7011 §3.3.1 requires padding to be SHORTER than any record; a collector may ingest a phantom empty receiver record or drop the message, and PSK Reporter ACKs nothing so either is invisible. Fired iff `len(call)+len(grid)+len(sw) ≡ 2 (mod 4)` → **~5 of 18 realistic call/grid combinations**, i.e. some stations broken and others fine. `OZ1LAV`/`JO65` on a release version string pads to 2 and was accidentally OK; the dev build's longer version string tripped it. Fix: `pad_data_set()` pads only while the pad stays shorter than the template minimum (alignment is a SHOULD, the MUST wins) |
| 63 | PSK Reporter datagram harness + bench hook | ✅ **Done** (3e06360) | — | `test/psk_harness.c` — strict IPFIX parser written independently from RFC 7011 (not from the builder, so it can disagree with it): accepts a hand-built conformant datagram, rejects **nine** targeted corruptions incl. a permanent regression vector for #62, and validates/dumps a real captured datagram. `pskreporter.c` gains compiled-out `PSK_BENCH_SELFTEST` which hex-dumps a real datagram to the log **without transmitting**, so the whole path is verifiable with no antenna, no QMX and no live band. Verified against real device output: 217 B, 1 receiver + 2 sender records, all field values correct. **This hook is what found #62** |
| 64 | PSK Reporter: unresolved/truncated callsigns were publishable | ✅ **Fixed + hw-verified** (c62eca5) | — | An unresolved hashed callsign reaches the spot hook as literal `"..."` (ft8_lib renders an unresolvable hash as `<...>`; the extractor strips the brackets). Harmless in the station table, but it would have been published as a reception report to a public database. Guard requires ≥1 letter + ≥1 digit and rejects anything but alphanumerics and `/` (compound calls still spot); over-long calls are dropped rather than `strncpy`-truncated into a DIFFERENT station's call. Hardware-verified: of 4 injected spots, `"..."` and `"OZ"` dropped, 2 valid accepted (`spots_accepted=2`) |
| — | PSK Reporter reaching the collector | ✅ **FIELD-VERIFIED 2026-07-27** | — | pskreporter.info's "Software in use" table lists **`QMX Panadapter v1.3.2`, 6 reporters** (BD4AHS, KI0ER, VE3OFA, W5JSS, W5NR, W7STF) — the collector accepts our field combination, template and the #62 padding fix end-to-end. FT4 is reported too (`ft8_test.c:921` sends `"FT4"` when the pool is FT4). **Two debugging notes kept:** the first datagram goes out 5–5.5 min after boot, so a short trial looks like failure; and PSK never ACKs, so this page is the ONLY signal — W7STF reported "not working" based on wspr.rocks not showing his spots while he was in fact in the reporter list |

| 51 | FT8 slot-0 decodes hugely exceed steady-state, then collapse (61 vs ~15) — thorough analysis | ✅ **SOLVED (2026-07-19, hw-verified)** | — | **ROOT CAUSE: the USB isochronous pipeline held only 9 ms of queued transfers** (Kconfig defaults `UAC_NUM_ISOC_URBS=3` × `UAC_NUM_PACKETS_PER_URB=3` × 1 ms). Every post-decode storm (dual-core LDPC + LVGL decode-list rebuild) paused the completion/resubmission pump >9 ms → the IN endpoint ran dry → **~170–350 ms of QMX audio lost AT THE WIRE every slot** — zero error status, invisible to every software counter (drop=0, backlog=0 all along). The hole destroyed each signal's opening Costas sync array → same-station sync scores dropped 5–13 pts with SNR unchanged (the fingerprint that cracked it) → weak decodes died, strong ones lost their duplicate margin. Slots 0/1 were pristine only because no decode storm had happened yet; bigger yields caused bigger storms → the oscillation and mid-session spikes. **Fix: 8 URBs × 40 packets = 320 ms of queued ISO stream** (sdkconfig+defaults), plus hardening found en route: UAC driver ringbuf 19,200 B (66 ms) → 288,000 B (1 s, PSRAM), audio_task pri 3→6, the fork's silent ringbuf-overflow drop now WARNs with a byte counter, per-second `RX xport` ISO stats, and `FT8_LDPC_MAX_ITERS` 15→30 (the 15-cut's reason was obsoleted by the hold-for-decode gate). **Verified: capture windows tile at 180,027±152 samples (was ~175,656, i.e. −360 ms/slot); unique decodes 15.8/slot sustained over 22 straight slots (was 6.3 steady / 14.5 slot-0 peak) — every slot now decodes at slot-0 level, WSJT-X-style.** Instrumentation kept in-tree (arm-start tiling log, per-slot slotdiag, per-decode dt=, xport stats) — cheap and they make any regression instantly visible. NOT the cause (all data-exonerated): band fading, ring backlog, producer drops, window anchoring, monitor-pool reuse, `last_frame` staleness, LVGL-vs-audio_task scheduling alone, candidate-cap (already a 2026-06-27 clean negative) |

| 26 | FT4 reliability — four stacked faults, each masking the next (bench + Samuel W7STF) | ✅ Shipped (v0.19.4) — but FT4 now soft-disabled, see #5 | — | **NOTE: FT4 as a whole is soft-disabled as of 2026-07-09 (internal-heap exhaustion → crashes, see #5).** These four fixes still apply if/when FT4 is re-enabled. (1) shared FFT scratch — the 2nd capture-monitor's kiss-FFT buffer spilled to PSRAM under internal-SRAM starvation → ~10× slower STFT → every slot on it decoded 0 (FT8's 15s slot hid it, FT4's 7.5s didn't); all monitors now share one internal-SRAM FFT config. (2) stuck-decoder watchdog gated off during TX/QSO + threshold 2→8 (was wiping the list mid-QSO). (3) auto-sync per-slot clamp `FT8_AUTOSYNC_MAX_STEP_MS=20` (FT4's noisy offsets were causing ~250ms clock jumps/slot). (4) slot-parity display used FT8's /15 grid → wrong E/O in FT4. The zero-decode audio-reset watchdog (`ft8_test.c`) hardcoded its toast/log as "FT8" and used a fixed 8-slot threshold = only ~60s in FT4 (7.5s slots) vs ~120s in FT8. Because FT4 is far less populated than FT8, a blank decode list is routine there, so a merely quiet band tripped a spurious reset. Now protocol-labelled + wall-clock based (`FT8_STUCK_RESET_SLOTS`→`FT8_STUCK_RESET_MS` 120000 / `ft8_op_mode_slot_ms()` = 8 FT8 / 16 FT4). Clean-boot verified; behavioural fix (no toast on a quiet FT4 band) awaits real-air confirmation |

| 27a | Spectrum/waterfall signal shifted, tunable across whole 48kHz window, audio gone until retuned (Dirk DK7CVD, groups.io #172933, Jun 30) | ✅ Fixed (2026-06-30) | — | **Root cause found from Dirk's SD diag log**: `cat: QMX IQ mode NOT confirmed (raw='(no response)')` at link-up. `cat.c`'s `Q9 1;`/`Q9;` IQ-mode handshake was single-shot — on failure it only logged a warning and the session proceeded with IQ mode silently off. Without IQ mode the QMX streams plain (non-IQ) audio: signal appears shifted, tunable across the entire 48 kHz window with the VFO knob, audio absent until retuned back into range, fixed by power-cycling the QMX (forces a fresh handshake on reconnect) — exactly Dirk's symptom, and exactly what our own (log-only, never seen live) warning text predicted. This was never a spectrum/waterfall rendering desync — both panes correctly render whatever audio the QMX is actually sending. Fix: handshake now retries up to 4 attempts (300ms apart) before giving up (`cat_get_iq_mode_confirmed()`); confirmed live on hardware same evening — attempt 1 reproduced Dirk's exact `(no response)` failure, attempt 2 confirmed ON. Also added a persistent red top-screen banner (`ui_set_iq_mode_warning()`) so if all 4 attempts ever fail, the user sees it immediately instead of being silently degraded for an entire session with no visible sign anything is wrong. |
| 27b | Tab5 crashed mid-session, 4× `reset_reason=panic/exception` in one log capture (Dirk DK7CVD, email + groups.io #172933, Jun 30) | 🔴 New, root cause unknown — no backtrace captured | Needs serial capture | Dirk's SD-persisted diag log shows 4 panic/exception resets in a single session — a real crash, not a button reset (no repeated power-cycle explanation fits). 3 of 4 land within ~1s of a pan/stroll/mode-toggle gesture in Panadapter view while `ft8_test`'s background capture was concurrently active on the same band — matches Dirk's own repro note ("only happens if the spectrum view band is the same as the FT8 band and both are tuned close to each other"). The diag log can't show the actual fault: panics print straight to UART and bypass the `esp_log_set_vprintf` hook the SD/flash/RAM ring relies on (see CLAUDE.md's diagnostic-logging section) — confirmed again here, all 4 capture windows cut off cleanly at `reset_reason=...` with zero backtrace lines before it. **Next step: ask Dirk to run `tools/capture_serial_log.ps1` (works over USB, no WiFi needed) next time it happens** — that's the only way to get a real backtrace and root-cause this. Don't guess a fix from the gesture/FT8-concurrency correlation alone; it's suggestive, not proof. |
