| 38 | Panadapter↔FT8/FT4 toggle leaks ~163KB PSRAM + several KB internal RAM per swipe (found 2026-07-07, live serial-log investigation) | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-28 - fixed by #279 and measured flat on the FT8 toggle path itself** | Medium–Large | Root cause confirmed byte-exact via live heap instrumentation: every FT8 entry spawns 3 fresh FreeRTOS tasks (`ft8_task` 64KB PSRAM stack, `ft8_decode_task` 64KB, `ft8_decode_worker_task` 32KB = 163840B) which all **self-delete** via `vTaskDelete(NULL)` on exit — FreeRTOS defers the actual stack/TCB free to the idle task (`prvCheckTasksWaitingTermination`), and CLAUDE.md already documents idle-task starvation is bad enough on both cores that its watchdog check is disabled, so the deferred free apparently never runs. Repeated toggling ratchets internal RAM down to ~1KB free (a real `ESP_ERR_NO_MEM` allocation failure was observed) and burns PSRAM at ~160KB/toggle (not fatal short-term — 20MB pool — but never recovers). **Fix attempt #1 FAILED**: `vTaskSuspend(NULL)` + external `vTaskDelete(handle)` from the joining parent — proven via instrumentation to free **zero bytes** every time (external delete defers to idle exactly like self-delete on this port), and likely froze the UI/touch thread entirely. **Fix attempt #2 FAILED**: full persistent-task redesign (spawn all 3 exactly once, loop forever via `ui_mode_get()` polling instead of destroy/recreate, reusing the already-proven `s_buf_busy[]` drain-wait pattern before freeing the pool) — clean build, but **crashed on hardware within 2 toggle cycles**: `Guru Meditation Error: Core 0 panic'ed (Load access fault)` at `MTVAL=0x6A`, traced via addr2line to `estimate_snr_db`/`ftx_decode_candidate` inside the core-0 decode worker — a stale-pointer read whose exact mechanism was NOT found despite careful tracing of the (unchanged) `decode_slot()`/worker-join contract. Both attempts fully reverted (`git checkout -- main/ft8_test.c`), device back on known-stable c04bd2d. **Do not attempt a 3rd live-only fix** — recommend building host-side/simulated verification of the 3-task pipeline's synchronization before ever flashing again, and/or considering making the capture pool (`s_mon_pool`/`s_cap_scratch`) ALSO persistent (never freed) to eliminate the pause/resume boundary entirely, where both crashes clustered. Full writeup + exact numbers + both crash signatures: memory `project_ft8_task_stack_leak_investigation`. **2026-08-20: a re-measurement was ATTEMPTED and is INVALID — recording it so it is not mistaken for good news.** 12 web-driven panadapter↔FT8 toggles showed `psram free` **unchanged at 17609 KB** and `int free` 56→58 KB, i.e. zero leak against a predicted ~2 MB. But the toggles did nothing at all: `screen` still reported `panadapter` after requesting `ft8`, and the capture held **0** `waiting for CAT`, **0** `respawning` and **0** guard refusals, so no task was ever spawned or destroyed. ⛔ **CORRECTION, same night: the reason given here was WRONG.** The toggles did nothing because the action name was wrong — it is **`set_screen`**, not `screen` — and `/api/cmd` answers an unknown action with `unknown action` and HTTP 200, so with the response piped to /dev/null it looked like a successful no-op. I had explained the flat heap by asserting `ui_request_base_mode()` was gated behind `qmx_wait_poll_cb()`'s QMX-wait prompt; **that was invented, never tested, and is false** — with the correct action the switch completes normally. So this item is still **unmeasured**, and the leak is neither confirmed nor refuted. Re-run it with `{"action":"set_screen","screen":"ft8"}` and confirm `waiting for CAT` appears per entry before trusting any number ⭐ **MEASURED CLOSED, 2026-08-28.** #279's reaper fixed this without anyone connecting the two: #38 names `ft8_task` (64 KB) + `ft8_decode_task` (64 KB) + `ft8_decode_worker_task` (32 KB) = 163,840 B self-deleting via `vTaskDelete(NULL)`, and #279 names **the same three tasks and the same arithmetic** (plus the 38 KB shared FFT scratch, hence its 218 KB). #279 was only ever verified over Panadapter/WSPR trips, so this row stayed open as a Medium-Large confirmed bug against a fix that had already landed. **Re-measured on #38's own path: 14 Panadapter<->FT8 round trips (8 + 6), PSRAM settled at exactly 16810 KB on every one - delta ZERO**, against a predicted ~163 KB/swipe (~2.3 MB over 14). Internal free sat at 40 KB and wobbled 37-40 NON-monotonically, i.e. noise, not the ratchet-to-1-KB-and-ESP_ERR_NO_MEM this row described. Corroborated by the mechanism, not just the total: the reap log prints `+64 KB`, `+64 KB`, `+32 KB` recoveries - exactly the three stacks - and this boot had 0 not-reapable warnings and 0 `rebuilt with no teardown`. ⛔ **Method notes, because this row already contains one invalid re-measurement.** Every switch was verified TWICE (the `/api/cmd` response BODY *and* `/api/status.screen` actually changing) - the 2026-08-20 attempt used the action `screen` instead of **`set_screen`**, got `unknown action` with HTTP 200, and read the resulting flat heap as good news. And PSRAM must be sampled **after the reaper has run**: a sample ~13 s after returning to the panadapter catches a consistent intermediate 15584 KB, which is a valid like-for-like comparison but is NOT the settled figure; one trip in the second run read 15584 for exactly that reason and was at 16810 KB thirty seconds later. |

| **v1.0 Release Gates — ALL MET (LoTW #1 live-verified 2026-07-14; #2 accepted 2026-07-07). Remaining: release mechanics only** | | | | |
| 1 | LoTW upload (TQSL) | ✅ DONE — live-verified 2026-07-14 (last v1.0 gate cleared) | — | On-device TQ8 signing + upload. TQ8 format reverse-verified against tqsllib source (SIGN_LOTW_V2.0, RSA-SHA1 PKCS#1v1.5, base64-wrapped sig fields); host harness `test/lotw_harness.c` verifies construction + signatures via openssl. Web UI: guided 2-page "LoTW setup" (TQSL export instructions + link to ARRL's save-certificate page → import), browser-side .p12 parse via embedded forge.js (passphrase never reaches the device), cert/key on SPIFFS + in config backup, cursor rewinds on cert import. **Live-verified end-to-end 2026-07-14** by the operator (OZ1LAV): real TQSL .p12 imported in-browser, 22 QSOs signed + uploaded to lotw.arrl.org, accepted. Fix along the way: LoTW setup wedged the httpd (fragile esp_hosted link — the ~10fps spectrum WS saturates the uplink, the 73KB forge fetch on top stalled the single-threaded httpd hard); fixed by pausing the WS client-side during setup/upload (9a6bd08) + resilient fetch-based forge loader |
| 2 | FT8 TX multi-day soak | ✅ Accepted as satisfied (2026-07-07, user call) | — | **Closed 2026-07-07 — no formal multi-day soak will be run; treated as satisfied by accumulated real-world + bench use (multiple full auto-pounce QSOs verified end-to-end).** Original caveats remain technically true but are accepted. No duty-cycle protection, no audio loopback verification, no over-temperature monitoring yet. All RX/panadapter/web/logging features already stable. Bench session 2026-06-29 verified multiple full auto-pounce QSOs completing correctly end-to-end (CQ→TX1→RPT→RR73→73→ADIF log) post-v0.19.2, but this is still single-session evidence, not the multi-day soak this gate requires. Blocks dropping the "beta" label |
| 22 | QMX `1_04` firmware compatibility + new features it enables | ✅ Closed (2026-07-07, user call) — AM+Tune shipped & field-verified; broader 1_04 surface won't be pursued | — | **Closed 2026-07-07 — the AM-mode + Antenna Tune deliverable is done and field-verified on real 1_04_002 hardware; the remaining 1_04 opportunities (`AI2` event-driven push, MU-based SSB-filter-dance simplification, `PS0;` power-off, further AM surfacing) are deprioritized as not worth it for now. Reference comparison doc kept for if this ever reopens.** **Update (v0.19.5):** AM mode (touch popup + web dropdown) and Antenna Tune (#3) both implemented and **field-verified on a real QMX+ running 1_04_002**, gated behind `cat_qmx_fw_at_least(1,4,0)` so 1_03_002 users see zero change. Settled on hardware: exit Tune by restoring the prior mode digit; AM has one fixed RX filter (no selectable BW). Still open below. Complete 1_03_002↔1_04_002 comparison now in **`docs/qmx-1_04-cat-comparison.md`** (built from the actual 1_04_001 CAT manual, cached in `docs/qmx-reference/` — 1_04_002 added no CAT changes): command-by-command diff (new: `AI`/`KD`/`MU`/`PS`/`TR`/`RR`; changed: `MD` gained 5=AM + 8=SWR-Tune, `PC` 3-digit ≥10 W already handled by us, new "MM Effect" Immediate/On-demand semantics; our entire FT8-TX/poll CAT surface textually unchanged), our-code impact map, ranked opportunities (one-button TUNE #3, `AI2` event-driven push replacing the 50 ms poll, MU-based SSB-filter-dance simplification, `PS0;` power-off button, AM surfacing), and a 7-step hardware verification checklist. Corrections vs earlier notes: our mode table ALREADY handles 5=AM — only the 8→"TUNE" label is missing (cosmetic, safe-now fix listed in the doc). Still true: `Q9 1;` silent-fail field report on 1_04 unexplained (our retry+banner mitigates); never test `AI` on 1_03_002; `1_04_002` install forces a Factory Reset. Next step unchanged: real `1_04` unit, then the checklist top-to-bottom. Blocks #3 |
| **Shelved Work** | | | | |
| 6 | CW Audio (speaker/headphone output only) | ✋ Shelved (v0.18.5/.6) | Unblock needed | Disabled to restore FT8 decode performance. Root cause: `cw_audio_preopen()`+`dsp_cw_forward()` degraded yield 2–3× even with audio off (v0.18.5); `cw_audio_init()` also spawned a priority-6 ghost task on core 1 preempting `fft_task` ~125×/slot, found+disabled in v0.18.6, confirmed via controlled A/B to fully restore v0.18.0-level yield. Fix needed before re-enabling: root-cause the original I2S/DMA/UAC contention AND fix task priority/cadence, then soak-test a full session. Blocks #7 only — does NOT block CW Phase 2 decode (#4b) |

| 31 | FT8 weak-signal rescue — Stage 2 residual subtraction (offline research, **NOT released**) | ⏸️ Research done, modest gain proven; next lever identified | Large (next phase) | **Offline test-harness only** (`test/ft8_stage2.c` + `ft8_test_harness.c`), on a **local-only branch — NOT on GitHub** (2026-07-07). Rewrote the residual decoder to operate on the real `uint8_t` waterfall (notch each decoded signal's tone cells toward the local noise floor). **Correction:** the prior session's "700 candidates / mask removal proven" was a `uint8`↔`float` **heap-corruption artifact** (140 = MAX_CANDIDATES ceiling from a corrupted waterfall), not real unmasking. Real result: rescues +1 on websdr_test1 (13/18→14/18, `CQ DX Z33Z KN11`), +1–2 ground-truth-verified decodes on 4/8 test files; a `scale=0.00` control proves the gain is the subtraction (not a wider heap); optimal scale 0.80–0.95 (1.00 over-notches). **Ceiling of magnitude-domain notching:** cannot recover deeply-weak (<~−6 dB) co-located signals — 4/5 websdr_test1 targets stay unrescued. **Next lever = coherent time-domain subtraction** (re-synth each decoded signal's complex waveform — GFSK synth already exists in `ft8_stage2.c` — phase-align, subtract from audio, re-STFT; the WSJT-X approach). Findings: `docs/ft8-stage2-findings.md`; memory `stage2_complete_checkpoint`. Local commit `2dcfcee` (unpushed) |
